### 21. Forgive, rebuke, and restore the repentant

**Corpus:** Gospels  
**Scope:** Universal  
**Earlier-list crosswalk:** #30, #31, #32  
**Canonical passages:** Matthew 6:14-15; 18:21-35; Mark 11:25; Luke 17:3-4

#### Command-family wording

> Release personal vengeance, confront sin truthfully, and forgive the repentant without keeping score.

#### Scripture text (World English Bible)

##### Matthew 6:14-15

> **14** “For if you forgive men their trespasses, your heavenly Father will also forgive you.
>
> **15** But if you don’t forgive men their trespasses, neither will your Father forgive your trespasses.

##### Matthew 18:21-35

> **21** Then Peter came and said to him, “Lord, how often shall my brother sin against me, and I forgive him? Until seven times?”
>
> **22** Jesus said to him, “I don’t tell you until seven times, but, until seventy times seven.
>
> **23** Therefore the Kingdom of Heaven is like a certain king who wanted to settle accounts with his servants.
>
> **24** When he had begun to settle, one was brought to him who owed him ten thousand talents.
>
> **25** But because he couldn’t pay, his lord commanded him to be sold, with his wife, his children, and all that he had, and payment to be made.
>
> **26** The servant therefore fell down and knelt before him, saying, ‘Lord, have patience with me, and I will repay you all!’
>
> **27** The lord of that servant, being moved with compassion, released him and forgave him the debt.
>
> **28** “But that servant went out and found one of his fellow servants who owed him one hundred denarii, and he grabbed him and took him by the throat, saying, ‘Pay me what you owe!’
>
> **29** “So his fellow servant fell down at his feet and begged him, saying, ‘Have patience with me, and I will repay you!’
>
> **30** He would not, but went and cast him into prison until he should pay back that which was due.
>
> **31** So when his fellow servants saw what was done, they were exceedingly sorry, and came and told their lord all that was done.
>
> **32** Then his lord called him in and said to him, ‘You wicked servant! I forgave you all that debt because you begged me.
>
> **33** Shouldn’t you also have had mercy on your fellow servant, even as I had mercy on you?’
>
> **34** His lord was angry, and delivered him to the tormentors until he should pay all that was due to him.
>
> **35** So my heavenly Father will also do to you, if you don’t each forgive your brother from your hearts for his misdeeds.”

##### Mark 11:25

> **25** Whenever you stand praying, forgive, if you have anything against anyone; so that your Father, who is in heaven, may also forgive you your transgressions.

##### Luke 17:3-4

> **3** Be careful. If your brother sins against you, rebuke him. If he repents, forgive him.
>
> **4** If he sins against you seven times in the day, and seven times returns, saying, ‘I repent,’ you shall forgive him.”

#### Reading the passages together

- Horizontal forgiveness rests in God's immeasurable mercy; rebuke serves repentance, while accountability and the ordered use of the Keys remain.

#### Law, Gospel, and confessional synthesis

Horizontal forgiveness rests in God's immeasurable mercy; rebuke serves repentance, while accountability and the ordered use of the Keys remain. In a confessional Lutheran reading, this command is not a ladder by which sinners earn justification. The Law exposes sin and directs the baptized life; the Gospel forgives, creates faith, joins the believer to Christ, and brings forth the fruits named here. The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

#### Early Christian witnesses

**Polycarp, Epistle to the Philippians 2 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Remembering what the Lord said in His teaching: Judge not, that you be not judged; forgive, and it shall be forgiven unto you; be merciful, that you may obtain mercy; with what measure you measure, it shall be measured to you again.

*Study significance:* Polycarp preserves several sayings of Jesus as a unified pattern for congregational life.

**Didache 4.14 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> In church you shall acknowledge your transgressions, and you shall not come near for your prayer with an evil conscience. This is the way of life.

*Study significance:* Repentance is concrete, ecclesial, and joined to prayer rather than reduced to vague regret.

#### Lutheran confessional witnesses

**Augsburg Confession XII**

> Properly speaking, repentance consists of these two parts: one is contrition, that is, terrors smiting the conscience through the knowledge of sin; the other is faith, born of the Gospel or of absolution, which believes that for Christ's sake sins are forgiven, comforts the conscience, and delivers it from terrors.

*Study significance:* Repentance includes both truthful sorrow over sin and faith in Christ's forgiveness.

**Small Catechism, Fifth Commandment**

> We should fear and love God that we may not hurt nor harm our neighbor in his body, but help and befriend him in every bodily need.

*Study significance:* Neighbor-love includes active bodily help, not merely refraining from violence.

**Further confessional anchors:** Small Catechism: Fourth through Tenth Commandments; Augsburg Confession VI (new obedience); Augsburg Confession XXVIII (the Church does not invent works that bind consciences); Formula of Concord, Solid Declaration IV (good works as necessary fruits, not merits).

#### Practicing the command

Pray for willingness to forgive, name serious sin, offer absolving words to the repentant, and involve proper authority.

#### Misuse caution

Do not force reconciliation without repentance, safety, or trustworthy change.

#### Questions for examination and meditation

1. What exactly does Jesus command, forbid, promise, or warn in these passages, and who first receives the words?
2. Which words expose sin or false trust, and which words direct you to Christ's gift, forgiveness, or sustaining promise?
3. What concrete act of repentance, faith, mercy, or vocation would embody **forgive, rebuke, and restore the repentant** this week without turning obedience into a basis of justification?

#### Prayer

Lord Jesus Christ, You call Your people to forgive, rebuke, and restore the repentant. Forgive my failure, keep me in Your Word and gifts, and bear this fruit in me for the good of my neighbor and the glory of the Father. Amen.

### 24. Address a sinning brother through ordered correction

**Corpus:** Gospels  
**Scope:** Churchly process  
**Earlier-list crosswalk:** #35  
**Canonical passages:** Matthew 18:15-20

#### Command-family wording

> Go first in appropriate privacy, then seek witnesses and the Church, aiming to regain the sinner.

#### Scripture text (World English Bible)

##### Matthew 18:15-20

> **15** “If your brother sins against you, go, show him his fault between you and him alone. If he listens to you, you have gained back your brother.
>
> **16** But if he doesn’t listen, take one or two more with you, that at the mouth of two or three witnesses every word may be established.
>
> **17** If he refuses to listen to them, tell it to the assembly. If he refuses to hear the assembly also, let him be to you as a Gentile or a tax collector.
>
> **18** Most certainly I tell you, whatever things you bind on earth will have been bound in heaven, and whatever things you release on earth will have been released in heaven.
>
> **19** Again, assuredly I tell you, that if two of you will agree on earth concerning anything that they will ask, it will be done for them by my Father who is in heaven.
>
> **20** For where two or three are gathered together in my name, there I am in the middle of them.”

#### Reading the passages together

- The process protects reputation, establishes testimony, and entrusts binding and loosing to the Church according to Christ's Word.

#### Law, Gospel, and confessional synthesis

The process protects reputation, establishes testimony, and entrusts binding and loosing to the Church according to Christ's Word. In a confessional Lutheran reading, this command is not a ladder by which sinners earn justification. The Law exposes sin and directs the baptized life; the Gospel forgives, creates faith, joins the believer to Christ, and brings forth the fruits named here. The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

#### Early Christian witnesses

**Didache 15.1 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Appoint, therefore, for yourselves bishops and deacons worthy of the Lord, men who are meek, not lovers of money, truthful, and proven; for they also render to you the service of prophets and teachers.

*Study significance:* Mission and pastoral care are entrusted to tested servants rather than self-appointed status seekers.

**Ignatius, Smyrnaeans 8 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Wherever the bishop shall appear, there let the multitude also be; even as, wherever Jesus Christ is, there is the catholic Church.

*Study significance:* The statement reflects an early concern for visible, ordered communion centered on Christ.

#### Lutheran confessional witnesses

**Augsburg Confession XIV**

> No one should publicly teach in the Church or administer the Sacraments unless he be regularly called.

*Study significance:* Christ's mission is carried out through ordered vocation rather than self-authorization.

**Small Catechism, Eighth Commandment**

> We should fear and love God that we may not deceitfully belie, betray, slander, or defame our neighbor, but defend him, speak well of him, and put the best construction on everything.

*Study significance:* Truthful correction and charitable interpretation belong together.

**Further confessional anchors:** Small Catechism: Fourth through Tenth Commandments; Augsburg Confession VI (new obedience); Augsburg Confession XXVIII (the Church does not invent works that bind consciences); Formula of Concord, Solid Declaration IV (good works as necessary fruits, not merits).

#### Practicing the command

Check facts, speak directly when safe, involve suitable witnesses and pastors, and use civil reporting channels when required.

#### Misuse caution

Matthew 18 is not a secrecy rule for abuse or crime.

#### Questions for examination and meditation

1. What exactly does Jesus command, forbid, promise, or warn in these passages, and who first receives the words?
2. Which words expose sin or false trust, and which words direct you to Christ's gift, forgiveness, or sustaining promise?
3. What concrete act of repentance, faith, mercy, or vocation would embody **address a sinning brother through ordered correction** this week without turning obedience into a basis of justification?

#### Prayer

Lord Jesus Christ, You call Your people to address a sinning brother through ordered correction. Forgive my failure, keep me in Your Word and gifts, and bear this fruit in me for the good of my neighbor and the glory of the Father. Amen.

<a id="command-025"></a>

### 25. Go and do likewise: become a neighbor in mercy

**Corpus:** Gospels  
**Scope:** Universal  
**Earlier-list crosswalk:** #36  
**Canonical passages:** Luke 10:25-37

#### Command-family wording

> Cross boundaries to give practical mercy to the person in need.

#### Scripture text (World English Bible)

##### Luke 10:25-37

> **25** Behold, a certain lawyer stood up and tested him, saying, “Teacher, what shall I do to inherit eternal life?”
>
> **26** He said to him, “What is written in the law? How do you read it?”
>
> **27** He answered, “You shall love the Lord your God with all your heart, with all your soul, with all your strength, and with all your mind; and your neighbor as yourself.”
>
> **28** He said to him, “You have answered correctly. Do this, and you will live.”
>
> **29** But he, desiring to justify himself, asked Jesus, “Who is my neighbor?”
>
> **30** Jesus answered, “A certain man was going down from Jerusalem to Jericho, and he fell among robbers, who both stripped him and beat him, and departed, leaving him half dead.
>
> **31** By chance a certain priest was going down that way. When he saw him, he passed by on the other side.
>
> **32** In the same way a Levite also, when he came to the place and saw him, passed by on the other side.
>
> **33** But a certain Samaritan, as he traveled, came where he was. When he saw him, he was moved with compassion,
>
> **34** came to him, and bound up his wounds, pouring on oil and wine. He set him on his own animal, brought him to an inn, and took care of him.
>
> **35** On the next day, when he departed, he took out two denarii, gave them to the host, and said to him, ‘Take care of him. Whatever you spend beyond that, I will repay you when I return.’
>
> **36** Now which of these three do you think seemed to be a neighbor to him who fell among the robbers?”
>
> **37** He said, “He who showed mercy on him.” Then Jesus said to him, “Go and do likewise.”

#### Reading the passages together

- The Good Samaritan exposes attempts to limit the neighbor and depicts mercy that sees, approaches, spends, and continues care.

#### Law, Gospel, and confessional synthesis

The Good Samaritan exposes attempts to limit the neighbor and depicts mercy that sees, approaches, spends, and continues care. In a confessional Lutheran reading, this command is not a ladder by which sinners earn justification. The Law exposes sin and directs the baptized life; the Gospel forgives, creates faith, joins the believer to Christ, and brings forth the fruits named here. The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

#### Early Christian witnesses

**Didache 1.5 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Give to everyone who asks you, and ask it not back; for the Father wills that to all should be given of our own blessings.

*Study significance:* The command is received as real generosity flowing from the Father's gifts.

**1 Clement 13.1-2 (Lightfoot/Roberts-Donaldson tradition, lightly modernized)**

> Let us be humble-minded, brethren, laying aside all haughtiness and pride and foolishness and angry feelings, especially remembering the words of the Lord Jesus: Be merciful, that you may obtain mercy; forgive, that it may be forgiven unto you.

*Study significance:* Clement explicitly uses Jesus' words to form a peaceful and humble congregation.

#### Lutheran confessional witnesses

**Small Catechism, Fifth Commandment**

> We should fear and love God that we may not hurt nor harm our neighbor in his body, but help and befriend him in every bodily need.

*Study significance:* Neighbor-love includes active bodily help, not merely refraining from violence.

**Augsburg Confession VI**

> This faith is bound to bring forth good fruits, and it is necessary to do good works commanded by God because of God's will; yet we should not rely on those works to merit justification before God.

*Study significance:* Good works are necessary fruits of faith, not merits that purchase God's favor.

**Further confessional anchors:** Small Catechism: Fourth through Tenth Commandments; Augsburg Confession VI (new obedience); Augsburg Confession XXVIII (the Church does not invent works that bind consciences); Formula of Concord, Solid Declaration IV (good works as necessary fruits, not merits).

#### Practicing the command

Notice rather than pass by, call or give competent help, contribute time and money, and cooperate across divisions.

#### Misuse caution

Prudent safety and competent help are compatible with mercy.

#### Questions for examination and meditation

1. What exactly does Jesus command, forbid, promise, or warn in these passages, and who first receives the words?
2. Which words expose sin or false trust, and which words direct you to Christ's gift, forgiveness, or sustaining promise?
3. What concrete act of repentance, faith, mercy, or vocation would embody **go and do likewise: become a neighbor in mercy** this week without turning obedience into a basis of justification?

#### Prayer

Lord Jesus Christ, You call Your people to go and do likewise: become a neighbor in mercy. Forgive my failure, keep me in Your Word and gifts, and bear this fruit in me for the good of my neighbor and the glory of the Father. Amen.

### 26. Serve Christ in the hungry, stranger, sick, and imprisoned

**Corpus:** Gospels  
**Scope:** Universal fruit of faith  
**Earlier-list crosswalk:** #37  
**Canonical passages:** Matthew 25:31-46

#### Command-family wording

> Attend to bodily need in Christ's name, recognizing that the King receives such service as done to Him.

#### Scripture text (World English Bible)

##### Matthew 25:31-46

> **31** “But when the Son of Man comes in his glory, and all the holy angels with him, then he will sit on the throne of his glory.
>
> **32** Before him all the nations will be gathered, and he will separate them one from another, as a shepherd separates the sheep from the goats.
>
> **33** He will set the sheep on his right hand, but the goats on the left.
>
> **34** Then the King will tell those on his right hand, ‘Come, blessed of my Father, inherit the Kingdom prepared for you from the foundation of the world;
>
> **35** for I was hungry and you gave me food to eat. I was thirsty and you gave me drink. I was a stranger and you took me in.
>
> **36** I was naked and you clothed me. I was sick and you visited me. I was in prison and you came to me.’
>
> **37** “Then the righteous will answer him, saying, ‘Lord, when did we see you hungry and feed you, or thirsty and give you a drink?
>
> **38** When did we see you as a stranger and take you in, or naked and clothe you?
>
> **39** When did we see you sick or in prison and come to you?’
>
> **40** “The King will answer them, ‘Most certainly I tell you, because you did it to one of the least of these my brothers, you did it to me.’
>
> **41** Then he will say also to those on the left hand, ‘Depart from me, you cursed, into the eternal fire which is prepared for the devil and his angels;
>
> **42** for I was hungry, and you didn’t give me food to eat; I was thirsty, and you gave me no drink;
>
> **43** I was a stranger, and you didn’t take me in; naked, and you didn’t clothe me; sick, and in prison, and you didn’t visit me.’
>
> **44** “Then they will also answer, saying, ‘Lord, when did we see you hungry, or thirsty, or a stranger, or naked, or sick, or in prison, and didn’t help you?’
>
> **45** “Then he will answer them, saying, ‘Most certainly I tell you, because you didn’t do it to one of the least of these, you didn’t do it to me.’
>
> **46** These will go away into eternal punishment, but the righteous into eternal life.”

#### Reading the passages together

- Works of mercy are public evidence of living faith, not a competing basis of justification.

#### Law, Gospel, and confessional synthesis

Works of mercy are public evidence of living faith, not a competing basis of justification. In a confessional Lutheran reading, this command is not a ladder by which sinners earn justification. The Law exposes sin and directs the baptized life; the Gospel forgives, creates faith, joins the believer to Christ, and brings forth the fruits named here. The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

#### Early Christian witnesses

**Didache 1.5 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Give to everyone who asks you, and ask it not back; for the Father wills that to all should be given of our own blessings.

*Study significance:* The command is received as real generosity flowing from the Father's gifts.

**1 Clement 49.1 (Lightfoot translation, lightly modernized)**

> Let the one who has love in Christ keep the commandments of Christ.

*Study significance:* The sentence binds Christian love to Christ's actual commands rather than sentiment alone.

#### Lutheran confessional witnesses

**Small Catechism, Fifth Commandment**

> We should fear and love God that we may not hurt nor harm our neighbor in his body, but help and befriend him in every bodily need.

*Study significance:* Neighbor-love includes active bodily help, not merely refraining from violence.

**Small Catechism, Seventh Commandment**

> We should fear and love God that we may not take our neighbor's money or property, nor get them by false ware or dealing, but help him to improve and protect his property and business.

*Study significance:* Generosity and economic integrity seek the neighbor's material good.

**Further confessional anchors:** Small Catechism: Fourth through Tenth Commandments; Augsburg Confession VI (new obedience); Augsburg Confession XXVIII (the Church does not invent works that bind consciences); Formula of Concord, Solid Declaration IV (good works as necessary fruits, not merits).

#### Practicing the command

Feed and visit, welcome strangers, support medical and prison ministry, and advocate for humane treatment.

#### Misuse caution

Do not turn this passage into salvation by charitable scorekeeping.

#### Questions for examination and meditation

1. What exactly does Jesus command, forbid, promise, or warn in these passages, and who first receives the words?
2. Which words expose sin or false trust, and which words direct you to Christ's gift, forgiveness, or sustaining promise?
3. What concrete act of repentance, faith, mercy, or vocation would embody **serve christ in the hungry, stranger, sick, and imprisoned** this week without turning obedience into a basis of justification?

#### Prayer

Lord Jesus Christ, You call Your people to serve christ in the hungry, stranger, sick, and imprisoned. Forgive my failure, keep me in Your Word and gifts, and bear this fruit in me for the good of my neighbor and the glory of the Father. Amen.

## Humility, Vocation, and Service

Christ overturns status-seeking and places greatness in receptive humility and service.

<a id="command-028"></a>

### 28. Turn and receive the kingdom with childlike humility

**Corpus:** Gospels  
**Scope:** Universal  
**Earlier-list crosswalk:** #39, #40, #41  
**Canonical passages:** Matthew 18:3-4; Mark 9:33-37; 10:15; Luke 9:46-48; 18:17

#### Command-family wording

> Abandon status-seeking and receive God's kingdom as a dependent child receives a gift.

#### Scripture text (World English Bible)

##### Matthew 18:3-4

> **3** and said, “Most certainly I tell you, unless you turn and become as little children, you will in no way enter into the Kingdom of Heaven.
>
> **4** Whoever therefore humbles himself as this little child is the greatest in the Kingdom of Heaven.

##### Mark 9:33-37

> **33** He came to Capernaum, and when he was in the house he asked them, “What were you arguing among yourselves on the way?”
>
> **34** But they were silent, for they had disputed with one another on the way about who was the greatest.
>
> **35** He sat down and called the twelve; and he said to them, “If any man wants to be first, he shall be last of all, and servant of all.”
>
> **36** He took a little child and set him in the middle of them. Taking him in his arms, he said to them,
>
> **37** “Whoever receives one such little child in my name receives me; and whoever receives me, doesn’t receive me, but him who sent me.”

##### Mark 10:15

> **15** Most certainly I tell you, whoever will not receive God’s Kingdom like a little child, he will in no way enter into it.”

##### Luke 9:46-48

> **46** An argument arose among them about which of them was the greatest.
>
> **47** Jesus, perceiving the reasoning of their hearts, took a little child, and set him by his side,
>
> **48** and said to them, “Whoever receives this little child in my name receives me. Whoever receives me receives him who sent me. For whoever is least among you all, this one will be great.”

##### Luke 18:17

> **17** Most certainly, I tell you, whoever doesn’t receive God’s Kingdom like a little child, he will in no way enter into it.”

#### Reading the passages together

- The child signifies low status, dependence, and receptivity; entry is received, not achieved.

#### Law, Gospel, and confessional synthesis

The child signifies low status, dependence, and receptivity; entry is received, not achieved. In a confessional Lutheran reading, this command is not a ladder by which sinners earn justification. The Law exposes sin and directs the baptized life; the Gospel forgives, creates faith, joins the believer to Christ, and brings forth the fruits named here. The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

#### Early Christian witnesses

**1 Clement 13.1-2 (Lightfoot/Roberts-Donaldson tradition, lightly modernized)**

> Let us be humble-minded, brethren, laying aside all haughtiness and pride and foolishness and angry feelings, especially remembering the words of the Lord Jesus: Be merciful, that you may obtain mercy; forgive, that it may be forgiven unto you.

*Study significance:* Clement explicitly uses Jesus' words to form a peaceful and humble congregation.

**Didache 1.1-2 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> There are two ways, one of life and one of death; but there is a great difference between these two ways. The way of life, then, is this: First, you shall love God who made you; second, your neighbor as yourself; and all things whatsoever you would not have done to you, do not do to another.

*Study significance:* An early Christian catechetical summary joins love of God, love of neighbor, and concrete conduct.

#### Lutheran confessional witnesses

**Augsburg Confession IV**

> People cannot be justified before God by their own strength, merits, or works, but are freely justified for Christ's sake, through faith, when they believe that they are received into favor and that their sins are forgiven for Christ's sake.

*Study significance:* The command must never be turned into a competing basis of justification.

**Augsburg Confession VI**

> This faith is bound to bring forth good fruits, and it is necessary to do good works commanded by God because of God's will; yet we should not rely on those works to merit justification before God.

*Study significance:* Good works are necessary fruits of faith, not merits that purchase God's favor.

**Further confessional anchors:** Small Catechism: Fourth and Fifth Commandments; Augsburg Confession VI and XVI (new obedience and civil life); Augsburg Confession XIV (ordered public ministry); Treatise on the Power and Primacy of the Pope (service rather than domination).

#### Practicing the command

Confess dependence on grace, listen before asserting rank, welcome low-status members, and resist ministry as a ladder.

#### Misuse caution

Childlikeness is not childishness or surrender of mature discernment.

#### Questions for examination and meditation

1. What exactly does Jesus command, forbid, promise, or warn in these passages, and who first receives the words?
2. Which words expose sin or false trust, and which words direct you to Christ's gift, forgiveness, or sustaining promise?
3. What concrete act of repentance, faith, mercy, or vocation would embody **turn and receive the kingdom with childlike humility** this week without turning obedience into a basis of justification?

#### Prayer

Lord Jesus Christ, You call Your people to turn and receive the kingdom with childlike humility. Forgive my failure, keep me in Your Word and gifts, and bear this fruit in me for the good of my neighbor and the glory of the Father. Amen.

### 29. Let the little children come to Jesus

**Corpus:** Gospels  
**Scope:** Church and household  
**Earlier-list crosswalk:** #42  
**Canonical passages:** Matthew 19:13-15; Mark 10:13-16; Luke 18:15-17

#### Command-family wording

> Do not hinder children; bring them to Christ to receive His blessing and kingdom.

#### Scripture text (World English Bible)

##### Matthew 19:13-15

> **13** Then little children were brought to him that he should lay his hands on them and pray; and the disciples rebuked them.
>
> **14** But Jesus said, “Allow the little children, and don’t forbid them to come to me; for the Kingdom of Heaven belongs to ones like these.”
>
> **15** He laid his hands on them, and departed from there.

##### Mark 10:13-16

> **13** They were bringing to him little children, that he should touch them, but the disciples rebuked those who were bringing them.
>
> **14** But when Jesus saw it, he was moved with indignation and said to them, “Allow the little children to come to me! Don’t forbid them, for God’s Kingdom belongs to such as these.
>
> **15** Most certainly I tell you, whoever will not receive God’s Kingdom like a little child, he will in no way enter into it.”
>
> **16** He took them in his arms and blessed them, laying his hands on them.

##### Luke 18:15-17

> **15** They were also bringing their babies to him, that he might touch them. But when the disciples saw it, they rebuked them.
>
> **16** Jesus summoned them, saying, “Allow the little children to come to me, and don’t hinder them, for God’s Kingdom belongs to such as these.
>
> **17** Most certainly, I tell you, whoever doesn’t receive God’s Kingdom like a little child, he will in no way enter into it.”

#### Reading the passages together

- Infants and children are genuine recipients of Christ's kingdom; adults and the Church must give access to His gifts.

#### Law, Gospel, and confessional synthesis

Infants and children are genuine recipients of Christ's kingdom; adults and the Church must give access to His gifts. In a confessional Lutheran reading, this command is not a ladder by which sinners earn justification. The Law exposes sin and directs the baptized life; the Gospel forgives, creates faith, joins the believer to Christ, and brings forth the fruits named here. The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

#### Early Christian witnesses

**Didache 7.1 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Concerning baptism, baptize thus: Having first rehearsed all these things, baptize in the name of the Father and of the Son and of the Holy Spirit, in living water.

*Study significance:* The early church explicitly connects catechesis, water, and the Triune name.

**Justin Martyr, First Apology 61 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Then they are brought by us where there is water and are regenerated in the same manner in which we were ourselves regenerated: in the name of God, the Father and Lord of the universe, and of our Savior Jesus Christ, and of the Holy Spirit.

*Study significance:* This second-century description witnesses to baptism with water and the Triune name.

#### Lutheran confessional witnesses

**Small Catechism, Baptism III**

> It is not the water indeed that does these things, but the word of God which is in and with the water, and faith which trusts this word of God in the water.

*Study significance:* Baptism is God's act through water joined to His word and promise.

**Augsburg Confession V**

> That we may obtain this faith, the Ministry of Teaching the Gospel and administering the Sacraments was instituted. Through the Word and Sacraments, as through instruments, the Holy Spirit is given, who works faith where and when it pleases God.

*Study significance:* Faith is created and sustained through Christ's appointed means rather than self-generated resolve.

**Further confessional anchors:** Small Catechism: Fourth and Fifth Commandments; Augsburg Confession VI and XVI (new obedience and civil life); Augsburg Confession XIV (ordered public ministry); Treatise on the Power and Primacy of the Pope (service rather than domination).

#### Practicing the command

Baptize, teach, pray with, include, and protect children; support households in catechesis.

#### Misuse caution

This command condemns spiritual neglect and every form of abuse under religious authority.

#### Questions for examination and meditation

1. What exactly does Jesus command, forbid, promise, or warn in these passages, and who first receives the words?
2. Which words expose sin or false trust, and which words direct you to Christ's gift, forgiveness, or sustaining promise?
3. What concrete act of repentance, faith, mercy, or vocation would embody **let the little children come to jesus** this week without turning obedience into a basis of justification?

#### Prayer

Lord Jesus Christ, You call Your people to let the little children come to jesus. Forgive my failure, keep me in Your Word and gifts, and bear this fruit in me for the good of my neighbor and the glory of the Father. Amen.

#### Command-family wording

> Guard vulnerable believers and never treat them as expendable.

> **6** but whoever causes one of these little ones who believe in me to stumble, it would be better for him if a huge millstone were hung around his neck and that he were sunk in the depths of the sea.
>
> **10** See that you don’t despise one of these little ones, for I tell you that in heaven their angels always see the face of my Father who is in heaven.
>
> **14** Even so it is not the will of your Father who is in heaven that one of these little ones should perish.

#### Reading the passages together

- Little ones include children and lowly disciples; Jesus attaches severe warnings to causing them to stumble.

#### Law, Gospel, and confessional synthesis

Little ones include children and lowly disciples; Jesus attaches severe warnings to causing them to stumble. In a confessional Lutheran reading, this command is not a ladder by which sinners earn justification. The Law exposes sin and directs the baptized life; the Gospel forgives, creates faith, joins the believer to Christ, and brings forth the fruits named here. The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

#### Early Christian witnesses

**Didache 2.2 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

*Study significance:* This early catechesis applies the way of life to the protection of bodies, marriage, property, and children.

**1 Clement 13.1-2 (Lightfoot/Roberts-Donaldson tradition, lightly modernized)**

> Let us be humble-minded, brethren, laying aside all haughtiness and pride and foolishness and angry feelings, especially remembering the words of the Lord Jesus: Be merciful, that you may obtain mercy; forgive, that it may be forgiven unto you.

*Study significance:* Clement explicitly uses Jesus' words to form a peaceful and humble congregation.

**Small Catechism, Fifth Commandment**

> We should fear and love God that we may not hurt nor harm our neighbor in his body, but help and befriend him in every bodily need.

**Small Catechism, Eighth Commandment**

> We should fear and love God that we may not deceitfully belie, betray, slander, or defame our neighbor, but defend him, speak well of him, and put the best construction on everything.

**Further confessional anchors:** Small Catechism: Fourth and Fifth Commandments; Augsburg Confession VI and XVI (new obedience and civil life); Augsburg Confession XIV (ordered public ministry); Treatise on the Power and Primacy of the Pope (service rather than domination).

Create safeguarding structures, take allegations seriously, avoid contempt for weak faith, and remove foreseeable stumbling blocks.

#### Misuse caution

#### Questions for examination and meditation

1. What exactly does Jesus command, forbid, promise, or warn in these passages, and who first receives the words?
2. Which words expose sin or false trust, and which words direct you to Christ's gift, forgiveness, or sustaining promise?
3. What concrete act of repentance, faith, mercy, or vocation would embody **do not despise the little ones** this week without turning obedience into a basis of justification?

#### Prayer

Lord Jesus Christ, You call Your people to do not despise the little ones. Forgive my failure, keep me in Your Word and gifts, and bear this fruit in me for the good of my neighbor and the glory of the Father. Amen.

<a id="command-031"></a>

### 31. Become the servant of all

**Corpus:** Gospels  
**Scope:** Universal, especially leaders  
**Earlier-list crosswalk:** #44, #45  
**Canonical passages:** Matthew 20:25-28; 23:11; Mark 9:35; 10:42-45; Luke 22:25-27

#### Command-family wording

> Seek greatness through service rather than domination.

#### Scripture text (World English Bible)

##### Matthew 20:25-28

> **25** But Jesus summoned them, and said, “You know that the rulers of the nations lord it over them, and their great ones exercise authority over them.
>
> **26** It shall not be so among you; but whoever desires to become great among you shall be your servant.
>
> **27** Whoever desires to be first among you shall be your bondservant,
>
> **28** even as the Son of Man came not to be served, but to serve, and to give his life as a ransom for many.”

##### Matthew 23:11

> **11** But he who is greatest among you will be your servant.

##### Mark 9:35

> **35** He sat down and called the twelve; and he said to them, “If any man wants to be first, he shall be last of all, and servant of all.”

##### Mark 10:42-45

> **42** Jesus summoned them and said to them, “You know that they who are recognized as rulers over the nations lord it over them, and their great ones exercise authority over them.
>
> **43** But it shall not be so among you, but whoever wants to become great among you shall be your servant.
>
> **44** Whoever of you wants to become first among you shall be bondservant of all.
>
> **45** For the Son of Man also came not to be served but to serve, and to give his life as a ransom for many.”

##### Luke 22:25-27

> **25** He said to them, “The kings of the nations lord it over them, and those who have authority over them are called ‘benefactors.’
>
> **26** But not so with you. Rather, the one who is greater among you, let him become as the younger, and one who is governing, as one who serves.
>
> **27** For who is greater, one who sits at the table, or one who serves? Isn’t it he who sits at the table? But I am among you as one who serves.

#### Reading the passages together

- Christian authority remains real but takes the form of responsibility under the ransom-giving Son of Man.

#### Law, Gospel, and confessional synthesis

Christian authority remains real but takes the form of responsibility under the ransom-giving Son of Man. In a confessional Lutheran reading, this command is not a ladder by which sinners earn justification. The Law exposes sin and directs the baptized life; the Gospel forgives, creates faith, joins the believer to Christ, and brings forth the fruits named here. The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

#### Early Christian witnesses

**1 Clement 13.1-2 (Lightfoot/Roberts-Donaldson tradition, lightly modernized)**

> Let us be humble-minded, brethren, laying aside all haughtiness and pride and foolishness and angry feelings, especially remembering the words of the Lord Jesus: Be merciful, that you may obtain mercy; forgive, that it may be forgiven unto you.

*Study significance:* Clement explicitly uses Jesus' words to form a peaceful and humble congregation.

**1 Clement 49.1 (Lightfoot translation, lightly modernized)**

> Let the one who has love in Christ keep the commandments of Christ.

*Study significance:* The sentence binds Christian love to Christ's actual commands rather than sentiment alone.

#### Lutheran confessional witnesses

**Augsburg Confession VI**

> This faith is bound to bring forth good fruits, and it is necessary to do good works commanded by God because of God's will; yet we should not rely on those works to merit justification before God.

*Study significance:* Good works are necessary fruits of faith, not merits that purchase God's favor.

**Small Catechism, Fifth Commandment**

> We should fear and love God that we may not hurt nor harm our neighbor in his body, but help and befriend him in every bodily need.

*Study significance:* Neighbor-love includes active bodily help, not merely refraining from violence.

**Further confessional anchors:** Small Catechism: Fourth and Fifth Commandments; Augsburg Confession VI and XVI (new obedience and civil life); Augsburg Confession XIV (ordered public ministry); Treatise on the Power and Primacy of the Pope (service rather than domination).

#### Practicing the command

Use authority transparently, take lowly tasks, develop others, accept accountability, and measure leadership by care.

#### Misuse caution

Servant leadership does not eliminate proper office, boundaries, or discipline.

#### Questions for examination and meditation

1. What exactly does Jesus command, forbid, promise, or warn in these passages, and who first receives the words?
2. Which words expose sin or false trust, and which words direct you to Christ's gift, forgiveness, or sustaining promise?
3. What concrete act of repentance, faith, mercy, or vocation would embody **become the servant of all** this week without turning obedience into a basis of justification?

#### Prayer

Lord Jesus Christ, You call Your people to become the servant of all. Forgive my failure, keep me in Your Word and gifts, and bear this fruit in me for the good of my neighbor and the glory of the Father. Amen.

<a id="command-032"></a>

### 32. Do not exalt yourself or chase religious titles

**Corpus:** Gospels  
**Scope:** Universal, especially religious leaders  
**Earlier-list crosswalk:** #46  
**Canonical passages:** Matthew 23:8-12; Luke 14:7-11; 18:9-14

#### Command-family wording

> Take the lower place and resist titles as grounds of superiority.

#### Scripture text (World English Bible)

##### Matthew 23:8-12

> **8** But you are not to be called ‘Rabbi’, for one is your teacher, the Christ, and all of you are brothers.
>
> **9** Call no man on the earth your father, for one is your Father, he who is in heaven.
>
> **10** Neither be called masters, for one is your master, the Christ.
>
> **11** But he who is greatest among you will be your servant.
>
> **12** Whoever exalts himself will be humbled, and whoever humbles himself will be exalted.

##### Luke 14:7-11

> **7** He spoke a parable to those who were invited, when he noticed how they chose the best seats, and said to them,
>
> **8** “When you are invited by anyone to a wedding feast, don’t sit in the best seat, since perhaps someone more honorable than you might be invited by him,
>
> **9** and he who invited both of you would come and tell you, ‘Make room for this person.’ Then you would begin, with shame, to take the lowest place.
>
> **10** But when you are invited, go and sit in the lowest place, so that when he who invited you comes, he may tell you, ‘Friend, move up higher.’ Then you will be honored in the presence of all who sit at the table with you.
>
> **11** For everyone who exalts himself will be humbled, and whoever humbles himself will be exalted.”

##### Luke 18:9-14

> **9** He also spoke this parable to certain people who were convinced of their own righteousness, and who despised all others:
>
> **10** “Two men went up into the temple to pray; one was a Pharisee, and the other was a tax collector.
>
> **11** The Pharisee stood and prayed by himself like this: ‘God, I thank you that I am not like the rest of men: extortionists, unrighteous, adulterers, or even like this tax collector.
>
> **12** I fast twice a week. I give tithes of all that I get.’
>
> **13** But the tax collector, standing far away, wouldn’t even lift up his eyes to heaven, but beat his chest, saying, ‘God, be merciful to me, a sinner!’
>
> **14** I tell you, this man went down to his house justified rather than the other; for everyone who exalts himself will be humbled, but he who humbles himself will be exalted.”

#### Reading the passages together

- Jesus attacks honor that displaces the one Father and Teacher; true humility receives mercy rather than comparing resumes.

#### Law, Gospel, and confessional synthesis

Jesus attacks honor that displaces the one Father and Teacher; true humility receives mercy rather than comparing resumes. In a confessional Lutheran reading, this command is not a ladder by which sinners earn justification. The Law exposes sin and directs the baptized life; the Gospel forgives, creates faith, joins the believer to Christ, and brings forth the fruits named here. The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

#### Early Christian witnesses

**1 Clement 13.1-2 (Lightfoot/Roberts-Donaldson tradition, lightly modernized)**

> Let us be humble-minded, brethren, laying aside all haughtiness and pride and foolishness and angry feelings, especially remembering the words of the Lord Jesus: Be merciful, that you may obtain mercy; forgive, that it may be forgiven unto you.

*Study significance:* Clement explicitly uses Jesus' words to form a peaceful and humble congregation.

**Didache 15.1 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Appoint, therefore, for yourselves bishops and deacons worthy of the Lord, men who are meek, not lovers of money, truthful, and proven; for they also render to you the service of prophets and teachers.

*Study significance:* Mission and pastoral care are entrusted to tested servants rather than self-appointed status seekers.

#### Lutheran confessional witnesses

**Augsburg Confession XIV**

> No one should publicly teach in the Church or administer the Sacraments unless he be regularly called.

*Study significance:* Christ's mission is carried out through ordered vocation rather than self-authorization.

**Augsburg Confession VI**

> This faith is bound to bring forth good fruits, and it is necessary to do good works commanded by God because of God's will; yet we should not rely on those works to merit justification before God.

*Study significance:* Good works are necessary fruits of faith, not merits that purchase God's favor.

**Further confessional anchors:** Small Catechism: Fourth and Fifth Commandments; Augsburg Confession VI and XVI (new obedience and civil life); Augsburg Confession XIV (ordered public ministry); Treatise on the Power and Primacy of the Pope (service rather than domination).

#### Practicing the command

Use titles functionally, confess sins, defer honor, create accountable cultures, and welcome unnoticed tasks.

#### Misuse caution

The text does not prohibit every ordinary family or vocational title.

#### Questions for examination and meditation

1. What exactly does Jesus command, forbid, promise, or warn in these passages, and who first receives the words?
2. Which words expose sin or false trust, and which words direct you to Christ's gift, forgiveness, or sustaining promise?
3. What concrete act of repentance, faith, mercy, or vocation would embody **do not exalt yourself or chase religious titles** this week without turning obedience into a basis of justification?

#### Prayer

Lord Jesus Christ, You call Your people to do not exalt yourself or chase religious titles. Forgive my failure, keep me in Your Word and gifts, and bear this fruit in me for the good of my neighbor and the glory of the Father. Amen.

> **12** So when he had washed their feet, put his outer garment back on, and sat down again, he said to them, “Do you know what I have done to you?
>
> **13** You call me, ‘Teacher’ and ‘Lord.’ You say so correctly, for so I am.
>

>
> **16** Most certainly I tell you, a servant is not greater than his lord, neither is one who is sent greater than he who sent him.
>
> **17** If you know these things, blessed are you if you do them.

#### Law, Gospel, and confessional synthesis

The foot washing first points to Christ's cleansing gift and then gives an example of self-lowering care. In a confessional Lutheran reading, this command is not a ladder by which sinners earn justification. The Law exposes sin and directs the baptized life; the Gospel forgives, creates faith, joins the believer to Christ, and brings forth the fruits named here. The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

#### Early Christian witnesses

**1 Clement 13.1-2 (Lightfoot/Roberts-Donaldson tradition, lightly modernized)**

> Let us be humble-minded, brethren, laying aside all haughtiness and pride and foolishness and angry feelings, especially remembering the words of the Lord Jesus: Be merciful, that you may obtain mercy; forgive, that it may be forgiven unto you.

*Study significance:* Clement explicitly uses Jesus' words to form a peaceful and humble congregation.

**1 Clement 49.1 (Lightfoot translation, lightly modernized)**

> Let the one who has love in Christ keep the commandments of Christ.

*Study significance:* The sentence binds Christian love to Christ's actual commands rather than sentiment alone.

#### Lutheran confessional witnesses

**Small Catechism, Fifth Commandment**

> We should fear and love God that we may not hurt nor harm our neighbor in his body, but help and befriend him in every bodily need.

*Study significance:* Neighbor-love includes active bodily help, not merely refraining from violence.

**Augsburg Confession VI**

> This faith is bound to bring forth good fruits, and it is necessary to do good works commanded by God because of God's will; yet we should not rely on those works to merit justification before God.

*Study significance:* Good works are necessary fruits of faith, not merits that purchase God's favor.

## Holiness, Integrity, and Discernment

Jesus reaches the heart, calls for truthful conduct, and teaches discerning rather than hypocritical judgment.

<a id="command-034"></a>

### 34. Speak truthfully without manipulative oaths

**Corpus:** Gospels  
**Scope:** Universal  
**Earlier-list crosswalk:** #48, #49  
**Canonical passages:** Matthew 5:33-37

#### Command-family wording

> Let yes mean yes and no mean no; do not use sacred language to prop up deceit.

#### Scripture text (World English Bible)

##### Matthew 5:33-37

> **33** “Again you have heard that it was said to the ancient ones, ‘You shall not make false vows, but shall perform to the Lord your vows,’
>
> **34** but I tell you, don’t swear at all: neither by heaven, for it is the throne of God;
>
> **35** nor by the earth, for it is the footstool of his feet; nor by Jerusalem, for it is the city of the great King.
>
> **36** Neither shall you swear by your head, for you can’t make one hair white or black.
>
> **37** But let your ‘Yes’ be ‘Yes’ and your ‘No’ be ‘No.’ Whatever is more than these is of the evil one.

#### Reading the passages together

- Jesus condemns casuistry that reserves honesty for specially worded vows; lawful solemn oaths can still serve the neighbor.

#### Law, Gospel, and confessional synthesis

Jesus condemns casuistry that reserves honesty for specially worded vows; lawful solemn oaths can still serve the neighbor. In a confessional Lutheran reading, this command is not a ladder by which sinners earn justification. The Law exposes sin and directs the baptized life; the Gospel forgives, creates faith, joins the believer to Christ, and brings forth the fruits named here. The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

#### Early Christian witnesses

**Didache 2.4-5; 4.4 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> You shall not be double-minded nor double-tongued, for to be double-tongued is a snare of death. Your speech shall not be false or empty, but fulfilled by deed.

*Study significance:* The witness treats integrity as speech verified by action.

**1 Clement 49.1 (Lightfoot translation, lightly modernized)**

> Let the one who has love in Christ keep the commandments of Christ.

*Study significance:* The sentence binds Christian love to Christ's actual commands rather than sentiment alone.

#### Lutheran confessional witnesses

**Small Catechism, Eighth Commandment**

> We should fear and love God that we may not deceitfully belie, betray, slander, or defame our neighbor, but defend him, speak well of him, and put the best construction on everything.

*Study significance:* Truthful correction and charitable interpretation belong together.

**Augsburg Confession VI**

> This faith is bound to bring forth good fruits, and it is necessary to do good works commanded by God because of God's will; yet we should not rely on those works to merit justification before God.

*Study significance:* Good works are necessary fruits of faith, not merits that purchase God's favor.

**Further confessional anchors:** Small Catechism: Ten Commandments; Augsburg Confession VI and XVI (new obedience and vocation); Formula of Concord, Solid Declaration VI (the Law as guide for the regenerate); Large Catechism: exposition of the relevant commandment.

#### Practicing the command

Use plain speech, keep promises, correct exaggeration, testify truthfully, and avoid invoking God to manipulate trust.

#### Misuse caution

Do not use this command to reject all lawful testimony without considering the wider biblical witness.

#### Questions for examination and meditation

1. What exactly does Jesus command, forbid, promise, or warn in these passages, and who first receives the words?
2. Which words expose sin or false trust, and which words direct you to Christ's gift, forgiveness, or sustaining promise?
3. What concrete act of repentance, faith, mercy, or vocation would embody **speak truthfully without manipulative oaths** this week without turning obedience into a basis of justification?

#### Prayer

Lord Jesus Christ, You call Your people to speak truthfully without manipulative oaths. Forgive my failure, keep me in Your Word and gifts, and bear this fruit in me for the good of my neighbor and the glory of the Father. Amen.

<a id="command-035"></a>

### 35. Reject lust and cultivate chastity

**Corpus:** Gospels  
**Scope:** Universal  
**Earlier-list crosswalk:** #50  
**Canonical passages:** Matthew 5:27-30

#### Command-family wording

> Do not nurture a look or imagination that turns another person into an object for sexual possession.

#### Scripture text (World English Bible)

##### Matthew 5:27-30

> **27** “You have heard that it was said, ‘You shall not commit adultery;’
>
> **28** but I tell you that everyone who gazes at a woman to lust after her has committed adultery with her already in his heart.
>
> **29** If your right eye causes you to stumble, pluck it out and throw it away from you. For it is more profitable for you that one of your members should perish than for your whole body to be cast into Gehenna.
>
> **30** If your right hand causes you to stumble, cut it off, and throw it away from you. For it is more profitable for you that one of your members should perish, than for your whole body to be cast into Gehenna.

#### Reading the passages together

- Jesus carries the Sixth Commandment into the heart and condemns desire deliberately entertained against God's order.

#### Law, Gospel, and confessional synthesis

Jesus carries the Sixth Commandment into the heart and condemns desire deliberately entertained against God's order. In a confessional Lutheran reading, this command is not a ladder by which sinners earn justification. The Law exposes sin and directs the baptized life; the Gospel forgives, creates faith, joins the believer to Christ, and brings forth the fruits named here. The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

#### Early Christian witnesses

**Justin Martyr, First Apology 15 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Concerning chastity, He uttered such sentiments as these: Whoever looks upon a woman to lust after her has committed adultery with her already in his heart before God.

*Study significance:* Justin quotes Jesus as the standard for inward as well as outward chastity.

**Didache 2.2 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> You shall not commit murder; you shall not commit adultery; you shall not commit sexual immorality; you shall not steal; you shall not practice magic or witchcraft; you shall not murder a child by abortion nor kill that which is born.

*Study significance:* This early catechesis applies the way of life to the protection of bodies, marriage, property, and children.

#### Lutheran confessional witnesses

**Small Catechism, Sixth Commandment**

> We should fear and love God that we may lead a chaste and decent life in words and deeds, and each love and honor his spouse.

*Study significance:* Jesus' teaching about desire and marriage is received as positive chastity and faithful honor.

**Augsburg Confession VI**

> This faith is bound to bring forth good fruits, and it is necessary to do good works commanded by God because of God's will; yet we should not rely on those works to merit justification before God.

*Study significance:* Good works are necessary fruits of faith, not merits that purchase God's favor.

**Further confessional anchors:** Small Catechism: Ten Commandments; Augsburg Confession VI and XVI (new obedience and vocation); Formula of Concord, Solid Declaration VI (the Law as guide for the regenerate); Large Catechism: exposition of the relevant commandment.

#### Practicing the command

Interrupt the gaze, limit media that trains lust, seek confession and accountability, honor bodies and marriages.

#### Misuse caution

Do not shift responsibility to the person being viewed or use shame as a substitute for help.

#### Questions for examination and meditation

1. What exactly does Jesus command, forbid, promise, or warn in these passages, and who first receives the words?
2. Which words expose sin or false trust, and which words direct you to Christ's gift, forgiveness, or sustaining promise?
3. What concrete act of repentance, faith, mercy, or vocation would embody **reject lust and cultivate chastity** this week without turning obedience into a basis of justification?

#### Prayer

Lord Jesus Christ, You call Your people to reject lust and cultivate chastity. Forgive my failure, keep me in Your Word and gifts, and bear this fruit in me for the good of my neighbor and the glory of the Father. Amen.

<a id="command-036"></a>

### 36. Remove occasions of sin and leave the old life

**Corpus:** Gospels  
**Scope:** Universal principle with personal and text-critical settings  
**Earlier-list crosswalk:** #51  
**Canonical passages:** Matthew 5:29-30; 18:8-9; Mark 9:42-48; John 5:14; 8:11 with textual note

#### Command-family wording

> Take radical, non-literal action against what repeatedly leads into sin.

#### Scripture text (World English Bible)

##### Matthew 5:29-30

> **29** If your right eye causes you to stumble, pluck it out and throw it away from you. For it is more profitable for you that one of your members should perish than for your whole body to be cast into Gehenna.
>
> **30** If your right hand causes you to stumble, cut it off, and throw it away from you. For it is more profitable for you that one of your members should perish, than for your whole body to be cast into Gehenna.

##### Matthew 18:8-9

> **8** If your hand or your foot causes you to stumble, cut it off and cast it from you. It is better for you to enter into life maimed or crippled, rather than having two hands or two feet to be cast into the eternal fire.
>
> **9** If your eye causes you to stumble, pluck it out and cast it from you. It is better for you to enter into life with one eye, rather than having two eyes to be cast into the Gehenna of fire.

##### Mark 9:42-48

> **42** “Whoever will cause one of these little ones who believe in me to stumble, it would be better for him if he were thrown into the sea with a millstone hung around his neck.
>
> **43** If your hand causes you to stumble, cut it off. It is better for you to enter into life maimed, rather than having your two hands to go into Gehenna, into the unquenchable fire,
>
> **44** ‘where their worm doesn’t die, and the fire is not quenched.’
>
> **45** If your foot causes you to stumble, cut it off. It is better for you to enter into life lame, rather than having your two feet to be cast into Gehenna, into the fire that will never be quenched-
>
> **46** ‘where their worm doesn’t die, and the fire is not quenched.’
>
> **47** If your eye causes you to stumble, throw it out. It is better for you to enter into God’s Kingdom with one eye, rather than having two eyes to be cast into the Gehenna of fire,
>
> **48** ‘where their worm doesn’t die, and the fire is not quenched.’

##### John 5:14

> **14** Afterward Jesus found him in the temple and said to him, “Behold, you are made well. Sin no more, so that nothing worse happens to you.”

##### John 8:11

> **11** She said, “No one, Lord.” Jesus said, “Neither do I condemn you. Go your way. From now on, sin no more.”

**Textual note: John 7:53-8:11 is absent from the earliest manuscripts and appears in different locations in some later witnesses. The narrative is retained here with its textual history clearly marked; no doctrine should depend on this passage alone.**

#### Reading the passages together

- The bodily hyperbole communicates sin's deadly seriousness, not a mandate for mutilation; forgiveness gives a new direction.

#### Law, Gospel, and confessional synthesis

The bodily hyperbole communicates sin's deadly seriousness, not a mandate for mutilation; forgiveness gives a new direction. In a confessional Lutheran reading, this command is not a ladder by which sinners earn justification. The Law exposes sin and directs the baptized life; the Gospel forgives, creates faith, joins the believer to Christ, and brings forth the fruits named here. The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

#### Early Christian witnesses

**Didache 2.2 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> You shall not commit murder; you shall not commit adultery; you shall not commit sexual immorality; you shall not steal; you shall not practice magic or witchcraft; you shall not murder a child by abortion nor kill that which is born.

*Study significance:* This early catechesis applies the way of life to the protection of bodies, marriage, property, and children.

**Augustine, Confessions 10.29 (Pusey translation, lightly modernized)**

> Give what You command, and command what You will.

*Study significance:* Augustine confesses that divine commands expose need and that God must supply the grace by which they are obeyed.

#### Lutheran confessional witnesses

**Small Catechism, Baptism IV**

> The Old Adam in us should, by daily contrition and repentance, be drowned and die with all sins and evil lusts, and a new person daily come forth and arise, who shall live before God in righteousness and purity forever.

*Study significance:* The baptized life is daily repentance and new life, not a one-time memory.

**Small Catechism, Sixth Commandment**

> We should fear and love God that we may lead a chaste and decent life in words and deeds, and each love and honor his spouse.

*Study significance:* Jesus' teaching about desire and marriage is received as positive chastity and faithful honor.

**Further confessional anchors:** Small Catechism: Ten Commandments; Augsburg Confession VI and XVI (new obedience and vocation); Formula of Concord, Solid Declaration VI (the Law as guide for the regenerate); Large Catechism: exposition of the relevant commandment.

#### Practicing the command

Remove access, change routines, seek pastoral and clinical help, make restitution, and establish accountability.

#### Misuse caution

Never interpret the imagery as permission for self-harm; mark John 7:53-8:11 for textual history.

#### Questions for examination and meditation

1. What exactly does Jesus command, forbid, promise, or warn in these passages, and who first receives the words?
2. Which words expose sin or false trust, and which words direct you to Christ's gift, forgiveness, or sustaining promise?
3. What concrete act of repentance, faith, mercy, or vocation would embody **remove occasions of sin and leave the old life** this week without turning obedience into a basis of justification?

#### Prayer

Lord Jesus Christ, You call Your people to remove occasions of sin and leave the old life. Forgive my failure, keep me in Your Word and gifts, and bear this fruit in me for the good of my neighbor and the glory of the Father. Amen.

<a id="command-037"></a>

### 37. Honor father and mother

**Corpus:** Gospels  
**Scope:** Universal within ordered vocation  
**Earlier-list crosswalk:** #52  
**Canonical passages:** Matthew 15:4-6; 19:19; Mark 7:9-13; 10:19; Luke 18:20

#### Command-family wording

> Honor parents and do not use religious excuses to evade concrete care.

#### Scripture text (World English Bible)

##### Matthew 15:4-6

> **4** For God commanded, ‘Honor your father and your mother,’ and, ‘He who speaks evil of father or mother, let him be put to death.’
>
> **5** But you say, ‘Whoever may tell his father or his mother, “Whatever help you might otherwise have gotten from me is a gift devoted to God,”
>
> **6** he shall not honor his father or mother.’ You have made the commandment of God void because of your tradition.

##### Matthew 19:19

> **19** ‘Honor your father and your mother.’ And, ‘You shall love your neighbor as yourself.’”

##### Mark 7:9-13

> **9** He said to them, “Full well do you reject the commandment of God, that you may keep your tradition.
>
> **10** For Moses said, ‘Honor your father and your mother;’ and, ‘He who speaks evil of father or mother, let him be put to death.’
>
> **11** But you say, ‘If a man tells his father or his mother, “Whatever profit you might have received from me is Corban,”’” that is to say, given to God,
>
> **12** “then you no longer allow him to do anything for his father or his mother,
>
> **13** making void the word of God by your tradition which you have handed down. You do many things like this.”

##### Mark 10:19

> **19** You know the commandments: ‘Do not murder,’ ‘Do not commit adultery,’ ‘Do not steal,’ ‘Do not give false testimony,’ ‘Do not defraud,’ ‘Honor your father and mother.’”

##### Luke 18:20

> **20** You know the commandments: ‘Don’t commit adultery,’ ‘Don’t murder,’ ‘Don’t steal,’ ‘Don’t give false testimony,’ ‘Honor your father and your mother.’”

#### Reading the passages together

- Jesus reaffirms the Fourth Commandment while keeping every human authority under God.

#### Law, Gospel, and confessional synthesis

Jesus reaffirms the Fourth Commandment while keeping every human authority under God. In a confessional Lutheran reading, this command is not a ladder by which sinners earn justification. The Law exposes sin and directs the baptized life; the Gospel forgives, creates faith, joins the believer to Christ, and brings forth the fruits named here. The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

#### Early Christian witnesses

**Didache 2.2 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> You shall not commit murder; you shall not commit adultery; you shall not commit sexual immorality; you shall not steal; you shall not practice magic or witchcraft; you shall not murder a child by abortion nor kill that which is born.

*Study significance:* This early catechesis applies the way of life to the protection of bodies, marriage, property, and children.

**1 Clement 13.1-2 (Lightfoot/Roberts-Donaldson tradition, lightly modernized)**

> Let us be humble-minded, brethren, laying aside all haughtiness and pride and foolishness and angry feelings, especially remembering the words of the Lord Jesus: Be merciful, that you may obtain mercy; forgive, that it may be forgiven unto you.

*Study significance:* Clement explicitly uses Jesus' words to form a peaceful and humble congregation.

#### Lutheran confessional witnesses

**Small Catechism, Fourth Commandment**

> We should fear and love God that we may not despise our parents and masters nor provoke them to anger, but give them honor, serve and obey them, and hold them in love and esteem.

*Study significance:* Honor is concrete service under God, not permission for authority to command sin.

**Augsburg Confession VI**

> This faith is bound to bring forth good fruits, and it is necessary to do good works commanded by God because of God's will; yet we should not rely on those works to merit justification before God.

*Study significance:* Good works are necessary fruits of faith, not merits that purchase God's favor.

**Further confessional anchors:** Small Catechism: Ten Commandments; Augsburg Confession VI and XVI (new obedience and vocation); Formula of Concord, Solid Declaration VI (the Law as guide for the regenerate); Large Catechism: exposition of the relevant commandment.

#### Practicing the command

Speak respectfully, provide appropriate care, honor lawful authority, and set boundaries against sin or abuse.

#### Misuse caution

Honor does not mean obeying commands to sin or concealing wrongdoing.

#### Questions for examination and meditation

1. What exactly does Jesus command, forbid, promise, or warn in these passages, and who first receives the words?
2. Which words expose sin or false trust, and which words direct you to Christ's gift, forgiveness, or sustaining promise?
3. What concrete act of repentance, faith, mercy, or vocation would embody **honor father and mother** this week without turning obedience into a basis of justification?

#### Prayer

Lord Jesus Christ, You call Your people to honor father and mother. Forgive my failure, keep me in Your Word and gifts, and bear this fruit in me for the good of my neighbor and the glory of the Father. Amen.

<a id="command-038"></a>

### 38. Do not separate what God has joined in marriage

**Corpus:** Gospels  
**Scope:** Universal teaching with pastoral application  
**Earlier-list crosswalk:** #53  
**Canonical passages:** Matthew 19:3-9; Mark 10:2-12

#### Command-family wording

> Honor the one-flesh union God creates; do not tear it apart for self-serving reasons.

#### Scripture text (World English Bible)

##### Matthew 19:3-9

> **3** Pharisees came to him, testing him and saying, “Is it lawful for a man to divorce his wife for any reason?”
>
> **4** He answered, “Haven’t you read that he who made them from the beginning made them male and female,
>
> **5** and said, ‘For this cause a man shall leave his father and mother, and shall be joined to his wife; and the two shall become one flesh’?
>
> **6** So that they are no more two, but one flesh. What therefore God has joined together, don’t let man tear apart.”
>
> **7** They asked him, “Why then did Moses command us to give her a certificate of divorce and divorce her?”
>
> **8** He said to them, “Moses, because of the hardness of your hearts, allowed you to divorce your wives, but from the beginning it has not been so.
>
> **9** I tell you that whoever divorces his wife, except for sexual immorality, and marries another, commits adultery; and he who marries her when she is divorced commits adultery.”

##### Mark 10:2-12

> **2** Pharisees came to him testing him, and asked him, “Is it lawful for a man to divorce his wife?”
>
> **3** He answered, “What did Moses command you?”
>
> **4** They said, “Moses allowed a certificate of divorce to be written, and to divorce her.”
>
> **5** But Jesus said to them, “For your hardness of heart, he wrote you this commandment.
>
> **6** But from the beginning of the creation, God made them male and female.
>
> **7** For this cause a man will leave his father and mother, and will join to his wife,
>
> **8** and the two will become one flesh, so that they are no longer two, but one flesh.
>
> **9** What therefore God has joined together, let no man separate.”
>
> **10** In the house, his disciples asked him again about the same matter.
>
> **11** He said to them, “Whoever divorces his wife and marries another commits adultery against her.
>
> **12** If a woman herself divorces her husband and marries another, she commits adultery.”

#### Reading the passages together

- Jesus returns marriage to creation and rejects easy divorce while pastoral judgment remains necessary in a fallen world.

#### Law, Gospel, and confessional synthesis

Jesus returns marriage to creation and rejects easy divorce while pastoral judgment remains necessary in a fallen world. In a confessional Lutheran reading, this command is not a ladder by which sinners earn justification. The Law exposes sin and directs the baptized life; the Gospel forgives, creates faith, joins the believer to Christ, and brings forth the fruits named here. The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

#### Early Christian witnesses

**Justin Martyr, First Apology 15 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Concerning chastity, He uttered such sentiments as these: Whoever looks upon a woman to lust after her has committed adultery with her already in his heart before God.

*Study significance:* Justin quotes Jesus as the standard for inward as well as outward chastity.

**Didache 2.2 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> You shall not commit murder; you shall not commit adultery; you shall not commit sexual immorality; you shall not steal; you shall not practice magic or witchcraft; you shall not murder a child by abortion nor kill that which is born.

*Study significance:* This early catechesis applies the way of life to the protection of bodies, marriage, property, and children.

#### Lutheran confessional witnesses

**Small Catechism, Sixth Commandment**

> We should fear and love God that we may lead a chaste and decent life in words and deeds, and each love and honor his spouse.

*Study significance:* Jesus' teaching about desire and marriage is received as positive chastity and faithful honor.

**Augsburg Confession VI**

> This faith is bound to bring forth good fruits, and it is necessary to do good works commanded by God because of God's will; yet we should not rely on those works to merit justification before God.

*Study significance:* Good works are necessary fruits of faith, not merits that purchase God's favor.

**Further confessional anchors:** Small Catechism: Ten Commandments; Augsburg Confession VI and XVI (new obedience and vocation); Formula of Concord, Solid Declaration VI (the Law as guide for the regenerate); Large Catechism: exposition of the relevant commandment.

#### Practicing the command

Keep vows, seek help early, practice fidelity, protect spouses and children, and involve pastors and qualified professionals.

#### Misuse caution

The text must not be used to trap someone in violence.

#### Questions for examination and meditation

1. What exactly does Jesus command, forbid, promise, or warn in these passages, and who first receives the words?
2. Which words expose sin or false trust, and which words direct you to Christ's gift, forgiveness, or sustaining promise?
3. What concrete act of repentance, faith, mercy, or vocation would embody **do not separate what god has joined in marriage** this week without turning obedience into a basis of justification?

#### Prayer

Lord Jesus Christ, You call Your people to do not separate what god has joined in marriage. Forgive my failure, keep me in Your Word and gifts, and bear this fruit in me for the good of my neighbor and the glory of the Father. Amen.
