### 39. Guard against every form of covetousness

**Corpus:** Gospels  
**Scope:** Universal  
**Earlier-list crosswalk:** #54  
**Canonical passages:** Luke 12:13-21

#### Command-family wording

> Watch the heart against greed; life does not consist in abundance of possessions.

#### Scripture text (World English Bible)

##### Luke 12:13-21

> **13** One of the multitude said to him, “Teacher, tell my brother to divide the inheritance with me.”
>
> **14** But he said to him, “Man, who made me a judge or an arbitrator over you?”
>
> **15** He said to them, “Beware! Keep yourselves from covetousness, for a man’s life doesn’t consist of the abundance of the things which he possesses.”
>
> **16** He spoke a parable to them, saying, “The ground of a certain rich man produced abundantly.
>
> **17** He reasoned within himself, saying, ‘What will I do, because I don’t have room to store my crops?’
>
> **18** He said, ‘This is what I will do. I will pull down my barns, build bigger ones, and there I will store all my grain and my goods.
>
> **19** I will tell my soul, “Soul, you have many goods laid up for many years. Take your ease, eat, drink, and be merry.”’
>
> **20** “But God said to him, ‘You foolish one, tonight your soul is required of you. The things which you have prepared-whose will they be?’
>
> **21** So is he who lays up treasure for himself, and is not rich toward God.”

#### Reading the passages together

- Covetousness is disordered trust before it becomes a financial act; wealth without richness toward God is folly.

#### Law, Gospel, and confessional synthesis

Covetousness is disordered trust before it becomes a financial act; wealth without richness toward God is folly. In a confessional Lutheran reading, this command is not a ladder by which sinners earn justification. The Law exposes sin and directs the baptized life; the Gospel forgives, creates faith, joins the believer to Christ, and brings forth the fruits named here. The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

#### Early Christian witnesses

**Didache 1.5 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Give to everyone who asks you, and ask it not back; for the Father wills that to all should be given of our own blessings.

*Study significance:* The command is received as real generosity flowing from the Father's gifts.

**Didache 2.2 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> You shall not commit murder; you shall not commit adultery; you shall not commit sexual immorality; you shall not steal; you shall not practice magic or witchcraft; you shall not murder a child by abortion nor kill that which is born.

*Study significance:* This early catechesis applies the way of life to the protection of bodies, marriage, property, and children.

#### Lutheran confessional witnesses

**Small Catechism, Seventh Commandment**

> We should fear and love God that we may not take our neighbor's money or property, nor get them by false ware or dealing, but help him to improve and protect his property and business.

*Study significance:* Generosity and economic integrity seek the neighbor's material good.

**Small Catechism, First Commandment**

> We should fear, love, and trust in God above all things.

*Study significance:* Every command family is finally ordered under trust in the true God.

**Further confessional anchors:** Small Catechism: Ten Commandments; Augsburg Confession VI and XVI (new obedience and vocation); Formula of Concord, Solid Declaration VI (the Law as guide for the regenerate); Large Catechism: exposition of the relevant commandment.

#### Practicing the command

Practice gratitude, disclose finances appropriately, make a giving plan, resist comparison, and write a just will.

#### Misuse caution

The command does not condemn saving, ownership, enterprise, or provision for dependents as such.

#### Questions for examination and meditation

1. What exactly does Jesus command, forbid, promise, or warn in these passages, and who first receives the words?
2. Which words expose sin or false trust, and which words direct you to Christ's gift, forgiveness, or sustaining promise?
3. What concrete act of repentance, faith, mercy, or vocation would embody **guard against every form of covetousness** this week without turning obedience into a basis of justification?

#### Prayer

Lord Jesus Christ, You call Your people to guard against every form of covetousness. Forgive my failure, keep me in Your Word and gifts, and bear this fruit in me for the good of my neighbor and the glory of the Father. Amen.

### 40. Beware the leaven of hypocrisy and unbelief

**Corpus:** Gospels  
**Scope:** Universal discernment  
**Earlier-list crosswalk:** #55  
**Canonical passages:** Matthew 16:5-12; Mark 8:14-21; Luke 12:1

#### Command-family wording

> Watch for teaching and conduct that quietly spreads hypocrisy, self-righteousness, or unbelief.

#### Scripture text (World English Bible)

##### Matthew 16:5-12

> **5** The disciples came to the other side and had forgotten to take bread.
>
> **6** Jesus said to them, “Take heed and beware of the yeast of the Pharisees and Sadducees.”
>
> **7** They reasoned among themselves, saying, “We brought no bread.”
>
> **8** Jesus, perceiving it, said, “Why do you reason among yourselves, you of little faith, because you have brought no bread?
>
> **9** Don’t you yet perceive or remember the five loaves for the five thousand, and how many baskets you took up,
>
> **10** or the seven loaves for the four thousand, and how many baskets you took up?
>
> **11** How is it that you don’t perceive that I didn’t speak to you concerning bread? But beware of the yeast of the Pharisees and Sadducees.”
>
> **12** Then they understood that he didn’t tell them to beware of the yeast of bread, but of the teaching of the Pharisees and Sadducees.

##### Mark 8:14-21

> **14** They forgot to take bread; and they didn’t have more than one loaf in the boat with them.
>
> **15** He warned them, saying, “Take heed: beware of the yeast of the Pharisees and the yeast of Herod.”
>
> **16** They reasoned with one another, saying, “It’s because we have no bread.”
>
> **17** Jesus, perceiving it, said to them, “Why do you reason that it’s because you have no bread? Don’t you perceive yet or understand? Is your heart still hardened?
>
> **18** Having eyes, don’t you see? Having ears, don’t you hear? Don’t you remember?
>
> **19** When I broke the five loaves among the five thousand, how many baskets full of broken pieces did you take up?” They told him, “Twelve.”
>
> **20** “When the seven loaves fed the four thousand, how many baskets full of broken pieces did you take up?” They told him, “Seven.”
>
> **21** He asked them, “Don’t you understand yet?”

##### Luke 12:1

> **1** Meanwhile, when a multitude of many thousands had gathered together, so much so that they trampled on each other, he began to tell his disciples first of all, “Beware of the yeast of the Pharisees, which is hypocrisy.

#### Reading the passages together

- Leaven pictures pervasive influence; religious systems can confess one thing while trusting status, signs, or tradition over the Word.

#### Law, Gospel, and confessional synthesis

Leaven pictures pervasive influence; religious systems can confess one thing while trusting status, signs, or tradition over the Word. In a confessional Lutheran reading, this command is not a ladder by which sinners earn justification. The Law exposes sin and directs the baptized life; the Gospel forgives, creates faith, joins the believer to Christ, and brings forth the fruits named here. The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

#### Early Christian witnesses

**Didache 2.4-5; 4.4 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> You shall not be double-minded nor double-tongued, for to be double-tongued is a snare of death. Your speech shall not be false or empty, but fulfilled by deed.

*Study significance:* The witness treats integrity as speech verified by action.

**Didache 11.1-2 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Whoever comes and teaches you all these things that have been said before, receive him. But if the teacher himself turns and teaches another doctrine to the destruction of this, do not hear him.

*Study significance:* Hospitality and openness are joined to doctrinal discernment.

#### Lutheran confessional witnesses

**Small Catechism, Eighth Commandment**

> We should fear and love God that we may not deceitfully belie, betray, slander, or defame our neighbor, but defend him, speak well of him, and put the best construction on everything.

*Study significance:* Truthful correction and charitable interpretation belong together.

**Augsburg Confession VI**

> This faith is bound to bring forth good fruits, and it is necessary to do good works commanded by God because of God's will; yet we should not rely on those works to merit justification before God.

*Study significance:* Good works are necessary fruits of faith, not merits that purchase God's favor.

**Further confessional anchors:** Small Catechism: Ten Commandments; Augsburg Confession VI and XVI (new obedience and vocation); Formula of Concord, Solid Declaration VI (the Law as guide for the regenerate); Large Catechism: exposition of the relevant commandment.

#### Practicing the command

Compare teaching with Scripture, examine institutional incentives, confess performative religion, and receive correction.

#### Misuse caution

Suspicion is not discernment; the warning does not authorize rumor or contempt for every tradition.

#### Questions for examination and meditation

1. What exactly does Jesus command, forbid, promise, or warn in these passages, and who first receives the words?
2. Which words expose sin or false trust, and which words direct you to Christ's gift, forgiveness, or sustaining promise?
3. What concrete act of repentance, faith, mercy, or vocation would embody **beware the leaven of hypocrisy and unbelief** this week without turning obedience into a basis of justification?

#### Prayer

Lord Jesus Christ, You call Your people to beware the leaven of hypocrisy and unbelief. Forgive my failure, keep me in Your Word and gifts, and bear this fruit in me for the good of my neighbor and the glory of the Father. Amen.

### 41. Judge with humility and righteous discernment

**Corpus:** Gospels  
**Scope:** Universal  
**Earlier-list crosswalk:** #56  
**Canonical passages:** Matthew 7:1-5; Luke 6:37-42; John 7:24

#### Command-family wording

> Do not condemn hypocritically or by appearances; repent first, then judge according to truth and mercy.

#### Scripture text (World English Bible)

##### Matthew 7:1-5

> **1** “Don’t judge, so that you won’t be judged.
>
> **2** For with whatever judgment you judge, you will be judged; and with whatever measure you measure, it will be measured to you.
>
> **3** Why do you see the speck that is in your brother’s eye, but don’t consider the beam that is in your own eye?
>
> **4** Or how will you tell your brother, ‘Let me remove the speck from your eye,’ and behold, the beam is in your own eye?
>
> **5** You hypocrite! First remove the beam out of your own eye, and then you can see clearly to remove the speck out of your brother’s eye.

##### Luke 6:37-42

> **37** Don’t judge, and you won’t be judged. Don’t condemn, and you won’t be condemned. Set free, and you will be set free.
>
> **38** “Give, and it will be given to you: good measure, pressed down, shaken together, and running over, will be given to you. For with the same measure you measure it will be measured back to you.”
>
> **39** He spoke a parable to them. “Can the blind guide the blind? Won’t they both fall into a pit?
>
> **40** A disciple is not above his teacher, but everyone when he is fully trained will be like his teacher.
>
> **41** Why do you see the speck of chaff that is in your brother’s eye, but don’t consider the beam that is in your own eye?
>
> **42** Or how can you tell your brother, ‘Brother, let me remove the speck of chaff that is in your eye,’ when you yourself don’t see the beam that is in your own eye? You hypocrite! First remove the beam from your own eye, and then you can see clearly to remove the speck of chaff that is in your brother’s eye.

##### John 7:24

> **24** Don’t judge according to appearance, but judge righteous judgment.”

#### Reading the passages together

- Jesus forbids self-exalting condemnation, not moral discernment, church discipline, or civil justice.

#### Law, Gospel, and confessional synthesis

Jesus forbids self-exalting condemnation, not moral discernment, church discipline, or civil justice. In a confessional Lutheran reading, this command is not a ladder by which sinners earn justification. The Law exposes sin and directs the baptized life; the Gospel forgives, creates faith, joins the believer to Christ, and brings forth the fruits named here. The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

#### Early Christian witnesses

**Polycarp, Epistle to the Philippians 2 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Remembering what the Lord said in His teaching: Judge not, that you be not judged; forgive, and it shall be forgiven unto you; be merciful, that you may obtain mercy; with what measure you measure, it shall be measured to you again.

*Study significance:* Polycarp preserves several sayings of Jesus as a unified pattern for congregational life.

**1 Clement 13.1-2 (Lightfoot/Roberts-Donaldson tradition, lightly modernized)**

> Let us be humble-minded, brethren, laying aside all haughtiness and pride and foolishness and angry feelings, especially remembering the words of the Lord Jesus: Be merciful, that you may obtain mercy; forgive, that it may be forgiven unto you.

*Study significance:* Clement explicitly uses Jesus' words to form a peaceful and humble congregation.

**Small Catechism, Eighth Commandment**

> We should fear and love God that we may not deceitfully belie, betray, slander, or defame our neighbor, but defend him, speak well of him, and put the best construction on everything.

**Augsburg Confession VI**

> This faith is bound to bring forth good fruits, and it is necessary to do good works commanded by God because of God's will; yet we should not rely on those works to merit justification before God.

*Study significance:* Good works are necessary fruits of faith, not merits that purchase God's favor.

**Further confessional anchors:** Small Catechism: Ten Commandments; Augsburg Confession VI and XVI (new obedience and vocation); Formula of Concord, Solid Declaration VI (the Law as guide for the regenerate); Large Catechism: exposition of the relevant commandment.

#### Practicing the command

Check facts, confess comparable sins, use fair processes, speak directly and charitably, and revise mistaken judgments.

#### Misuse caution

Judge not must not silence safeguarding or doctrinal discernment.

#### Questions for examination and meditation

1. What exactly does Jesus command, forbid, promise, or warn in these passages, and who first receives the words?
2. Which words expose sin or false trust, and which words direct you to Christ's gift, forgiveness, or sustaining promise?
3. What concrete act of repentance, faith, mercy, or vocation would embody **judge with humility and righteous discernment** this week without turning obedience into a basis of justification?

Lord Jesus Christ, You call Your people to judge with humility and righteous discernment. Forgive my failure, keep me in Your Word and gifts, and bear this fruit in me for the good of my neighbor and the glory of the Father. Amen.

**Corpus:** Gospels

> Do not place holy things where they will be knowingly profaned; steward them with discernment.

#### Scripture text (World English Bible)

##### Matthew 7:6

> **6** “Don’t give that which is holy to the dogs, neither throw your pearls before the pigs, lest perhaps they trample them under their feet, and turn and tear you to pieces.

- The saying balances the warning against arrogant judgment with responsibility for sacred gifts and teaching.

#### Law, Gospel, and confessional synthesis

The saying balances the warning against arrogant judgment with responsibility for sacred gifts and teaching. In a confessional Lutheran reading, this command is not a ladder by which sinners earn justification. The Law exposes sin and directs the baptized life; the Gospel forgives, creates faith, joins the believer to Christ, and brings forth the fruits named here. The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

**Didache 11.1-2 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Whoever comes and teaches you all these things that have been said before, receive him. But if the teacher himself turns and teaches another doctrine to the destruction of this, do not hear him.

<a id="command-043"></a>

### 43. Answer evil with generous non-retaliation

**Corpus:** Gospels  
**Scope:** Universal personal ethic distinguished from public office  
**Earlier-list crosswalk:** #58, #59, #60, #61  
**Canonical passages:** Matthew 5:38-42; Luke 6:29-30

#### Command-family wording

> Do not repay personal evil in kind; absorb insult, go beyond coercive demands, and give with open-handed wisdom.

#### Scripture text (World English Bible)

##### Matthew 5:38-42

> **38** “You have heard that it was said, ‘An eye for an eye, and a tooth for a tooth.’
>
> **39** But I tell you, don’t resist him who is evil; but whoever strikes you on your right cheek, turn to him the other also.
>
> **40** If anyone sues you to take away your coat, let him have your cloak also.
>
> **41** Whoever compels you to go one mile, go with him two.
>
> **42** Give to him who asks you, and don’t turn away him who desires to borrow from you.

##### Luke 6:29-30

> **29** To him who strikes you on the cheek, offer also the other; and from him who takes away your cloak, don’t withhold your coat also.
>
> **30** Give to everyone who asks you, and don’t ask him who takes away your goods to give them back again.

#### Reading the passages together

- Jesus breaks cycles of personal vengeance while public offices still have duties to protect neighbors and restrain evil.

#### Law, Gospel, and confessional synthesis

Jesus breaks cycles of personal vengeance while public offices still have duties to protect neighbors and restrain evil. In a confessional Lutheran reading, this command is not a ladder by which sinners earn justification. The Law exposes sin and directs the baptized life; the Gospel forgives, creates faith, joins the believer to Christ, and brings forth the fruits named here. The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

#### Early Christian witnesses

**Didache 1.4 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Abstain from fleshly and worldly lusts. If anyone gives you a blow on your right cheek, turn to him the other also; and you shall be perfect.

*Study significance:* This directly echoes the Sermon on the Mount and links non-retaliation with holiness.

**Justin Martyr, First Apology 16 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Concerning our being patient of injuries: Whoever shall strike you on the right cheek, turn to him the other also. And: Let your good works shine before men, that they, seeing them, may glorify your Father who is in heaven.

*Study significance:* The early apologist places non-retaliation and visible good works together as Christian witness.

#### Lutheran confessional witnesses

**Small Catechism, Fifth Commandment**

> We should fear and love God that we may not hurt nor harm our neighbor in his body, but help and befriend him in every bodily need.

*Study significance:* Neighbor-love includes active bodily help, not merely refraining from violence.

**Augsburg Confession VI**

> This faith is bound to bring forth good fruits, and it is necessary to do good works commanded by God because of God's will; yet we should not rely on those works to merit justification before God.

*Study significance:* Good works are necessary fruits of faith, not merits that purchase God's favor.

**Further confessional anchors:** Small Catechism: Ten Commandments; Augsburg Confession VI and XVI (new obedience and vocation); Formula of Concord, Solid Declaration VI (the Law as guide for the regenerate); Large Catechism: exposition of the relevant commandment.

#### Practicing the command

Refuse revenge, de-escalate, give responsibly, accept small losses for peace, and use lawful protection for others.

#### Misuse caution

Non-retaliation does not forbid self-protection, reporting crime, or just public law.

#### Questions for examination and meditation

1. What exactly does Jesus command, forbid, promise, or warn in these passages, and who first receives the words?
2. Which words expose sin or false trust, and which words direct you to Christ's gift, forgiveness, or sustaining promise?
3. What concrete act of repentance, faith, mercy, or vocation would embody **answer evil with generous non-retaliation** this week without turning obedience into a basis of justification?

#### Prayer

Lord Jesus Christ, You call Your people to answer evil with generous non-retaliation. Forgive my failure, keep me in Your Word and gifts, and bear this fruit in me for the good of my neighbor and the glory of the Father. Amen.

<a id="command-044"></a>

### 44. Remember Lot's wife

**Corpus:** Gospels  
**Scope:** Universal eschatological warning  
**Earlier-list crosswalk:** #62  
**Canonical passages:** Luke 17:28-33

#### Command-family wording

> Do not look back longingly to a world under judgment when the Son of Man calls forward.

#### Scripture text (World English Bible)

##### Luke 17:28-33

> **28** Likewise, even as it was in the days of Lot: they ate, they drank, they bought, they sold, they planted, they built;
>
> **29** but in the day that Lot went out from Sodom, it rained fire and sulfur from the sky and destroyed them all.
>
> **30** It will be the same way in the day that the Son of Man is revealed.
>
> **31** In that day, he who will be on the housetop and his goods in the house, let him not go down to take them away. Let him who is in the field likewise not turn back.
>
> **32** Remember Lot’s wife!
>
> **33** Whoever seeks to save his life loses it, but whoever loses his life preserves it.

#### Reading the passages together

- The warning exposes a divided heart attached to the life being abandoned.

#### Law, Gospel, and confessional synthesis

The warning exposes a divided heart attached to the life being abandoned. In a confessional Lutheran reading, this command is not a ladder by which sinners earn justification. The Law exposes sin and directs the baptized life; the Gospel forgives, creates faith, joins the believer to Christ, and brings forth the fruits named here. The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

#### Early Christian witnesses

**Didache 16.1 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Watch for your life's sake. Let not your lamps be quenched, nor your loins unloosed; but be ready, for you do not know the hour in which our Lord comes.

*Study significance:* The passage directly preserves the church's early reception of Jesus' readiness language.

**Didache 1.1-2 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> There are two ways, one of life and one of death; but there is a great difference between these two ways. The way of life, then, is this: First, you shall love God who made you; second, your neighbor as yourself; and all things whatsoever you would not have done to you, do not do to another.

*Study significance:* An early Christian catechetical summary joins love of God, love of neighbor, and concrete conduct.

#### Lutheran confessional witnesses

**Augsburg Confession XII**

> Properly speaking, repentance consists of these two parts: one is contrition, that is, terrors smiting the conscience through the knowledge of sin; the other is faith, born of the Gospel or of absolution, which believes that for Christ's sake sins are forgiven, comforts the conscience, and delivers it from terrors.

*Study significance:* Repentance includes both truthful sorrow over sin and faith in Christ's forgiveness.

**Small Catechism, First Commandment**

> We should fear, love, and trust in God above all things.

*Study significance:* Every command family is finally ordered under trust in the true God.

**Further confessional anchors:** Small Catechism: Ten Commandments; Augsburg Confession VI and XVI (new obedience and vocation); Formula of Concord, Solid Declaration VI (the Law as guide for the regenerate); Large Catechism: exposition of the relevant commandment.

#### Practicing the command

Do not romanticize former sin, leave promptly when obedience requires it, hold property lightly, and rehearse Christ's coming.

#### Misuse caution

The text is not permission for impulsive flight from ordinary obligations.

#### Questions for examination and meditation

1. What exactly does Jesus command, forbid, promise, or warn in these passages, and who first receives the words?
2. Which words expose sin or false trust, and which words direct you to Christ's gift, forgiveness, or sustaining promise?
3. What concrete act of repentance, faith, mercy, or vocation would embody **remember lot's wife** this week without turning obedience into a basis of justification?

#### Prayer

Lord Jesus Christ, You call Your people to remember lot's wife. Forgive my failure, keep me in Your Word and gifts, and bear this fruit in me for the good of my neighbor and the glory of the Father. Amen.

<a id="command-045"></a>

### 45. Be perfect as your heavenly Father is perfect

**Corpus:** Gospels  
**Scope:** Universal goal of the Law  
**Earlier-list crosswalk:** #63  
**Canonical passages:** Matthew 5:43-48

#### Command-family wording

> Let the Father's complete, enemy-embracing love shape your life.

#### Scripture text (World English Bible)

##### Matthew 5:43-48

> **43** “You have heard that it was said, ‘You shall love your neighbor and hate your enemy.’
>
> **44** But I tell you, love your enemies, bless those who curse you, do good to those who hate you, and pray for those who mistreat you and persecute you,
>
> **45** that you may be children of your Father who is in heaven. For he makes his sun to rise on the evil and the good, and sends rain on the just and the unjust.
>
> **46** For if you love those who love you, what reward do you have? Don’t even the tax collectors do the same?
>
> **47** If you only greet your friends, what more do you do than others? Don’t even the tax collectors do the same?
>
> **48** Therefore you shall be perfect, just as your Father in heaven is perfect.

#### Reading the passages together

- The command strips away claims of adequate righteousness, drives sinners to Christ, and names the direction of sanctification.

#### Law, Gospel, and confessional synthesis

The command strips away claims of adequate righteousness, drives sinners to Christ, and names the direction of sanctification. In a confessional Lutheran reading, this command is not a ladder by which sinners earn justification. The Law exposes sin and directs the baptized life; the Gospel forgives, creates faith, joins the believer to Christ, and brings forth the fruits named here. The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

#### Early Christian witnesses

**Didache 1.4 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Abstain from fleshly and worldly lusts. If anyone gives you a blow on your right cheek, turn to him the other also; and you shall be perfect.

*Study significance:* This directly echoes the Sermon on the Mount and links non-retaliation with holiness.

**1 Clement 49.1 (Lightfoot translation, lightly modernized)**

> Let the one who has love in Christ keep the commandments of Christ.

*Study significance:* The sentence binds Christian love to Christ's actual commands rather than sentiment alone.

#### Lutheran confessional witnesses

**Augsburg Confession IV**

> People cannot be justified before God by their own strength, merits, or works, but are freely justified for Christ's sake, through faith, when they believe that they are received into favor and that their sins are forgiven for Christ's sake.

*Study significance:* The command must never be turned into a competing basis of justification.

**Augsburg Confession VI**

> This faith is bound to bring forth good fruits, and it is necessary to do good works commanded by God because of God's will; yet we should not rely on those works to merit justification before God.

*Study significance:* Good works are necessary fruits of faith, not merits that purchase God's favor.

**Further confessional anchors:** Small Catechism: Ten Commandments; Augsburg Confession VI and XVI (new obedience and vocation); Formula of Concord, Solid Declaration VI (the Law as guide for the regenerate); Large Catechism: exposition of the relevant commandment.

#### Practicing the command

Reject selective love, pray for enemies, confess partiality, and pursue mature integrity.

#### Misuse caution

Do not teach sinless perfectionism or make maturity the basis for assurance.

#### Questions for examination and meditation

1. What exactly does Jesus command, forbid, promise, or warn in these passages, and who first receives the words?
2. Which words expose sin or false trust, and which words direct you to Christ's gift, forgiveness, or sustaining promise?
3. What concrete act of repentance, faith, mercy, or vocation would embody **be perfect as your heavenly father is perfect** this week without turning obedience into a basis of justification?

#### Prayer

Lord Jesus Christ, You call Your people to be perfect as your heavenly father is perfect. Forgive my failure, keep me in Your Word and gifts, and bear this fruit in me for the good of my neighbor and the glory of the Father. Amen.

## Prayer, Worship, and the Means of Grace

The Father gives His gifts through Christ's appointed Word, prayer, Baptism, absolution, and Supper.

<a id="command-046"></a>

### 46. Practice almsgiving, prayer, and fasting before the Father

**Corpus:** Gospels  
**Scope:** Universal, with fasting as a free discipline  
**Earlier-list crosswalk:** #64, #65, #68  
**Canonical passages:** Matthew 6:1-6,16-18

#### Command-family wording

> Practice works of piety for the Father's sake rather than human applause.

#### Scripture text (World English Bible)

##### Matthew 6:1-6,16-18

> **1** “Be careful that you don’t do your charitable giving before men, to be seen by them, or else you have no reward from your Father who is in heaven.
>
> **2** Therefore, when you do merciful deeds, don’t sound a trumpet before yourself, as the hypocrites do in the synagogues and in the streets, that they may get glory from men. Most certainly I tell you, they have received their reward.
>
> **3** But when you do merciful deeds, don’t let your left hand know what your right hand does,
>
> **4** so that your merciful deeds may be in secret, then your Father who sees in secret will reward you openly.
>
> **5** “When you pray, you shall not be as the hypocrites, for they love to stand and pray in the synagogues and in the corners of the streets, that they may be seen by men. Most certainly, I tell you, they have received their reward.
>
> **6** But you, when you pray, enter into your inner room, and having shut your door, pray to your Father who is in secret; and your Father who sees in secret will reward you openly.
>
> **16** “Moreover when you fast, don’t be like the hypocrites, with sad faces. For they disfigure their faces that they may be seen by men to be fasting. Most certainly I tell you, they have received their reward.
>
> **17** But you, when you fast, anoint your head and wash your face,
>
> **18** so that you are not seen by men to be fasting, but by your Father who is in secret; and your Father, who sees in secret, will reward you.

#### Reading the passages together

- Jesus does not abolish almsgiving, prayer, or fasting; He purifies their audience and promise. The Father sees in secret, and these practices flow from faith rather than purchasing grace.

#### Law, Gospel, and confessional synthesis

Jesus does not abolish almsgiving, prayer, or fasting; He purifies their audience and promise. The Father sees in secret, and these practices flow from faith rather than purchasing grace. In a confessional Lutheran reading, this command is not a ladder by which sinners earn justification. The Law exposes sin and directs the baptized life; the Gospel forgives, creates faith, joins the believer to Christ, and brings forth the fruits named here. The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

#### Early Christian witnesses

**Didache 8.2-3 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Neither pray as the hypocrites, but as the Lord commanded in his Gospel, thus pray: Our Father, who art in heaven. Three times in the day pray so.

*Study significance:* Jesus' model prayer was received as a repeated, formative practice rather than empty repetition.

**Didache 1.5 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Give to everyone who asks you, and ask it not back; for the Father wills that to all should be given of our own blessings.

*Study significance:* The command is received as real generosity flowing from the Father's gifts.

#### Lutheran confessional witnesses

**Small Catechism, Lord's Prayer, Introduction**

> God would thereby tenderly invite us to believe that He is our true Father and that we are His true children, so that we may ask Him confidently with all assurance, as dear children ask their dear father.

*Study significance:* Christian prayer rests on the Father's promise rather than quantity of words or emotional intensity.

**Augsburg Confession VI**

> This faith is bound to bring forth good fruits, and it is necessary to do good works commanded by God because of God's will; yet we should not rely on those works to merit justification before God.

*Study significance:* Good works are necessary fruits of faith, not merits that purchase God's favor.

**Further confessional anchors:** Small Catechism: Lord's Prayer, Baptism, Confession, and Sacrament of the Altar; Augsburg Confession V, IX, X, and XXIV; Apology XIII and XXIV (Sacraments and the Mass); Large Catechism: Lord's Prayer and Sacrament of the Altar.

#### Practicing the command

Give without publicity, maintain private prayer, and fast in a safe and freely chosen way that serves repentance and prayer.

#### Misuse caution

Fasting is not meritorious, compulsory in one uniform form, or suitable when medically unsafe.

#### Questions for examination and meditation

1. What exactly does Jesus command, forbid, promise, or warn in these passages, and who first receives the words?
2. Which words expose sin or false trust, and which words direct you to Christ's gift, forgiveness, or sustaining promise?
3. What concrete act of repentance, faith, mercy, or vocation would embody **practice almsgiving, prayer, and fasting before the father** this week without turning obedience into a basis of justification?

#### Prayer

Lord Jesus Christ, You call Your people to practice almsgiving, prayer, and fasting before the father. Forgive my failure, keep me in Your Word and gifts, and bear this fruit in me for the good of my neighbor and the glory of the Father. Amen.

### 47. Do not use empty phrases as though prayer manipulated God

**Corpus:** Gospels  
**Scope:** Universal  
**Earlier-list crosswalk:** #66  
**Canonical passages:** Matthew 6:7-8

#### Command-family wording

> Do not multiply words as a technique for forcing a hearing from God.

#### Scripture text (World English Bible)

##### Matthew 6:7-8

> **7** In praying, don’t use vain repetitions as the Gentiles do; for they think that they will be heard for their much speaking.
>
> **8** Therefore don’t be like them, for your Father knows what things you need before you ask him.

#### Reading the passages together

- The Father knows our need before we ask. Prayer rests on His command and promise, not verbal performance or altered consciousness.

#### Law, Gospel, and confessional synthesis

The Father knows our need before we ask. Prayer rests on His command and promise, not verbal performance or altered consciousness. In a confessional Lutheran reading, this command is not a ladder by which sinners earn justification. The Law exposes sin and directs the baptized life; the Gospel forgives, creates faith, joins the believer to Christ, and brings forth the fruits named here. The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

#### Early Christian witnesses

**Didache 8.2-3 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Neither pray as the hypocrites, but as the Lord commanded in his Gospel, thus pray: Our Father, who art in heaven. Three times in the day pray so.

*Study significance:* Jesus' model prayer was received as a repeated, formative practice rather than empty repetition.

**Augustine, Confessions 10.29 (Pusey translation, lightly modernized)**

> Give what You command, and command what You will.

*Study significance:* Augustine confesses that divine commands expose need and that God must supply the grace by which they are obeyed.

#### Lutheran confessional witnesses

**Small Catechism, Lord's Prayer, Introduction**

> God would thereby tenderly invite us to believe that He is our true Father and that we are His true children, so that we may ask Him confidently with all assurance, as dear children ask their dear father.

*Study significance:* Christian prayer rests on the Father's promise rather than quantity of words or emotional intensity.

**Augsburg Confession IV**

> People cannot be justified before God by their own strength, merits, or works, but are freely justified for Christ's sake, through faith, when they believe that they are received into favor and that their sins are forgiven for Christ's sake.

*Study significance:* The command must never be turned into a competing basis of justification.

**Further confessional anchors:** Small Catechism: Lord's Prayer, Baptism, Confession, and Sacrament of the Altar; Augsburg Confession V, IX, X, and XXIV; Apology XIII and XXIV (Sacraments and the Mass); Large Catechism: Lord's Prayer and Sacrament of the Altar.

#### Practicing the command

Pray plainly, use the Psalms and Lord's Prayer, and let repeated petitions remain attentive and faithful.

#### Misuse caution

Jesus is not condemning liturgy, written prayers, or sincere repetition; He condemns thoughtless and manipulative babbling.

#### Questions for examination and meditation

1. What exactly does Jesus command, forbid, promise, or warn in these passages, and who first receives the words?
2. Which words expose sin or false trust, and which words direct you to Christ's gift, forgiveness, or sustaining promise?
3. What concrete act of repentance, faith, mercy, or vocation would embody **do not use empty phrases as though prayer manipulated god** this week without turning obedience into a basis of justification?

#### Prayer

Lord Jesus Christ, You call Your people to do not use empty phrases as though prayer manipulated god. Forgive my failure, keep me in Your Word and gifts, and bear this fruit in me for the good of my neighbor and the glory of the Father. Amen.

<a id="command-048"></a>

### 48. Pray as Jesus teaches in the Lord's Prayer

**Corpus:** Gospels  
**Scope:** Universal  
**Earlier-list crosswalk:** #67  
**Canonical passages:** Matthew 6:9-13; Luke 11:1-4

#### Command-family wording

> Address the Father through the pattern and petitions Jesus gives.

#### Scripture text (World English Bible)

##### Matthew 6:9-13

> **9** Pray like this: “‘Our Father in heaven, may your name be kept holy.
>
> **10** Let your Kingdom come. Let your will be done on earth as it is in heaven.
>
> **11** Give us today our daily bread.
>
> **12** Forgive us our debts, as we also forgive our debtors.
>
> **13** Bring us not into temptation, but deliver us from the evil one. For yours is the Kingdom, the power, and the glory forever. Amen.’

##### Luke 11:1-4

> **1** When he finished praying in a certain place, one of his disciples said to him, “Lord, teach us to pray, just as John also taught his disciples.”
>
> **2** He said to them, “When you pray, say, ‘Our Father in heaven, may your name be kept holy. May your Kingdom come. May your will be done on earth, as it is in heaven.
>
> **3** Give us day by day our daily bread.
>
> **4** Forgive us our sins, for we ourselves also forgive everyone who is indebted to us. Bring us not into temptation, but deliver us from the evil one.’”

#### Reading the passages together

- The Lord's Prayer is both a fixed gift and a school of prayer: God's name, kingdom, will, daily bread, forgiveness, protection, and deliverance order Christian desire.

#### Law, Gospel, and confessional synthesis

The Lord's Prayer is both a fixed gift and a school of prayer: God's name, kingdom, will, daily bread, forgiveness, protection, and deliverance order Christian desire. In a confessional Lutheran reading, this command is not a ladder by which sinners earn justification. The Law exposes sin and directs the baptized life; the Gospel forgives, creates faith, joins the believer to Christ, and brings forth the fruits named here. The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

#### Early Christian witnesses

**Didache 8.2-3 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Neither pray as the hypocrites, but as the Lord commanded in his Gospel, thus pray: Our Father, who art in heaven. Three times in the day pray so.

*Study significance:* Jesus' model prayer was received as a repeated, formative practice rather than empty repetition.

**1 Clement 13.1-2 (Lightfoot/Roberts-Donaldson tradition, lightly modernized)**

> Let us be humble-minded, brethren, laying aside all haughtiness and pride and foolishness and angry feelings, especially remembering the words of the Lord Jesus: Be merciful, that you may obtain mercy; forgive, that it may be forgiven unto you.

*Study significance:* Clement explicitly uses Jesus' words to form a peaceful and humble congregation.

#### Lutheran confessional witnesses

**Small Catechism, Lord's Prayer, Introduction**

> God would thereby tenderly invite us to believe that He is our true Father and that we are His true children, so that we may ask Him confidently with all assurance, as dear children ask their dear father.

*Study significance:* Christian prayer rests on the Father's promise rather than quantity of words or emotional intensity.

**Augsburg Confession V**

> That we may obtain this faith, the Ministry of Teaching the Gospel and administering the Sacraments was instituted. Through the Word and Sacraments, as through instruments, the Holy Spirit is given, who works faith where and when it pleases God.

*Study significance:* Faith is created and sustained through Christ's appointed means rather than self-generated resolve.

**Further confessional anchors:** Small Catechism: Lord's Prayer, Baptism, Confession, and Sacrament of the Altar; Augsburg Confession V, IX, X, and XXIV; Apology XIII and XXIV (Sacraments and the Mass); Large Catechism: Lord's Prayer and Sacrament of the Altar.

#### Practicing the command

Pray it daily, learn each petition from the Small Catechism, expand its petitions for particular needs, and use it in household and congregation.

#### Misuse caution

Do not treat it as a technique whose correct recitation guarantees a chosen temporal outcome.

#### Questions for examination and meditation

1. What exactly does Jesus command, forbid, promise, or warn in these passages, and who first receives the words?
2. Which words expose sin or false trust, and which words direct you to Christ's gift, forgiveness, or sustaining promise?
3. What concrete act of repentance, faith, mercy, or vocation would embody **pray as jesus teaches in the lord's prayer** this week without turning obedience into a basis of justification?

#### Prayer

Lord Jesus Christ, You call Your people to pray as jesus teaches in the lord's prayer. Forgive my failure, keep me in Your Word and gifts, and bear this fruit in me for the good of my neighbor and the glory of the Father. Amen.

### 49. Ask, seek, and knock

**Corpus:** Gospels  
**Scope:** Universal  
**Earlier-list crosswalk:** #69  
**Canonical passages:** Matthew 7:7-11; Luke 11:5-13

#### Command-family wording

> Persist in prayer to the Father, trusting His goodness.

#### Scripture text (World English Bible)

##### Matthew 7:7-11

> **7** “Ask, and it will be given you. Seek, and you will find. Knock, and it will be opened for you.
>
> **8** For everyone who asks receives. He who seeks finds. To him who knocks it will be opened.
>
> **9** Or who is there among you who, if his son asks him for bread, will give him a stone?
>
> **10** Or if he asks for a fish, who will give him a serpent?
>
> **11** If you then, being evil, know how to give good gifts to your children, how much more will your Father who is in heaven give good things to those who ask him!

##### Luke 11:5-13

> **5** He said to them, “Which of you, if you go to a friend at midnight and tell him, ‘Friend, lend me three loaves of bread,
>
> **6** for a friend of mine has come to me from a journey, and I have nothing to set before him,’
>
> **7** and he from within will answer and say, ‘Don’t bother me. The door is now shut, and my children are with me in bed. I can’t get up and give it to you’?
>
> **8** I tell you, although he will not rise and give it to him because he is his friend, yet because of his persistence, he will get up and give him as many as he needs.
>
> **9** “I tell you, keep asking, and it will be given you. Keep seeking, and you will find. Keep knocking, and it will be opened to you.
>
> **10** For everyone who asks receives. He who seeks finds. To him who knocks it will be opened.
>
> **11** “Which of you fathers, if your son asks for bread, will give him a stone? Or if he asks for a fish, he won’t give him a snake instead of a fish, will he?
>
> **12** Or if he asks for an egg, he won’t give him a scorpion, will he?
>
> **13** If you then, being evil, know how to give good gifts to your children, how much more will your heavenly Father give the Holy Spirit to those who ask him?”

- Jesus grounds bold prayer in the Father's character and climaxes the promise with the gift of the Holy Spirit. Persistence is faith's exercise, not leverage over God.

*Study significance:* Christian prayer rests on the Father's promise rather than quantity of words or emotional intensity.

**Augsburg Confession IV**

> People cannot be justified before God by their own strength, merits, or works, but are freely justified for Christ's sake, through faith, when they believe that they are received into favor and that their sins are forgiven for Christ's sake.

*Study significance:* The command must never be turned into a competing basis of justification.

**Further confessional anchors:** Small Catechism: Lord's Prayer, Baptism, Confession, and Sacrament of the Altar; Augsburg Confession V, IX, X, and XXIV; Apology XIII and XXIV (Sacraments and the Mass); Large Catechism: Lord's Prayer and Sacrament of the Altar.

#### Practicing the command

Bring needs repeatedly, pray from explicit promises, ask others to intercede, and receive God's answer through His Word and wise providence.

#### Misuse caution

The promise does not guarantee every requested object, timing, or imagined solution.

#### Questions for examination and meditation

1. What exactly does Jesus command, forbid, promise, or warn in these passages, and who first receives the words?
2. Which words expose sin or false trust, and which words direct you to Christ's gift, forgiveness, or sustaining promise?
3. What concrete act of repentance, faith, mercy, or vocation would embody **ask, seek, and knock** this week without turning obedience into a basis of justification?

#### Prayer

Lord Jesus Christ, You call Your people to ask, seek, and knock. Forgive my failure, keep me in Your Word and gifts, and bear this fruit in me for the good of my neighbor and the glory of the Father. Amen.

<a id="command-050"></a>

### 50. See the harvest and pray for laborers

**Corpus:** Gospels  
**Scope:** Universal prayer with churchly implementation  
**Earlier-list crosswalk:** #70  
**Canonical passages:** Matthew 9:35-38; Luke 10:2; John 4:35

#### Command-family wording

> See people with Christ's compassion and ask the Lord of the harvest to send workers.

#### Scripture text (World English Bible)

##### Matthew 9:35-38

> **35** Jesus went about all the cities and the villages, teaching in their synagogues and preaching the Good News of the Kingdom, and healing every disease and every sickness among the people.
>
> **36** But when he saw the multitudes, he was moved with compassion for them because they were harassed and scattered, like sheep without a shepherd.
>
> **37** Then he said to his disciples, “The harvest indeed is plentiful, but the laborers are few.
>
> **38** Pray therefore that the Lord of the harvest will send out laborers into his harvest.”

##### Luke 10:2

> **2** Then he said to them, “The harvest is indeed plentiful, but the laborers are few. Pray therefore to the Lord of the harvest, that he may send out laborers into his harvest.

##### John 4:35

> **35** Don’t you say, ‘There are yet four months until the harvest’? Behold, I tell you, lift up your eyes and look at the fields, that they are white for harvest already.

#### Reading the passages together

- Mission begins in the Lord's compassion and sending. The Church does not manufacture the harvest or the ministry; God creates faith through His appointed means.

#### Law, Gospel, and confessional synthesis

Mission begins in the Lord's compassion and sending. The Church does not manufacture the harvest or the ministry; God creates faith through His appointed means. In a confessional Lutheran reading, this command is not a ladder by which sinners earn justification. The Law exposes sin and directs the baptized life; the Gospel forgives, creates faith, joins the believer to Christ, and brings forth the fruits named here. The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

#### Early Christian witnesses

**Didache 15.1 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Appoint, therefore, for yourselves bishops and deacons worthy of the Lord, men who are meek, not lovers of money, truthful, and proven; for they also render to you the service of prophets and teachers.

*Study significance:* Mission and pastoral care are entrusted to tested servants rather than self-appointed status seekers.

**Justin Martyr, First Apology 67 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> On the day called Sunday, all who live in cities or in the country gather together to one place, and the memoirs of the apostles or the writings of the prophets are read as long as time permits.

*Study significance:* The church gathers around apostolic and prophetic Scripture rather than private inspiration alone.

#### Lutheran confessional witnesses

**Augsburg Confession V**

> That we may obtain this faith, the Ministry of Teaching the Gospel and administering the Sacraments was instituted. Through the Word and Sacraments, as through instruments, the Holy Spirit is given, who works faith where and when it pleases God.

*Study significance:* Faith is created and sustained through Christ's appointed means rather than self-generated resolve.

**Augsburg Confession XIV**

> No one should publicly teach in the Church or administer the Sacraments unless he be regularly called.

*Study significance:* Christ's mission is carried out through ordered vocation rather than self-authorization.

**Further confessional anchors:** Small Catechism: Lord's Prayer, Baptism, Confession, and Sacrament of the Altar; Augsburg Confession V, IX, X, and XXIV; Apology XIII and XXIV (Sacraments and the Mass); Large Catechism: Lord's Prayer and Sacrament of the Altar.

#### Practicing the command

Pray by name for pastors, missionaries, teachers, and hearers; support theological formation; invite qualified people to consider service; and welcome neighbors.

#### Misuse caution

Urgency does not cancel doctrinal formation, orderly calling, safeguarding, or care for workers and their households.

#### Questions for examination and meditation

1. What exactly does Jesus command, forbid, promise, or warn in these passages, and who first receives the words?
2. Which words expose sin or false trust, and which words direct you to Christ's gift, forgiveness, or sustaining promise?
3. What concrete act of repentance, faith, mercy, or vocation would embody **see the harvest and pray for laborers** this week without turning obedience into a basis of justification?

#### Prayer

Lord Jesus Christ, You call Your people to see the harvest and pray for laborers. Forgive my failure, keep me in Your Word and gifts, and bear this fruit in me for the good of my neighbor and the glory of the Father. Amen.

<a id="command-051"></a>

### 51. Take, eat, drink, and do this in remembrance of Jesus

**Corpus:** Gospels  
**Scope:** Churchly and sacramental  
**Earlier-list crosswalk:** #71  
**Canonical passages:** Matthew 26:26-29; Mark 14:22-25; Luke 22:14-20; 1 Corinthians 11:23-26

#### Command-family wording

> Receive the Supper Christ instituted and continue its proclamation until He comes.

#### Scripture text (World English Bible)

##### Matthew 26:26-29

> **26** As they were eating, Jesus took bread, gave thanks for it, and broke it. He gave to the disciples and said, “Take, eat; this is my body.”
>
> **27** He took the cup, gave thanks, and gave to them, saying, “All of you drink it,
>
> **28** for this is my blood of the new covenant, which is poured out for many for the remission of sins.
>
> **29** But I tell you that I will not drink of this fruit of the vine from now on, until that day when I drink it anew with you in my Father’s Kingdom.”

##### Mark 14:22-25

> **22** As they were eating, Jesus took bread, and when he had blessed it, he broke it and gave to them, and said, “Take, eat. This is my body.”
>
> **23** He took the cup, and when he had given thanks, he gave to them. They all drank of it.
>
> **24** He said to them, “This is my blood of the new covenant, which is poured out for many.
>
> **25** Most certainly I tell you, I will no more drink of the fruit of the vine until that day when I drink it anew in God’s Kingdom.”

##### Luke 22:14-20

> **14** When the hour had come, he sat down with the twelve apostles.
>
> **15** He said to them, “I have earnestly desired to eat this Passover with you before I suffer,
>
> **16** for I tell you, I will no longer by any means eat of it until it is fulfilled in God’s Kingdom.”
>
> **17** He received a cup, and when he had given thanks, he said, “Take this and share it among yourselves,
>
> **18** for I tell you, I will not drink at all again from the fruit of the vine, until God’s Kingdom comes.”
>
> **19** He took bread, and when he had given thanks, he broke and gave it to them, saying, “This is my body which is given for you. Do this in memory of me.”
>
> **20** Likewise, he took the cup after supper, saying, “This cup is the new covenant in my blood, which is poured out for you.

##### 1 Corinthians 11:23-26

> **23** For I received from the Lord that which also I delivered to you, that the Lord Jesus on the night in which he was betrayed took bread.
>
> **24** When he had given thanks, he broke it and said, “Take, eat. This is my body, which is broken for you. Do this in memory of me.”
>
> **25** In the same way he also took the cup after supper, saying, “This cup is the new covenant in my blood. Do this, as often as you drink, in memory of me.”
>
> **26** For as often as you eat this bread and drink this cup, you proclaim the Lord’s death until he comes.

**Apostolic witness: this passage is Paul's received account of Jesus' institution of the Supper, included because the command family explicitly cites it.**

#### Reading the passages together

- Christ joins His testamentary words to bread and wine and gives His body and blood for the forgiveness of sins. Remembrance is the Church's reception and proclamation of His saving gift, not bare mental recollection.

#### Law, Gospel, and confessional synthesis

Christ joins His testamentary words to bread and wine and gives His body and blood for the forgiveness of sins. Remembrance is the Church's reception and proclamation of His saving gift, not bare mental recollection. In a confessional Lutheran reading, this command is not a ladder by which sinners earn justification. The Law exposes sin and directs the baptized life; the Gospel forgives, creates faith, joins the believer to Christ, and brings forth the fruits named here. The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

#### Early Christian witnesses

**Didache 9.1, 4 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Now concerning the Eucharist, give thanks after this manner. As this broken bread was scattered upon the mountains and, being gathered together, became one, so may your Church be gathered together from the ends of the earth into your kingdom.

*Study significance:* The prayer connects the meal with thanksgiving, the kingdom, and the unity of the Church.

**Justin Martyr, First Apology 66 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> This food is called among us Eucharistia. We do not receive these as common bread and common drink; but as Jesus Christ our Savior was made flesh, so likewise have we been taught that the food blessed by the prayer of His word is the flesh and blood of that Jesus who was made flesh.

*Study significance:* Justin gives an actual early description of the church's reverent reception of the Supper.

#### Lutheran confessional witnesses

**Small Catechism, Sacrament of the Altar I**

> It is the true body and blood of our Lord Jesus Christ, under the bread and wine, for us Christians to eat and to drink, instituted by Christ Himself.

*Study significance:* The Supper is received according to Christ's institution and promise.

**Augsburg Confession V**

> That we may obtain this faith, the Ministry of Teaching the Gospel and administering the Sacraments was instituted. Through the Word and Sacraments, as through instruments, the Holy Spirit is given, who works faith where and when it pleases God.

*Study significance:* Faith is created and sustained through Christ's appointed means rather than self-generated resolve.

**Further confessional anchors:** Small Catechism: Lord's Prayer, Baptism, Confession, and Sacrament of the Altar; Augsburg Confession V, IX, X, and XXIV; Apology XIII and XXIV (Sacraments and the Mass); Large Catechism: Lord's Prayer and Sacrament of the Altar.

#### Practicing the command

Attend Divine Service, learn the Words of Institution, examine yourself through the Catechism, reconcile where possible, and receive according to your congregation's pastoral practice.

#### Misuse caution

Do not detach the command from Christ's body and blood, forgiveness, the gathered Church, or pastoral care.

#### Questions for examination and meditation

1. What exactly does Jesus command, forbid, promise, or warn in these passages, and who first receives the words?
2. Which words expose sin or false trust, and which words direct you to Christ's gift, forgiveness, or sustaining promise?
3. What concrete act of repentance, faith, mercy, or vocation would embody **take, eat, drink, and do this in remembrance of jesus** this week without turning obedience into a basis of justification?

#### Prayer

Lord Jesus Christ, You call Your people to take, eat, drink, and do this in remembrance of jesus. Forgive my failure, keep me in Your Word and gifts, and bear this fruit in me for the good of my neighbor and the glory of the Father. Amen.

<a id="command-052"></a>

### 52. Watch and pray so that you do not enter temptation

**Corpus:** Gospels  
**Scope:** Universal  
**Earlier-list crosswalk:** #72  
**Canonical passages:** Matthew 26:38-41; Mark 14:34-38; Luke 22:40,46

#### Command-family wording

> Stay spiritually awake and pray against temptation, recognizing the weakness of the flesh.

#### Scripture text (World English Bible)

##### Matthew 26:38-41

> **38** Then Jesus said to them, “My soul is exceedingly sorrowful, even to death. Stay here and watch with me.”
>
> **39** He went forward a little, fell on his face, and prayed, saying, “My Father, if it is possible, let this cup pass away from me; nevertheless, not what I desire, but what you desire.”
>
> **40** He came to the disciples and found them sleeping, and said to Peter, “What, couldn’t you watch with me for one hour?
>
> **41** Watch and pray, that you don’t enter into temptation. The spirit indeed is willing, but the flesh is weak.”

##### Mark 14:34-38

> **34** He said to them, “My soul is exceedingly sorrowful, even to death. Stay here and watch.”
>
> **35** He went forward a little, and fell on the ground, and prayed that if it were possible, the hour might pass away from him.
>
> **36** He said, “Abba, Father, all things are possible to you. Please remove this cup from me. However, not what I desire, but what you desire.”
>
> **37** He came and found them sleeping, and said to Peter, “Simon, are you sleeping? Couldn’t you watch one hour?
>
> **38** Watch and pray, that you may not enter into temptation. The spirit indeed is willing, but the flesh is weak.”

##### Luke 22:40,46

> **40** When he was at the place, he said to them, “Pray that you don’t enter into temptation.”
>
> **46** and said to them, “Why do you sleep? Rise and pray that you may not enter into temptation.”

#### Reading the passages together

- The disciples' failure exposes human weakness while Jesus faithfully goes to His Passion. Vigilance therefore depends on His victory and His intercession.

#### Law, Gospel, and confessional synthesis

The disciples' failure exposes human weakness while Jesus faithfully goes to His Passion. Vigilance therefore depends on His victory and His intercession. In a confessional Lutheran reading, this command is not a ladder by which sinners earn justification. The Law exposes sin and directs the baptized life; the Gospel forgives, creates faith, joins the believer to Christ, and brings forth the fruits named here. The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

#### Early Christian witnesses

**Didache 16.1 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Watch for your life's sake. Let not your lamps be quenched, nor your loins unloosed; but be ready, for you do not know the hour in which our Lord comes.

*Study significance:* The passage directly preserves the church's early reception of Jesus' readiness language.

**Didache 8.2-3 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Neither pray as the hypocrites, but as the Lord commanded in his Gospel, thus pray: Our Father, who art in heaven. Three times in the day pray so.

*Study significance:* Jesus' model prayer was received as a repeated, formative practice rather than empty repetition.

#### Lutheran confessional witnesses

**Small Catechism, Lord's Prayer, Introduction**

> God would thereby tenderly invite us to believe that He is our true Father and that we are His true children, so that we may ask Him confidently with all assurance, as dear children ask their dear father.

*Study significance:* Christian prayer rests on the Father's promise rather than quantity of words or emotional intensity.

**Augsburg Confession XII**

> Properly speaking, repentance consists of these two parts: one is contrition, that is, terrors smiting the conscience through the knowledge of sin; the other is faith, born of the Gospel or of absolution, which believes that for Christ's sake sins are forgiven, comforts the conscience, and delivers it from terrors.

*Study significance:* Repentance includes both truthful sorrow over sin and faith in Christ's forgiveness.

**Further confessional anchors:** Small Catechism: Lord's Prayer, Baptism, Confession, and Sacrament of the Altar; Augsburg Confession V, IX, X, and XXIV; Apology XIII and XXIV (Sacraments and the Mass); Large Catechism: Lord's Prayer and Sacrament of the Altar.

#### Practicing the command

Name recurring temptations, pray before predictable pressure, use Scripture, seek accountability, sleep and rest responsibly, and flee dangerous settings.

#### Misuse caution

Watchfulness is not anxious self-surveillance or confidence in willpower.

#### Questions for examination and meditation

1. What exactly does Jesus command, forbid, promise, or warn in these passages, and who first receives the words?
2. Which words expose sin or false trust, and which words direct you to Christ's gift, forgiveness, or sustaining promise?
3. What concrete act of repentance, faith, mercy, or vocation would embody **watch and pray so that you do not enter temptation** this week without turning obedience into a basis of justification?

#### Prayer

Lord Jesus Christ, You call Your people to watch and pray so that you do not enter temptation. Forgive my failure, keep me in Your Word and gifts, and bear this fruit in me for the good of my neighbor and the glory of the Father. Amen.

### 53. Rejoice when persecuted for Christ and His righteousness

**Corpus:** Gospels  
**Scope:** Universal under genuine persecution  
**Earlier-list crosswalk:** #73  
**Canonical passages:** Matthew 5:10-12; Luke 6:22-23

#### Command-family wording

> Rejoice in God's promised kingdom even when faithful confession brings rejection or suffering.

#### Scripture text (World English Bible)

##### Matthew 5:10-12

> **10** Blessed are those who have been persecuted for righteousness’ sake, for theirs is the Kingdom of Heaven.
>
> **11** “Blessed are you when people reproach you, persecute you, and say all kinds of evil against you falsely, for my sake.
>
> **12** Rejoice, and be exceedingly glad, for great is your reward in heaven. For that is how they persecuted the prophets who were before you.

##### Luke 6:22-23

> **22** Blessed are you when men hate you, and when they exclude and mock you, and throw out your name as evil, for the Son of Man’s sake.
>
> **23** Rejoice in that day and leap for joy, for behold, your reward is great in heaven, for their fathers did the same thing to the prophets.

#### Reading the passages together

- The joy is eschatological and can coexist with grief. It rests on belonging to Christ and sharing the prophets' pattern, not on pain itself.

#### Law, Gospel, and confessional synthesis

The joy is eschatological and can coexist with grief. It rests on belonging to Christ and sharing the prophets' pattern, not on pain itself. In a confessional Lutheran reading, this command is not a ladder by which sinners earn justification. The Law exposes sin and directs the baptized life; the Gospel forgives, creates faith, joins the believer to Christ, and brings forth the fruits named here. The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

#### Early Christian witnesses

**Justin Martyr, First Apology 16 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Concerning our being patient of injuries: Whoever shall strike you on the right cheek, turn to him the other also. And: Let your good works shine before men, that they, seeing them, may glorify your Father who is in heaven.

*Study significance:* The early apologist places non-retaliation and visible good works together as Christian witness.

**Didache 16.1 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Watch for your life's sake. Let not your lamps be quenched, nor your loins unloosed; but be ready, for you do not know the hour in which our Lord comes.

*Study significance:* The passage directly preserves the church's early reception of Jesus' readiness language.

#### Lutheran confessional witnesses

**Augsburg Confession IV**

> People cannot be justified before God by their own strength, merits, or works, but are freely justified for Christ's sake, through faith, when they believe that they are received into favor and that their sins are forgiven for Christ's sake.

*Study significance:* The command must never be turned into a competing basis of justification.

**Augsburg Confession VI**

> This faith is bound to bring forth good fruits, and it is necessary to do good works commanded by God because of God's will; yet we should not rely on those works to merit justification before God.

*Study significance:* Good works are necessary fruits of faith, not merits that purchase God's favor.

**Further confessional anchors:** Small Catechism: Lord's Prayer, Baptism, Confession, and Sacrament of the Altar; Augsburg Confession V, IX, X, and XXIV; Apology XIII and XXIV (Sacraments and the Mass); Large Catechism: Lord's Prayer and Sacrament of the Altar.

#### Practicing the command

Pray, lament, seek help, support persecuted Christians, refuse shame about Christ, and remember the resurrection and promised reward.

#### Misuse caution

Not every criticism, consequence, or interpersonal conflict counts as persecution for Christ.

#### Questions for examination and meditation

1. What exactly does Jesus command, forbid, promise, or warn in these passages, and who first receives the words?
2. Which words expose sin or false trust, and which words direct you to Christ's gift, forgiveness, or sustaining promise?
3. What concrete act of repentance, faith, mercy, or vocation would embody **rejoice when persecuted for christ and his righteousness** this week without turning obedience into a basis of justification?

#### Prayer

Lord Jesus Christ, You call Your people to rejoice when persecuted for christ and his righteousness. Forgive my failure, keep me in Your Word and gifts, and bear this fruit in me for the good of my neighbor and the glory of the Father. Amen.

>

In a confessional Lutheran reading, this command is not a ladder by which sinners earn justification. The Law exposes sin and directs the baptized life; the Gospel forgives, creates faith, joins the believer to Christ, and brings forth the fruits named here. The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

**Justin Martyr, First Apology 16 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

**Justin Martyr, First Apology 67 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> On the day called Sunday, all who live in cities or in the country gather together to one place, and the memoirs of the apostles or the writings of the prophets are read as long as time permits.

*Study significance:* The church gathers around apostolic and prophetic Scripture rather than private inspiration alone.

#### Lutheran confessional witnesses

**Augsburg Confession VI**

> This faith is bound to bring forth good fruits, and it is necessary to do good works commanded by God because of God's will; yet we should not rely on those works to merit justification before God.

*Study significance:* Good works are necessary fruits of faith, not merits that purchase God's favor.

**Augsburg Confession V**

> That we may obtain this faith, the Ministry of Teaching the Gospel and administering the Sacraments was instituted. Through the Word and Sacraments, as through instruments, the Holy Spirit is given, who works faith where and when it pleases God.

*Study significance:* Faith is created and sustained through Christ's appointed means rather than self-generated resolve.

Speak the Creed without embarrassment, explain the hope of Christ, serve neighbors publicly, and give credit to God and collaborators.
