<a id="command-055"></a>

### 55. Store treasure in heaven and labor for enduring food

**Corpus:** Gospels  
**Scope:** Universal  
**Earlier-list crosswalk:** #75  
**Canonical passages:** Matthew 6:19-24; Luke 12:32-34; John 6:27

#### Command-family wording

> Do not make perishable wealth or food your master; treasure God's kingdom and receive the food the Son gives.

#### Scripture text (World English Bible)

##### Matthew 6:19-24

> **19** “Don’t lay up treasures for yourselves on the earth, where moth and rust consume, and where thieves break through and steal;
>
> **20** but lay up for yourselves treasures in heaven, where neither moth nor rust consume, and where thieves don’t break through and steal;
>
> **21** for where your treasure is, there your heart will be also.
>
> **22** “The lamp of the body is the eye. If therefore your eye is sound, your whole body will be full of light.
>
> **23** But if your eye is evil, your whole body will be full of darkness. If therefore the light that is in you is darkness, how great is the darkness!
>
> **24** “No one can serve two masters, for either he will hate the one and love the other, or else he will be devoted to one and despise the other. You can’t serve both God and Mammon.

##### Luke 12:32-34

> **32** “Don’t be afraid, little flock, for it is your Father’s good pleasure to give you the Kingdom.
>
> **33** Sell what you have and give gifts to the needy. Make for yourselves purses which don’t grow old, a treasure in the heavens that doesn’t fail, where no thief approaches and no moth destroys.
>
> **34** For where your treasure is, there will your heart be also.

##### John 6:27

> **27** Don’t work for the food which perishes, but for the food which remains to eternal life, which the Son of Man will give to you. For God the Father has sealed him.”

#### Reading the passages together

- The heart follows its treasure, and no one can serve both God and mammon. The imperishable food is given by the Son of Man, not earned by religious exertion.

#### Law, Gospel, and confessional synthesis

The heart follows its treasure, and no one can serve both God and mammon. The imperishable food is given by the Son of Man, not earned by religious exertion. In a confessional Lutheran reading, this command is not a ladder by which sinners earn justification. The Law exposes sin and directs the baptized life; the Gospel forgives, creates faith, joins the believer to Christ, and brings forth the fruits named here. The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

#### Early Christian witnesses

**Didache 1.5 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Give to everyone who asks you, and ask it not back; for the Father wills that to all should be given of our own blessings.

*Study significance:* The command is received as real generosity flowing from the Father's gifts.

**Didache 1.1-2 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> There are two ways, one of life and one of death; but there is a great difference between these two ways. The way of life, then, is this: First, you shall love God who made you; second, your neighbor as yourself; and all things whatsoever you would not have done to you, do not do to another.

*Study significance:* An early Christian catechetical summary joins love of God, love of neighbor, and concrete conduct.

#### Lutheran confessional witnesses

**Small Catechism, First Commandment**

> We should fear, love, and trust in God above all things.

*Study significance:* Every command family is finally ordered under trust in the true God.

**Small Catechism, Seventh Commandment**

> We should fear and love God that we may not take our neighbor's money or property, nor get them by false ware or dealing, but help him to improve and protect his property and business.

*Study significance:* Generosity and economic integrity seek the neighbor's material good.

**Further confessional anchors:** Small Catechism: First Commandment and First/Second Petitions; Augsburg Confession XVI and XX (civil affairs and faith/good works); Apology XVI (political order and Christian freedom); Formula of Concord, Solid Declaration IV and VI.

#### Practicing the command

Give generously, simplify where possessions rule, budget around vocation and mercy, receive Word and Sacrament, and audit what consumes attention.

#### Misuse caution

This is not a heavenly merit account and does not condemn prudent provision or delight in created gifts.

#### Questions for examination and meditation

1. What exactly does Jesus command, forbid, promise, or warn in these passages, and who first receives the words?
2. Which words expose sin or false trust, and which words direct you to Christ's gift, forgiveness, or sustaining promise?
3. What concrete act of repentance, faith, mercy, or vocation would embody **store treasure in heaven and labor for enduring food** this week without turning obedience into a basis of justification?

#### Prayer

Lord Jesus Christ, You call Your people to store treasure in heaven and labor for enduring food. Forgive my failure, keep me in Your Word and gifts, and bear this fruit in me for the good of my neighbor and the glory of the Father. Amen.

<a id="command-056"></a>

### 56. Seek first God's kingdom

**Corpus:** Gospels  
**Scope:** Universal  
**Earlier-list crosswalk:** #76  
**Canonical passages:** Matthew 6:31-34; Luke 12:29-31

#### Command-family wording

> Order life under God's reign and righteousness rather than anxious pursuit of necessities.

#### Scripture text (World English Bible)

##### Matthew 6:31-34

> **31** “Therefore don’t be anxious, saying, ‘What will we eat?’, ‘What will we drink?’ or, ‘With what will we be clothed?’
>
> **32** For the Gentiles seek after all these things; for your heavenly Father knows that you need all these things.
>
> **33** But seek first God’s Kingdom and his righteousness; and all these things will be given to you as well.
>
> **34** Therefore don’t be anxious for tomorrow, for tomorrow will be anxious for itself. Each day’s own evil is sufficient.

##### Luke 12:29-31

> **29** “Don’t seek what you will eat or what you will drink; neither be anxious.
>
> **30** For the nations of the world seek after all of these things, but your Father knows that you need these things.
>
> **31** But seek God’s Kingdom, and all these things will be added to you.

#### Reading the passages together

- The kingdom comes by God's Word and Spirit. Seeking it means receiving the King's gifts and living in the vocations His Word orders.

#### Law, Gospel, and confessional synthesis

The kingdom comes by God's Word and Spirit. Seeking it means receiving the King's gifts and living in the vocations His Word orders. In a confessional Lutheran reading, this command is not a ladder by which sinners earn justification. The Law exposes sin and directs the baptized life; the Gospel forgives, creates faith, joins the believer to Christ, and brings forth the fruits named here. The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

#### Early Christian witnesses

**Didache 1.1-2 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> There are two ways, one of life and one of death; but there is a great difference between these two ways. The way of life, then, is this: First, you shall love God who made you; second, your neighbor as yourself; and all things whatsoever you would not have done to you, do not do to another.

*Study significance:* An early Christian catechetical summary joins love of God, love of neighbor, and concrete conduct.

**Augustine, Confessions 10.29 (Pusey translation, lightly modernized)**

> Give what You command, and command what You will.

*Study significance:* Augustine confesses that divine commands expose need and that God must supply the grace by which they are obeyed.

#### Lutheran confessional witnesses

**Small Catechism, First Commandment**

> We should fear, love, and trust in God above all things.

*Study significance:* Every command family is finally ordered under trust in the true God.

**Augsburg Confession IV**

> People cannot be justified before God by their own strength, merits, or works, but are freely justified for Christ's sake, through faith, when they believe that they are received into favor and that their sins are forgiven for Christ's sake.

*Study significance:* The command must never be turned into a competing basis of justification.

**Further confessional anchors:** Small Catechism: First Commandment and First/Second Petitions; Augsburg Confession XVI and XX (civil affairs and faith/good works); Apology XVI (political order and Christian freedom); Formula of Concord, Solid Declaration IV and VI.

#### Practicing the command

Prioritize worship, Scripture, prayer, honest work, care of dependents, and service to neighbors while asking for daily bread.

#### Misuse caution

Seeking the kingdom does not excuse neglect of ordinary duties, planning, medicine, or work.

#### Questions for examination and meditation

1. What exactly does Jesus command, forbid, promise, or warn in these passages, and who first receives the words?
2. Which words expose sin or false trust, and which words direct you to Christ's gift, forgiveness, or sustaining promise?
3. What concrete act of repentance, faith, mercy, or vocation would embody **seek first god's kingdom** this week without turning obedience into a basis of justification?

#### Prayer

Lord Jesus Christ, You call Your people to seek first god's kingdom. Forgive my failure, keep me in Your Word and gifts, and bear this fruit in me for the good of my neighbor and the glory of the Father. Amen.

### 57. Beware false prophets, false christs, and deceivers

**Corpus:** Gospels  
**Scope:** Universal  
**Earlier-list crosswalk:** #77  
**Canonical passages:** Matthew 7:15-20; 24:4-5,11,23-26; Mark 13:5-6,21-23; Luke 21:8

#### Command-family wording

> Test teachers and claims rather than following impressive words, signs, or urgent predictions.

#### Scripture text (World English Bible)

##### Matthew 7:15-20

> **15** “Beware of false prophets, who come to you in sheep’s clothing, but inwardly are ravening wolves.
>
> **16** By their fruits you will know them. Do you gather grapes from thorns or figs from thistles?
>
> **17** Even so, every good tree produces good fruit, but the corrupt tree produces evil fruit.
>
> **18** A good tree can’t produce evil fruit, neither can a corrupt tree produce good fruit.
>
> **19** Every tree that doesn’t grow good fruit is cut down and thrown into the fire.
>
> **20** Therefore by their fruits you will know them.

##### Matthew 24:4-5,11,23-26

> **4** Jesus answered them, “Be careful that no one leads you astray.
>
> **5** For many will come in my name, saying, ‘I am the Christ,’ and will lead many astray.
>
> **11** Many false prophets will arise and will lead many astray.
>
> **23** “Then if any man tells you, ‘Behold, here is the Christ!’ or, ‘There!’ don’t believe it.
>
> **24** For false christs and false prophets will arise, and they will show great signs and wonders, so as to lead astray, if possible, even the chosen ones.
>
> **25** “Behold, I have told you beforehand.
>
> **26** “If therefore they tell you, ‘Behold, he is in the wilderness,’ don’t go out; or ‘Behold, he is in the inner rooms,’ don’t believe it.

##### Mark 13:5-6,21-23

> **5** Jesus, answering, began to tell them, “Be careful that no one leads you astray.
>
> **6** For many will come in my name, saying, ‘I am he!’ and will lead many astray.
>
> **21** Then if anyone tells you, ‘Look, here is the Christ!’ or, ‘Look, there!’ don’t believe it.
>
> **22** For false christs and false prophets will arise and will show signs and wonders, that they may lead astray, if possible, even the chosen ones.
>
> **23** But you watch. “Behold, I have told you all things beforehand.

##### Luke 21:8

> **8** He said, “Watch out that you don’t get led astray, for many will come in my name, saying, ‘I am he,’ and, ‘The time is at hand.’ Therefore don’t follow them.

#### Reading the passages together

- False teaching is known by its confession and fruit under the apostolic Word. Signs do not authenticate a message that contradicts Christ's doctrine.

#### Law, Gospel, and confessional synthesis

False teaching is known by its confession and fruit under the apostolic Word. Signs do not authenticate a message that contradicts Christ's doctrine. In a confessional Lutheran reading, this command is not a ladder by which sinners earn justification. The Law exposes sin and directs the baptized life; the Gospel forgives, creates faith, joins the believer to Christ, and brings forth the fruits named here. The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

#### Early Christian witnesses

**Didache 11.1-2 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Whoever comes and teaches you all these things that have been said before, receive him. But if the teacher himself turns and teaches another doctrine to the destruction of this, do not hear him.

*Study significance:* Hospitality and openness are joined to doctrinal discernment.

**Justin Martyr, First Apology 67 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> On the day called Sunday, all who live in cities or in the country gather together to one place, and the memoirs of the apostles or the writings of the prophets are read as long as time permits.

*Study significance:* The church gathers around apostolic and prophetic Scripture rather than private inspiration alone.

#### Lutheran confessional witnesses

**Augsburg Confession V**

> That we may obtain this faith, the Ministry of Teaching the Gospel and administering the Sacraments was instituted. Through the Word and Sacraments, as through instruments, the Holy Spirit is given, who works faith where and when it pleases God.

*Study significance:* Faith is created and sustained through Christ's appointed means rather than self-generated resolve.

**Augsburg Confession XIV**

> No one should publicly teach in the Church or administer the Sacraments unless he be regularly called.

*Study significance:* Christ's mission is carried out through ordered vocation rather than self-authorization.

**Further confessional anchors:** Small Catechism: First Commandment and First/Second Petitions; Augsburg Confession XVI and XX (civil affairs and faith/good works); Apology XVI (political order and Christian freedom); Formula of Concord, Solid Declaration IV and VI.

#### Practicing the command

Know Scripture and the Creeds, ask what a teacher says about Christ and salvation, examine accountability and fruit, and consult faithful pastors.

#### Misuse caution

Discernment must not become rumor, guilt by association, or confidence that charisma proves either truth or falsehood.

#### Questions for examination and meditation

1. What exactly does Jesus command, forbid, promise, or warn in these passages, and who first receives the words?
2. Which words expose sin or false trust, and which words direct you to Christ's gift, forgiveness, or sustaining promise?
3. What concrete act of repentance, faith, mercy, or vocation would embody **beware false prophets, false christs, and deceivers** this week without turning obedience into a basis of justification?

#### Prayer

Lord Jesus Christ, You call Your people to beware false prophets, false christs, and deceivers. Forgive my failure, keep me in Your Word and gifts, and bear this fruit in me for the good of my neighbor and the glory of the Father. Amen.

## Watchfulness, Judgment, and Readiness

Christians live awake, repentant, and hopeful because the Lord will return.

<a id="command-058"></a>

### 58. Stay awake, keep watch, and be ready for the Son of Man

**Corpus:** Gospels  
**Scope:** Universal  
**Earlier-list crosswalk:** #78, #79, #80  
**Canonical passages:** Matthew 24:42-44; 25:1-13; Mark 13:32-37; Luke 12:35-40; 21:34-36

#### Command-family wording

> Live ready for Christ's return without knowing the day or hour.

#### Scripture text (World English Bible)

##### Matthew 24:42-44

> **42** Watch therefore, for you don’t know in what hour your Lord comes.
>
> **43** But know this, that if the master of the house had known in what watch of the night the thief was coming, he would have watched, and would not have allowed his house to be broken into.
>
> **44** Therefore also be ready, for in an hour that you don’t expect, the Son of Man will come.

##### Matthew 25:1-13

> **1** “Then the Kingdom of Heaven will be like ten virgins who took their lamps and went out to meet the bridegroom.
>
> **2** Five of them were foolish, and five were wise.
>
> **3** Those who were foolish, when they took their lamps, took no oil with them,
>
> **4** but the wise took oil in their vessels with their lamps.
>
> **5** Now while the bridegroom delayed, they all slumbered and slept.
>
> **6** But at midnight there was a cry, ‘Behold! The bridegroom is coming! Come out to meet him!’
>
> **7** Then all those virgins arose, and trimmed their lamps.
>
> **8** The foolish said to the wise, ‘Give us some of your oil, for our lamps are going out.’
>
> **9** But the wise answered, saying, ‘What if there isn’t enough for us and you? You go rather to those who sell, and buy for yourselves.’
>
> **10** While they went away to buy, the bridegroom came, and those who were ready went in with him to the wedding feast, and the door was shut.
>
> **11** Afterward the other virgins also came, saying, ‘Lord, Lord, open to us.’
>
> **12** But he answered, ‘Most certainly I tell you, I don’t know you.’
>
> **13** Watch therefore, for you don’t know the day nor the hour in which the Son of Man is coming.

##### Mark 13:32-37

> **32** “But of that day or that hour no one knows-not even the angels in heaven, nor the Son, but only the Father.
>
> **33** Watch, keep alert, and pray; for you don’t know when the time is.
>
> **34** “It is like a man traveling to another country, having left his house and given authority to his servants, and to each one his work, and also commanded the doorkeeper to keep watch.
>
> **35** Watch therefore, for you don’t know when the lord of the house is coming-whether at evening, or at midnight, or when the rooster crows, or in the morning;
>
> **36** lest, coming suddenly, he might find you sleeping.
>
> **37** What I tell you, I tell all: Watch!”

##### Luke 12:35-40

> **35** “Let your waist be dressed and your lamps burning.
>
> **36** Be like men watching for their lord when he returns from the wedding feast, that when he comes and knocks, they may immediately open to him.
>
> **37** Blessed are those servants whom the lord will find watching when he comes. Most certainly I tell you that he will dress himself, make them recline, and will come and serve them.
>
> **38** They will be blessed if he comes in the second or third watch and finds them so.
>
> **39** But know this, that if the master of the house had known in what hour the thief was coming, he would have watched and not allowed his house to be broken into.
>
> **40** Therefore be ready also, for the Son of Man is coming in an hour that you don’t expect him.”

##### Luke 21:34-36

> **34** “So be careful, or your hearts will be loaded down with carousing, drunkenness, and cares of this life, and that day will come on you suddenly.
>
> **35** For it will come like a snare on all those who dwell on the surface of all the earth.
>
> **36** Therefore be watchful all the time, praying that you may be counted worthy to escape all these things that will happen, and to stand before the Son of Man.”

#### Reading the passages together

- Readiness is persevering faith that receives Christ's gifts and serves in one's calling. Jesus expressly excludes date-setting and complacency alike.

#### Law, Gospel, and confessional synthesis

Readiness is persevering faith that receives Christ's gifts and serves in one's calling. Jesus expressly excludes date-setting and complacency alike. In a confessional Lutheran reading, this command is not a ladder by which sinners earn justification. The Law exposes sin and directs the baptized life; the Gospel forgives, creates faith, joins the believer to Christ, and brings forth the fruits named here. The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

#### Early Christian witnesses

**Didache 16.1 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Watch for your life's sake. Let not your lamps be quenched, nor your loins unloosed; but be ready, for you do not know the hour in which our Lord comes.

*Study significance:* The passage directly preserves the church's early reception of Jesus' readiness language.

**Justin Martyr, First Apology 67 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> On the day called Sunday, all who live in cities or in the country gather together to one place, and the memoirs of the apostles or the writings of the prophets are read as long as time permits.

*Study significance:* The church gathers around apostolic and prophetic Scripture rather than private inspiration alone.

#### Lutheran confessional witnesses

**Augsburg Confession XII**

> Properly speaking, repentance consists of these two parts: one is contrition, that is, terrors smiting the conscience through the knowledge of sin; the other is faith, born of the Gospel or of absolution, which believes that for Christ's sake sins are forgiven, comforts the conscience, and delivers it from terrors.

*Study significance:* Repentance includes both truthful sorrow over sin and faith in Christ's forgiveness.

**Augsburg Confession IV**

> People cannot be justified before God by their own strength, merits, or works, but are freely justified for Christ's sake, through faith, when they believe that they are received into favor and that their sins are forgiven for Christ's sake.

*Study significance:* The command must never be turned into a competing basis of justification.

**Further confessional anchors:** Augsburg Confession XVII (Christ's return and final judgment); Small Catechism: Second Article of the Creed; Formula of Concord, Solid Declaration XI (election in Christ); Large Catechism: Creed and daily repentance.

#### Practicing the command

Remain in worship and absolution, confess sins promptly, keep promises, care for those entrusted to you, and pray for Christ's coming.

#### Misuse caution

Do not replace watchfulness with end-times calculations, conspiracy fixation, panic, or abandonment of vocation.

#### Questions for examination and meditation

1. What exactly does Jesus command, forbid, promise, or warn in these passages, and who first receives the words?
2. Which words expose sin or false trust, and which words direct you to Christ's gift, forgiveness, or sustaining promise?
3. What concrete act of repentance, faith, mercy, or vocation would embody **stay awake, keep watch, and be ready for the son of man** this week without turning obedience into a basis of justification?

#### Prayer

Lord Jesus Christ, You call Your people to stay awake, keep watch, and be ready for the son of man. Forgive my failure, keep me in Your Word and gifts, and bear this fruit in me for the good of my neighbor and the glory of the Father. Amen.

### 59. Hear attentively and take care how you hear

**Corpus:** Gospels  
**Scope:** Universal  
**Earlier-list crosswalk:** #81  
**Canonical passages:** Matthew 11:15; 13:9,43; Mark 4:9,23-25; Luke 8:8,18; 14:35

#### Command-family wording

> Listen to Jesus' Word with receptive attention and responsibility.

#### Scripture text (World English Bible)

##### Matthew 11:15

> **15** He who has ears to hear, let him hear.

##### Matthew 13:9,43

> **9** He who has ears to hear, let him hear.”
>
> **43** Then the righteous will shine like the sun in the Kingdom of their Father. He who has ears to hear, let him hear.

##### Mark 4:9,23-25

> **9** He said, “Whoever has ears to hear, let him hear.”
>
> **23** If any man has ears to hear, let him hear.”
>
> **24** He said to them, “Take heed what you hear. With whatever measure you measure, it will be measured to you; and more will be given to you who hear.
>
> **25** For whoever has, to him more will be given; and he who doesn’t have, even that which he has will be taken away from him.”

##### Luke 8:8,18

> **8** Some fell into the good ground and grew and produced one hundred times as much fruit.” As he said these things, he called out, “He who has ears to hear, let him hear!”
>
> **18** Be careful therefore how you hear. For whoever has, to him will be given; and whoever doesn’t have, from him will be taken away even that which he thinks he has.”

##### Luke 14:35

> **35** It is fit neither for the soil nor for the manure pile. It is thrown out. He who has ears to hear, let him hear.”

#### Reading the passages together

- The repeated summons identifies hearing as more than sound reception: the Word exposes, gives, and bears fruit. What is heard must not be smothered by unbelief or distraction.

#### Law, Gospel, and confessional synthesis

The repeated summons identifies hearing as more than sound reception: the Word exposes, gives, and bears fruit. What is heard must not be smothered by unbelief or distraction. In a confessional Lutheran reading, this command is not a ladder by which sinners earn justification. The Law exposes sin and directs the baptized life; the Gospel forgives, creates faith, joins the believer to Christ, and brings forth the fruits named here. The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

#### Early Christian witnesses

**Justin Martyr, First Apology 67 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> On the day called Sunday, all who live in cities or in the country gather together to one place, and the memoirs of the apostles or the writings of the prophets are read as long as time permits.

*Study significance:* The church gathers around apostolic and prophetic Scripture rather than private inspiration alone.

**1 Clement 49.1 (Lightfoot translation, lightly modernized)**

> Let the one who has love in Christ keep the commandments of Christ.

*Study significance:* The sentence binds Christian love to Christ's actual commands rather than sentiment alone.

#### Lutheran confessional witnesses

**Augsburg Confession V**

> That we may obtain this faith, the Ministry of Teaching the Gospel and administering the Sacraments was instituted. Through the Word and Sacraments, as through instruments, the Holy Spirit is given, who works faith where and when it pleases God.

*Study significance:* Faith is created and sustained through Christ's appointed means rather than self-generated resolve.

**Augsburg Confession VI**

> This faith is bound to bring forth good fruits, and it is necessary to do good works commanded by God because of God's will; yet we should not rely on those works to merit justification before God.

*Study significance:* Good works are necessary fruits of faith, not merits that purchase God's favor.

**Further confessional anchors:** Augsburg Confession XVII (Christ's return and final judgment); Small Catechism: Second Article of the Creed; Formula of Concord, Solid Declaration XI (election in Christ); Large Catechism: Creed and daily repentance.

#### Practicing the command

Read passages in context, listen carefully in preaching, ask clarifying questions, meditate, and act on what the text plainly says.

#### Misuse caution

Private impressions, novelty, and selective quotation must not be equated with the apostolic Word.

#### Questions for examination and meditation

1. What exactly does Jesus command, forbid, promise, or warn in these passages, and who first receives the words?
2. Which words expose sin or false trust, and which words direct you to Christ's gift, forgiveness, or sustaining promise?
3. What concrete act of repentance, faith, mercy, or vocation would embody **hear attentively and take care how you hear** this week without turning obedience into a basis of justification?

## Kingdom Priorities and Public Witness

The kingdom orders possessions, public life, speech, and witness under God's reign.

<a id="command-060"></a>

### 60. Render to Caesar what is Caesar's and to God what is God's

**Corpus:** Gospels  
**Scope:** Universal with distinct civil and spiritual vocations  
**Earlier-list crosswalk:** Added by canonical audit  
**Canonical passages:** Matthew 22:15-22; Mark 12:13-17; Luke 20:20-26

#### Command-family wording

> Give civil authorities their due while giving God the worship and obedience that belong to Him alone.

#### Scripture text (World English Bible)

##### Matthew 22:15-22

> **15** Then the Pharisees went and took counsel how they might entrap him in his talk.
>
> **16** They sent their disciples to him, along with the Herodians, saying, “Teacher, we know that you are honest, and teach the way of God in truth, no matter whom you teach; for you aren’t partial to anyone.
>
> **17** Tell us therefore, what do you think? Is it lawful to pay taxes to Caesar, or not?”
>
> **18** But Jesus perceived their wickedness, and said, “Why do you test me, you hypocrites?
>
> **19** Show me the tax money.” They brought to him a denarius.
>
> **20** He asked them, “Whose is this image and inscription?”
>
> **21** They said to him, “Caesar’s.” Then he said to them, “Give therefore to Caesar the things that are Caesar’s, and to God the things that are God’s.”
>
> **22** When they heard it, they marveled, and left him and went away.

##### Mark 12:13-17

> **13** They sent some of the Pharisees and the Herodians to him, that they might trap him with words.
>
> **14** When they had come, they asked him, “Teacher, we know that you are honest, and don’t defer to anyone; for you aren’t partial to anyone, but truly teach the way of God. Is it lawful to pay taxes to Caesar, or not?
>
> **15** Shall we give, or shall we not give?” But he, knowing their hypocrisy, said to them, “Why do you test me? Bring me a denarius, that I may see it.”
>
> **16** They brought it. He said to them, “Whose is this image and inscription?” They said to him, “Caesar’s.”
>
> **17** Jesus answered them, “Give to Caesar the things that are Caesar’s, and to God the things that are God’s.” They marveled greatly at him.

##### Luke 20:20-26

> **20** They watched him and sent out spies, who pretended to be righteous, that they might trap him in something he said, so as to deliver him up to the power and authority of the governor.
>
> **21** They asked him, “Teacher, we know that you say and teach what is right, and aren’t partial to anyone, but truly teach the way of God.
>
> **22** Is it lawful for us to pay taxes to Caesar, or not?”
>
> **23** But he perceived their craftiness, and said to them, “Why do you test me?
>
> **24** Show me a denarius. Whose image and inscription are on it?” They answered, “Caesar’s.”
>
> **25** He said to them, “Then give to Caesar the things that are Caesar’s, and to God the things that are God’s.”
>
> **26** They weren’t able to trap him in his words before the people. They marveled at his answer and were silent.

#### Reading the passages together

- Jesus neither deifies the state nor abolishes civil order. Lutheran teaching distinguishes God's temporal governance from the Church's ministry without separating either from His authority.

#### Law, Gospel, and confessional synthesis

Jesus neither deifies the state nor abolishes civil order. Lutheran teaching distinguishes God's temporal governance from the Church's ministry without separating either from His authority. In a confessional Lutheran reading, this command is not a ladder by which sinners earn justification. The Law exposes sin and directs the baptized life; the Gospel forgives, creates faith, joins the believer to Christ, and brings forth the fruits named here. The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

#### Early Christian witnesses

**Justin Martyr, First Apology 17 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Everywhere we, more readily than all people, endeavor to pay to those appointed by you the taxes and assessments, as we have been taught by Him.

*Study significance:* Justin applies Jesus' teaching about Caesar to ordinary civic duties without making the state divine.

**1 Clement 49.1 (Lightfoot translation, lightly modernized)**

> Let the one who has love in Christ keep the commandments of Christ.

*Study significance:* The sentence binds Christian love to Christ's actual commands rather than sentiment alone.

#### Lutheran confessional witnesses

**Augsburg Confession XVI**

> Lawful civil ordinances are good works of God, and it is right for Christians to bear civil office, sit as judges, judge matters by existing laws, and engage in just wars.

*Study significance:* The confession receives civil vocation as a real sphere of neighbor-love under God.

**Small Catechism, First Commandment**

> We should fear, love, and trust in God above all things.

*Study significance:* Every command family is finally ordered under trust in the true God.

**Further confessional anchors:** Small Catechism: First Commandment and First/Second Petitions; Augsburg Confession XVI and XX (civil affairs and faith/good works); Apology XVI (political order and Christian freedom); Formula of Concord, Solid Declaration IV and VI.

#### Practicing the command

Pay lawful taxes, obey just laws, participate responsibly, honor public office, and refuse commands that require sin while accepting consequences faithfully.

#### Misuse caution

Do not baptize a political program as the kingdom of God or use spiritual language to evade public responsibility.

#### Questions for examination and meditation

1. What exactly does Jesus command, forbid, promise, or warn in these passages, and who first receives the words?
2. Which words expose sin or false trust, and which words direct you to Christ's gift, forgiveness, or sustaining promise?
3. What concrete act of repentance, faith, mercy, or vocation would embody **render to caesar what is caesar's and to god what is god's** this week without turning obedience into a basis of justification?

#### Prayer

Lord Jesus Christ, You call Your people to render to caesar what is caesar's and to god what is god's. Forgive my failure, keep me in Your Word and gifts, and bear this fruit in me for the good of my neighbor and the glory of the Father. Amen.

<a id="command-061"></a>

### 61. Have salt in yourselves and be at peace with one another

**Corpus:** Gospels  
**Scope:** Universal, especially within the community of disciples  
**Earlier-list crosswalk:** Added by canonical audit  
**Canonical passages:** Mark 9:49-50

#### Command-family wording

> Receive the costly distinctiveness of discipleship and pursue peace among Christ's people.

#### Scripture text (World English Bible)

##### Mark 9:49-50

> **49** For everyone will be salted with fire, and every sacrifice will be seasoned with salt.
>
> **50** Salt is good, but if the salt has lost its saltiness, with what will you season it? Have salt in yourselves, and be at peace with one another.”

#### Reading the passages together

- Salt evokes preservation, sacrifice, and covenant faithfulness; peace is not blandness but reconciled life shaped by Jesus' warning against causing others to stumble.

#### Law, Gospel, and confessional synthesis

Salt evokes preservation, sacrifice, and covenant faithfulness; peace is not blandness but reconciled life shaped by Jesus' warning against causing others to stumble. In a confessional Lutheran reading, this command is not a ladder by which sinners earn justification. The Law exposes sin and directs the baptized life; the Gospel forgives, creates faith, joins the believer to Christ, and brings forth the fruits named here. The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

#### Early Christian witnesses

**1 Clement 13.1-2 (Lightfoot/Roberts-Donaldson tradition, lightly modernized)**

> Let us be humble-minded, brethren, laying aside all haughtiness and pride and foolishness and angry feelings, especially remembering the words of the Lord Jesus: Be merciful, that you may obtain mercy; forgive, that it may be forgiven unto you.

*Study significance:* Clement explicitly uses Jesus' words to form a peaceful and humble congregation.

**Didache 2.4-5; 4.4 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> You shall not be double-minded nor double-tongued, for to be double-tongued is a snare of death. Your speech shall not be false or empty, but fulfilled by deed.

*Study significance:* The witness treats integrity as speech verified by action.

#### Lutheran confessional witnesses

**Augsburg Confession VI**

> This faith is bound to bring forth good fruits, and it is necessary to do good works commanded by God because of God's will; yet we should not rely on those works to merit justification before God.

*Study significance:* Good works are necessary fruits of faith, not merits that purchase God's favor.

**Small Catechism, Eighth Commandment**

> We should fear and love God that we may not deceitfully belie, betray, slander, or defame our neighbor, but defend him, speak well of him, and put the best construction on everything.

*Study significance:* Truthful correction and charitable interpretation belong together.

**Further confessional anchors:** Small Catechism: First Commandment and First/Second Petitions; Augsburg Confession XVI and XX (civil affairs and faith/good works); Apology XVI (political order and Christian freedom); Formula of Concord, Solid Declaration IV and VI.

#### Practicing the command

Welcome correction, restrain status rivalry, speak truth without contempt, reconcile quickly, and preserve the community's confession and care.

#### Misuse caution

Peace must not be purchased by sacrificing truth, safety, or accountability.

#### Questions for examination and meditation

1. What exactly does Jesus command, forbid, promise, or warn in these passages, and who first receives the words?
2. Which words expose sin or false trust, and which words direct you to Christ's gift, forgiveness, or sustaining promise?
3. What concrete act of repentance, faith, mercy, or vocation would embody **have salt in yourselves and be at peace with one another** this week without turning obedience into a basis of justification?

#### Prayer

Lord Jesus Christ, You call Your people to have salt in yourselves and be at peace with one another. Forgive my failure, keep me in Your Word and gifts, and bear this fruit in me for the good of my neighbor and the glory of the Father. Amen.

## Mission, Ministry, and Church

The risen Christ sends His Church through ordered vocations to proclaim, baptize, teach, forgive, and shepherd.

<a id="command-062"></a>

### 62. Be wise as serpents and innocent as doves

**Corpus:** Gospels  
**Scope:** Mission-specific wisdom with broad Christian application  
**Earlier-list crosswalk:** #82  
**Canonical passages:** Matthew 10:16

#### Command-family wording

> Join prudent awareness with moral innocence when sent among danger.

#### Scripture text (World English Bible)

##### Matthew 10:16

> **16** “Behold, I send you out as sheep among wolves. Therefore be wise as serpents and harmless as doves.

#### Reading the passages together

- Jesus rejects both gullibility and manipulative self-protection. The messenger may assess risk and act strategically without adopting the deceit or violence of persecutors.

#### Law, Gospel, and confessional synthesis

Jesus rejects both gullibility and manipulative self-protection. The messenger may assess risk and act strategically without adopting the deceit or violence of persecutors. In a confessional Lutheran reading, this command is not a ladder by which sinners earn justification. The Law exposes sin and directs the baptized life; the Gospel forgives, creates faith, joins the believer to Christ, and brings forth the fruits named here. The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

#### Early Christian witnesses

**Didache 11.1-2 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Whoever comes and teaches you all these things that have been said before, receive him. But if the teacher himself turns and teaches another doctrine to the destruction of this, do not hear him.

*Study significance:* Hospitality and openness are joined to doctrinal discernment.

**1 Clement 13.1-2 (Lightfoot/Roberts-Donaldson tradition, lightly modernized)**

> Let us be humble-minded, brethren, laying aside all haughtiness and pride and foolishness and angry feelings, especially remembering the words of the Lord Jesus: Be merciful, that you may obtain mercy; forgive, that it may be forgiven unto you.

*Study significance:* Clement explicitly uses Jesus' words to form a peaceful and humble congregation.

#### Lutheran confessional witnesses

**Augsburg Confession XVI**

> Lawful civil ordinances are good works of God, and it is right for Christians to bear civil office, sit as judges, judge matters by existing laws, and engage in just wars.

*Study significance:* The confession receives civil vocation as a real sphere of neighbor-love under God.

**Small Catechism, Eighth Commandment**

> We should fear and love God that we may not deceitfully belie, betray, slander, or defame our neighbor, but defend him, speak well of him, and put the best construction on everything.

*Study significance:* Truthful correction and charitable interpretation belong together.

**Further confessional anchors:** Augsburg Confession V, VII, IX, XIV, and XXVIII; Small Catechism: Baptism, Confession/Keys, and Sacrament of the Altar; Treatise on the Power and Primacy of the Pope; Apology XIII-XIV and XXVIII.

#### Practicing the command

Plan carefully, protect confidential information, verify claims, prepare lawful safeguards, speak truthfully, and avoid needless provocation.

#### Misuse caution

Prudence is neither cowardice nor permission to lie, exploit, or retaliate.

#### Questions for examination and meditation

1. What exactly does Jesus command, forbid, promise, or warn in these passages, and who first receives the words?
2. Which words expose sin or false trust, and which words direct you to Christ's gift, forgiveness, or sustaining promise?
3. What concrete act of repentance, faith, mercy, or vocation would embody **be wise as serpents and innocent as doves** this week without turning obedience into a basis of justification?

<a id="command-063"></a>

### 63. Proclaim the kingdom and carry out the mercy entrusted to Christ's messengers

**Corpus:** Gospels  
**Scope:** Particular apostolic and missionary commissions with continuing principles  
**Earlier-list crosswalk:** #83  
**Canonical passages:** Matthew 10:5-8; Luke 9:1-6; 10:1-9

#### Command-family wording

> Announce God's reign and serve suffering people under the authority Jesus actually gives.

#### Scripture text (World English Bible)

##### Matthew 10:5-8

> **5** Jesus sent these twelve out and commanded them, saying, “Don’t go among the Gentiles, and don’t enter into any city of the Samaritans.
>
> **6** Rather, go to the lost sheep of the house of Israel.
>
> **7** As you go, preach, saying, ‘The Kingdom of Heaven is at hand!’
>
> **8** Heal the sick, cleanse the lepers, and cast out demons. Freely you received, so freely give.

##### Luke 9:1-6

> **1** He called the twelve together and gave them power and authority over all demons, and to cure diseases.
>
> **2** He sent them out to preach God’s Kingdom and to heal the sick.
>
> **3** He said to them, “Take nothing for your journey-no staffs, nor wallet, nor bread, nor money. Don’t have two tunics each.
>
> **4** Into whatever house you enter, stay there, and depart from there.
>
> **5** As many as don’t receive you, when you depart from that city, shake off even the dust from your feet for a testimony against them.”
>
> **6** They departed and went throughout the villages, preaching the Good News and healing everywhere.

##### Luke 10:1-9

> **1** Now after these things, the Lord also appointed seventy others, and sent them two by two ahead of him into every city and place where he was about to come.
>
> **2** Then he said to them, “The harvest is indeed plentiful, but the laborers are few. Pray therefore to the Lord of the harvest, that he may send out laborers into his harvest.
>
> **3** Go your ways. Behold, I send you out as lambs among wolves.
>
> **4** Carry no purse, nor wallet, nor sandals. Greet no one on the way.
>
> **5** Into whatever house you enter, first say, ‘Peace be to this house.’
>
> **6** If a son of peace is there, your peace will rest on him; but if not, it will return to you.
>
> **7** Remain in that same house, eating and drinking the things they give, for the laborer is worthy of his wages. Don’t go from house to house.
>
> **8** Into whatever city you enter and they receive you, eat the things that are set before you.
>
> **9** Heal the sick who are there and tell them, ‘God’s Kingdom has come near to you.’

#### Reading the passages together

- These passages give particular disciples authority to heal and expel demons as signs of the kingdom. They do not promise identical extraordinary powers to every Christian or make miracles the ordinary means of grace.

#### Law, Gospel, and confessional synthesis

These passages give particular disciples authority to heal and expel demons as signs of the kingdom. They do not promise identical extraordinary powers to every Christian or make miracles the ordinary means of grace. In a confessional Lutheran reading, this command is not a ladder by which sinners earn justification. The Law exposes sin and directs the baptized life; the Gospel forgives, creates faith, joins the believer to Christ, and brings forth the fruits named here. The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

#### Early Christian witnesses

**Didache 15.1 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Appoint, therefore, for yourselves bishops and deacons worthy of the Lord, men who are meek, not lovers of money, truthful, and proven; for they also render to you the service of prophets and teachers.

*Study significance:* Mission and pastoral care are entrusted to tested servants rather than self-appointed status seekers.

**Justin Martyr, First Apology 67 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> On the day called Sunday, all who live in cities or in the country gather together to one place, and the memoirs of the apostles or the writings of the prophets are read as long as time permits.

*Study significance:* The church gathers around apostolic and prophetic Scripture rather than private inspiration alone.

#### Lutheran confessional witnesses

**Augsburg Confession V**

> That we may obtain this faith, the Ministry of Teaching the Gospel and administering the Sacraments was instituted. Through the Word and Sacraments, as through instruments, the Holy Spirit is given, who works faith where and when it pleases God.

*Study significance:* Faith is created and sustained through Christ's appointed means rather than self-generated resolve.

**Augsburg Confession XIV**

> No one should publicly teach in the Church or administer the Sacraments unless he be regularly called.

*Study significance:* Christ's mission is carried out through ordered vocation rather than self-authorization.

**Further confessional anchors:** Augsburg Confession V, VII, IX, XIV, and XXVIII; Small Catechism: Baptism, Confession/Keys, and Sacrament of the Altar; Treatise on the Power and Primacy of the Pope; Apology XIII-XIV and XXVIII.

#### Practicing the command

Pray for the sick, seek responsible medical care, visit and serve them, proclaim Christ, and handle alleged spiritual oppression pastorally and soberly.

#### Misuse caution

Do not commercialize miracles, blame sufferers for insufficient faith, discourage treatment, or imitate apostolic signs without warrant.

#### Questions for examination and meditation

1. What exactly does Jesus command, forbid, promise, or warn in these passages, and who first receives the words?
2. Which words expose sin or false trust, and which words direct you to Christ's gift, forgiveness, or sustaining promise?
3. What concrete act of repentance, faith, mercy, or vocation would embody **proclaim the kingdom and carry out the mercy entrusted to christ's messengers** this week without turning obedience into a basis of justification?

#### Prayer

Lord Jesus Christ, You call Your people to proclaim the kingdom and carry out the mercy entrusted to christ's messengers. Forgive my failure, keep me in Your Word and gifts, and bear this fruit in me for the good of my neighbor and the glory of the Father. Amen.

### 64. Freely give what you freely received

**Corpus:** Gospels  
**Scope:** Missionary principle

#### Scripture text (World English Bible)

##### Matthew 10:8

> **8** Heal the sick, cleanse the lepers, and cast out demons. Freely you received, so freely give.

#### Reading the passages together

- Grace is free because Christ has paid its cost. Ministers may receive support, but the Gospel, forgiveness, prayer, and access to pastoral care are not products sold to the desperate.

#### Law, Gospel, and confessional synthesis

Grace is free because Christ has paid its cost. Ministers may receive support, but the Gospel, forgiveness, prayer, and access to pastoral care are not products sold to the desperate. In a confessional Lutheran reading, this command is not a ladder by which sinners earn justification. The Law exposes sin and directs the baptized life; the Gospel forgives, creates faith, joins the believer to Christ, and brings forth the fruits named here. The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

#### Early Christian witnesses

**Didache 1.5 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Give to everyone who asks you, and ask it not back; for the Father wills that to all should be given of our own blessings.

**1 Clement 49.1 (Lightfoot translation, lightly modernized)**

> Let the one who has love in Christ keep the commandments of Christ.

**Augsburg Confession V**

> That we may obtain this faith, the Ministry of Teaching the Gospel and administering the Sacraments was instituted. Through the Word and Sacraments, as through instruments, the Holy Spirit is given, who works faith where and when it pleases God.

*Study significance:* Faith is created and sustained through Christ's appointed means rather than self-generated resolve.

**Augsburg Confession VI**

> This faith is bound to bring forth good fruits, and it is necessary to do good works commanded by God because of God's will; yet we should not rely on those works to merit justification before God.

*Study significance:* Good works are necessary fruits of faith, not merits that purchase God's favor.

Use transparent compensation, provide access regardless of wealth, give teaching and mercy generously, and reject pay-to-play spirituality.

> Do not oppose genuine service merely because it lies outside one's immediate circle.

#### Scripture text (World English Bible)

>
> **39** But Jesus said, “Don’t forbid him, for there is no one who will do a mighty work in my name and be able quickly to speak evil of me.

**Augsburg Confession VI**

> This faith is bound to bring forth good fruits, and it is necessary to do good works commanded by God because of God's will; yet we should not rely on those works to merit justification before God.

### 66. Make disciples of all nations by going, baptizing, and teaching

**Corpus:** Gospels  
**Scope:** Church-wide commission carried out through ordered vocations  
**Earlier-list crosswalk:** #86, #87, #88  
**Canonical passages:** Matthew 28:18-20; Mark 16:15-16 with textual note

#### Command-family wording

> Under the risen Lord's authority, bring the Gospel to all peoples, baptize in the triune name, and teach all He commanded.

#### Scripture text (World English Bible)

##### Matthew 28:18-20

> **18** Jesus came to them and spoke to them, saying, “All authority has been given to me in heaven and on earth.
>
> **19** Go and make disciples of all nations, baptizing them in the name of the Father and of the Son and of the Holy Spirit,
>
> **20** teaching them to observe all things that I commanded you. Behold, I am with you always, even to the end of the age.” Amen.

##### Mark 16:15-16

> **15** He said to them, “Go into all the world and preach the Good News to the whole creation.
>
> **16** He who believes and is baptized will be saved; but he who disbelieves will be condemned.

**Textual note: Mark 16:9-20 is absent from the two earliest complete Greek manuscripts and is marked in many modern editions. The commission to proclaim and baptize is independently attested in Matthew 28:18-20; no doctrine here should depend on Mark 16 alone.**

#### Reading the passages together

- Disciples are made through Christ's appointed means, not coercion or marketing. Baptism and continuing catechesis belong together under His promise to remain with the Church.

#### Law, Gospel, and confessional synthesis

Disciples are made through Christ's appointed means, not coercion or marketing. Baptism and continuing catechesis belong together under His promise to remain with the Church. In a confessional Lutheran reading, this command is not a ladder by which sinners earn justification. The Law exposes sin and directs the baptized life; the Gospel forgives, creates faith, joins the believer to Christ, and brings forth the fruits named here. The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

#### Early Christian witnesses

**Didache 7.1 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Concerning baptism, baptize thus: Having first rehearsed all these things, baptize in the name of the Father and of the Son and of the Holy Spirit, in living water.

*Study significance:* The early church explicitly connects catechesis, water, and the Triune name.

**Justin Martyr, First Apology 61 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Then they are brought by us where there is water and are regenerated in the same manner in which we were ourselves regenerated: in the name of God, the Father and Lord of the universe, and of our Savior Jesus Christ, and of the Holy Spirit.

*Study significance:* This second-century description witnesses to baptism with water and the Triune name.

#### Lutheran confessional witnesses

**Augsburg Confession V**

> That we may obtain this faith, the Ministry of Teaching the Gospel and administering the Sacraments was instituted. Through the Word and Sacraments, as through instruments, the Holy Spirit is given, who works faith where and when it pleases God.

*Study significance:* Faith is created and sustained through Christ's appointed means rather than self-generated resolve.

**Small Catechism, Baptism III**

> It is not the water indeed that does these things, but the word of God which is in and with the water, and faith which trusts this word of God in the water.

*Study significance:* Baptism is God's act through water joined to His word and promise.

**Further confessional anchors:** Augsburg Confession V, VII, IX, XIV, and XXVIII; Small Catechism: Baptism, Confession/Keys, and Sacrament of the Altar; Treatise on the Power and Primacy of the Pope; Apology XIII-XIV and XXVIII.

#### Practicing the command

Support called ministers and missionaries, welcome every ethnicity and class, bring children and adults to Baptism, teach the whole counsel of Christ, and live as witnesses in daily vocation.

#### Misuse caution

Do not separate evangelism from Baptism and teaching, practice coercion, or treat the textual status of Mark 16:9-20 as irrelevant.

#### Questions for examination and meditation

1. What exactly does Jesus command, forbid, promise, or warn in these passages, and who first receives the words?
2. Which words expose sin or false trust, and which words direct you to Christ's gift, forgiveness, or sustaining promise?
3. What concrete act of repentance, faith, mercy, or vocation would embody **make disciples of all nations by going, baptizing, and teaching** this week without turning obedience into a basis of justification?

#### Prayer

Lord Jesus Christ, You call Your people to make disciples of all nations by going, baptizing, and teaching. Forgive my failure, keep me in Your Word and gifts, and bear this fruit in me for the good of my neighbor and the glory of the Father. Amen.

<a id="command-067"></a>

### 67. Receive the Holy Spirit and exercise Christ's word of forgiveness

**Corpus:** Gospels  
**Scope:** Apostolic and churchly office with consolation for all believers  
**Earlier-list crosswalk:** #89  
**Canonical passages:** John 20:19-23

#### Command-family wording

> Receive the risen Christ's Spirit-giving commission concerning the forgiveness and retention of sins.

#### Scripture text (World English Bible)

##### John 20:19-23

> **19** When therefore it was evening on that day, the first day of the week, and when the doors were locked where the disciples were assembled, for fear of the Jews, Jesus came and stood in the middle and said to them, “Peace be to you.”
>
> **20** When he had said this, he showed them his hands and his side. The disciples therefore were glad when they saw the Lord.
>
> **21** Jesus therefore said to them again, “Peace be to you. As the Father has sent me, even so I send you.”
>
> **22** When he had said this, he breathed on them, and said to them, “Receive the Holy Spirit!
>
> **23** If you forgive anyone’s sins, they have been forgiven them. If you retain anyone’s sins, they have been retained.”

#### Reading the passages together

- The passage grounds the Church's Office of the Keys: Christ authorizes the public proclamation of forgiveness to the repentant and the withholding of forgiveness from the unrepentant according to His Word.

#### Law, Gospel, and confessional synthesis

The passage grounds the Church's Office of the Keys: Christ authorizes the public proclamation of forgiveness to the repentant and the withholding of forgiveness from the unrepentant according to His Word. In a confessional Lutheran reading, this command is not a ladder by which sinners earn justification. The Law exposes sin and directs the baptized life; the Gospel forgives, creates faith, joins the believer to Christ, and brings forth the fruits named here. The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

#### Early Christian witnesses

**Didache 4.14 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> In church you shall acknowledge your transgressions, and you shall not come near for your prayer with an evil conscience. This is the way of life.

*Study significance:* Repentance is concrete, ecclesial, and joined to prayer rather than reduced to vague regret.

**Ignatius, Smyrnaeans 8 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Wherever the bishop shall appear, there let the multitude also be; even as, wherever Jesus Christ is, there is the catholic Church.

*Study significance:* The statement reflects an early concern for visible, ordered communion centered on Christ.

#### Lutheran confessional witnesses

**Augsburg Confession V**

> That we may obtain this faith, the Ministry of Teaching the Gospel and administering the Sacraments was instituted. Through the Word and Sacraments, as through instruments, the Holy Spirit is given, who works faith where and when it pleases God.

*Study significance:* Faith is created and sustained through Christ's appointed means rather than self-generated resolve.

**Augsburg Confession XIV**

> No one should publicly teach in the Church or administer the Sacraments unless he be regularly called.

*Study significance:* Christ's mission is carried out through ordered vocation rather than self-authorization.

**Further confessional anchors:** Augsburg Confession V, VII, IX, XIV, and XXVIII; Small Catechism: Baptism, Confession/Keys, and Sacrament of the Altar; Treatise on the Power and Primacy of the Pope; Apology XIII-XIV and XXVIII.

#### Practicing the command

Receive individual absolution, support faithful pastors, speak the Gospel to burdened neighbors, and use church discipline for repentance and restoration.

#### Misuse caution

Retention is not a personal weapon, and absolution is not permission to conceal crimes or bypass safeguarding.

#### Questions for examination and meditation

1. What exactly does Jesus command, forbid, promise, or warn in these passages, and who first receives the words?
2. Which words expose sin or false trust, and which words direct you to Christ's gift, forgiveness, or sustaining promise?
3. What concrete act of repentance, faith, mercy, or vocation would embody **receive the holy spirit and exercise christ's word of forgiveness** this week without turning obedience into a basis of justification?

#### Prayer

Lord Jesus Christ, You call Your people to receive the holy spirit and exercise christ's word of forgiveness. Forgive my failure, keep me in Your Word and gifts, and bear this fruit in me for the good of my neighbor and the glory of the Father. Amen.

<a id="command-068"></a>

### 68. Feed and tend Christ's sheep

**Corpus:** Gospels  
**Scope:** Pastoral and apostolic charge with implications for all caregivers  
**Earlier-list crosswalk:** #90  
**Canonical passages:** John 21:15-19

#### Command-family wording

> Care for Christ's people by feeding, guarding, and tending those who belong to Him.

#### Scripture text (World English Bible)

##### John 21:15-19

> **15** So when they had eaten their breakfast, Jesus said to Simon Peter, “Simon, son of Jonah, do you love me more than these?” He said to him, “Yes, Lord; you know that I have affection for you.” He said to him, “Feed my lambs.”
>
> **16** He said to him again a second time, “Simon, son of Jonah, do you love me?” He said to him, “Yes, Lord; you know that I have affection for you.” He said to him, “Tend my sheep.”
>
> **17** He said to him the third time, “Simon, son of Jonah, do you have affection for me?” Peter was grieved because he asked him the third time, “Do you have affection for me?” He said to him, “Lord, you know everything. You know that I have affection for you.” Jesus said to him, “Feed my sheep.
>
> **18** Most certainly I tell you, when you were young, you dressed yourself and walked where you wanted to. But when you are old, you will stretch out your hands, and another will dress you and carry you where you don’t want to go.”
>
> **19** Now he said this, signifying by what kind of death he would glorify God. When he had said this, he said to him, “Follow me.”

#### Reading the passages together

- Jesus restores Peter and entrusts him with pastoral care. The sheep remain Christ's; ministry is accountable stewardship under the crucified and risen Shepherd.

#### Law, Gospel, and confessional synthesis

Jesus restores Peter and entrusts him with pastoral care. The sheep remain Christ's; ministry is accountable stewardship under the crucified and risen Shepherd. In a confessional Lutheran reading, this command is not a ladder by which sinners earn justification. The Law exposes sin and directs the baptized life; the Gospel forgives, creates faith, joins the believer to Christ, and brings forth the fruits named here. The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

#### Early Christian witnesses

**Didache 15.1 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Appoint, therefore, for yourselves bishops and deacons worthy of the Lord, men who are meek, not lovers of money, truthful, and proven; for they also render to you the service of prophets and teachers.

*Study significance:* Mission and pastoral care are entrusted to tested servants rather than self-appointed status seekers.

**Ignatius, Smyrnaeans 8 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Wherever the bishop shall appear, there let the multitude also be; even as, wherever Jesus Christ is, there is the catholic Church.

*Study significance:* The statement reflects an early concern for visible, ordered communion centered on Christ.

#### Lutheran confessional witnesses

**Augsburg Confession XIV**

> No one should publicly teach in the Church or administer the Sacraments unless he be regularly called.

*Study significance:* Christ's mission is carried out through ordered vocation rather than self-authorization.

**Augsburg Confession VI**

> This faith is bound to bring forth good fruits, and it is necessary to do good works commanded by God because of God's will; yet we should not rely on those works to merit justification before God.

*Study significance:* Good works are necessary fruits of faith, not merits that purchase God's favor.

**Further confessional anchors:** Augsburg Confession V, VII, IX, XIV, and XXVIII; Small Catechism: Baptism, Confession/Keys, and Sacrament of the Altar; Treatise on the Power and Primacy of the Pope; Apology XIII-XIV and XXVIII.

#### Practicing the command

Pastors preach the whole Word, administer the Sacraments, visit, teach, correct, and protect; congregations support and hold shepherds accountable.

#### Misuse caution

Pastoral authority is not ownership, domination, celebrity, or immunity from correction.

#### Questions for examination and meditation

1. What exactly does Jesus command, forbid, promise, or warn in these passages, and who first receives the words?
2. Which words expose sin or false trust, and which words direct you to Christ's gift, forgiveness, or sustaining promise?
3. What concrete act of repentance, faith, mercy, or vocation would embody **feed and tend christ's sheep** this week without turning obedience into a basis of justification?

#### Prayer

Lord Jesus Christ, You call Your people to feed and tend christ's sheep. Forgive my failure, keep me in Your Word and gifts, and bear this fruit in me for the good of my neighbor and the glory of the Father. Amen.

## Discipleship and Union with Christ

The disciple lives from Christ, follows His voice, and bears fruit through communion with Him.

<a id="command-069"></a>

### 69. Continue in Jesus' Word

**Corpus:** Gospels  
**Scope:** Universal  
**Earlier-list crosswalk:** #92  
**Canonical passages:** John 8:31-32

#### Command-family wording

> Remain in Jesus' teaching rather than treating initial interest as completed discipleship.

#### Scripture text (World English Bible)

##### John 8:31-32

> **31** Jesus therefore said to those Jews who had believed him, “If you remain in my word, then you are truly my disciples.
>
> **32** You will know the truth, and the truth will make you free.”

#### Reading the passages together

- The truth that frees is Christ's Word, which exposes slavery to sin and gives the Son's freedom. Perseverance is sustained by His promise and means of grace.

#### Law, Gospel, and confessional synthesis

The truth that frees is Christ's Word, which exposes slavery to sin and gives the Son's freedom. Perseverance is sustained by His promise and means of grace. In a confessional Lutheran reading, this command is not a ladder by which sinners earn justification. The Law exposes sin and directs the baptized life; the Gospel forgives, creates faith, joins the believer to Christ, and brings forth the fruits named here. The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

#### Early Christian witnesses

**Justin Martyr, First Apology 67 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> On the day called Sunday, all who live in cities or in the country gather together to one place, and the memoirs of the apostles or the writings of the prophets are read as long as time permits.

*Study significance:* The church gathers around apostolic and prophetic Scripture rather than private inspiration alone.

**Didache 1.1-2 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> There are two ways, one of life and one of death; but there is a great difference between these two ways. The way of life, then, is this: First, you shall love God who made you; second, your neighbor as yourself; and all things whatsoever you would not have done to you, do not do to another.

*Study significance:* An early Christian catechetical summary joins love of God, love of neighbor, and concrete conduct.

#### Lutheran confessional witnesses

**Augsburg Confession V**

> That we may obtain this faith, the Ministry of Teaching the Gospel and administering the Sacraments was instituted. Through the Word and Sacraments, as through instruments, the Holy Spirit is given, who works faith where and when it pleases God.

*Study significance:* Faith is created and sustained through Christ's appointed means rather than self-generated resolve.

**Augsburg Confession VI**

> This faith is bound to bring forth good fruits, and it is necessary to do good works commanded by God because of God's will; yet we should not rely on those works to merit justification before God.

*Study significance:* Good works are necessary fruits of faith, not merits that purchase God's favor.

**Further confessional anchors:** Augsburg Confession IV and VI (faith and new obedience); Apology of the Augsburg Confession IV (faith and fruits); Small Catechism: First Commandment and Baptism IV; Formula of Concord, Solid Declaration IV and VI (good works and the Law).

#### Practicing the command

Read Scripture regularly, remain in preaching and catechesis, confess the Creeds, test ideas by the text, and return after failure.

#### Misuse caution

Christian freedom is not autonomy from Christ's Word or the Church's need for teaching.

#### Questions for examination and meditation

1. What exactly does Jesus command, forbid, promise, or warn in these passages, and who first receives the words?
2. Which words expose sin or false trust, and which words direct you to Christ's gift, forgiveness, or sustaining promise?
3. What concrete act of repentance, faith, mercy, or vocation would embody **continue in jesus' word** this week without turning obedience into a basis of justification?

#### Prayer

Lord Jesus Christ, You call Your people to continue in jesus' word. Forgive my failure, keep me in Your Word and gifts, and bear this fruit in me for the good of my neighbor and the glory of the Father. Amen.

### 70. Hear Jesus' words and put them into practice

**Corpus:** Gospels  
**Scope:** Universal  
**Earlier-list crosswalk:** #93  
**Canonical passages:** Matthew 7:24-27; Mark 4:24; Luke 6:46-49; 8:18; 11:28; John 8:47; 10:27

#### Command-family wording

> Receive Jesus' Word in faith and let obedience follow in concrete life.

#### Scripture text (World English Bible)

##### Matthew 7:24-27

> **24** “Everyone therefore who hears these words of mine and does them, I will liken him to a wise man who built his house on the rock.
>
> **25** The rain came down, the floods came, and the winds blew and beat on that house; and it didn’t fall, for it was founded on the rock.
>
> **26** Everyone who hears these words of mine and doesn’t do them will be like a foolish man who built his house on the sand.
>
> **27** The rain came down, the floods came, and the winds blew and beat on that house; and it fell-and its fall was great.”

##### Mark 4:24

> **24** He said to them, “Take heed what you hear. With whatever measure you measure, it will be measured to you; and more will be given to you who hear.

##### Luke 6:46-49

> **46** “Why do you call me, ‘Lord, Lord,’ and don’t do the things which I say?
>
> **47** Everyone who comes to me, and hears my words and does them, I will show you who he is like.
>
> **48** He is like a man building a house, who dug and went deep and laid a foundation on the rock. When a flood arose, the stream broke against that house, and could not shake it, because it was founded on the rock.
>
> **49** But he who hears and doesn’t do, is like a man who built a house on the earth without a foundation, against which the stream broke, and immediately it fell; and the ruin of that house was great.”

##### Luke 8:18

> **18** Be careful therefore how you hear. For whoever has, to him will be given; and whoever doesn’t have, from him will be taken away even that which he thinks he has.”

##### Luke 11:28

> **28** But he said, “On the contrary, blessed are those who hear the word of God, and keep it.”

##### John 8:47

> **47** He who is of God hears the words of God. For this cause you don’t hear, because you are not of God.”

##### John 10:27

> **27** My sheep hear my voice, and I know them, and they follow me.

#### Reading the passages together

- Doing does not earn the foundation; it reveals living hearing. Christ's Word both judges false profession and creates the faithful life it commands.

#### Law, Gospel, and confessional synthesis

Doing does not earn the foundation; it reveals living hearing. Christ's Word both judges false profession and creates the faithful life it commands. In a confessional Lutheran reading, this command is not a ladder by which sinners earn justification. The Law exposes sin and directs the baptized life; the Gospel forgives, creates faith, joins the believer to Christ, and brings forth the fruits named here. The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

#### Early Christian witnesses

**Didache 1.1-2 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> There are two ways, one of life and one of death; but there is a great difference between these two ways. The way of life, then, is this: First, you shall love God who made you; second, your neighbor as yourself; and all things whatsoever you would not have done to you, do not do to another.

*Study significance:* An early Christian catechetical summary joins love of God, love of neighbor, and concrete conduct.

**1 Clement 49.1 (Lightfoot translation, lightly modernized)**

> Let the one who has love in Christ keep the commandments of Christ.

*Study significance:* The sentence binds Christian love to Christ's actual commands rather than sentiment alone.

#### Lutheran confessional witnesses

**Augsburg Confession V**

> That we may obtain this faith, the Ministry of Teaching the Gospel and administering the Sacraments was instituted. Through the Word and Sacraments, as through instruments, the Holy Spirit is given, who works faith where and when it pleases God.

*Study significance:* Faith is created and sustained through Christ's appointed means rather than self-generated resolve.

**Augsburg Confession VI**

> This faith is bound to bring forth good fruits, and it is necessary to do good works commanded by God because of God's will; yet we should not rely on those works to merit justification before God.

*Study significance:* Good works are necessary fruits of faith, not merits that purchase God's favor.

**Further confessional anchors:** Augsburg Confession IV and VI (faith and new obedience); Apology of the Augsburg Confession IV (faith and fruits); Small Catechism: First Commandment and Baptism IV; Formula of Concord, Solid Declaration IV and VI (good works and the Law).

#### Practicing the command

After hearing, name the specific neighbor and action involved, pray for help, act, confess failure, and begin again in forgiveness.

#### Misuse caution

Do not make obedience the basis of justification or reduce hearing to information without embodied fruit.

#### Questions for examination and meditation

1. What exactly does Jesus command, forbid, promise, or warn in these passages, and who first receives the words?
2. Which words expose sin or false trust, and which words direct you to Christ's gift, forgiveness, or sustaining promise?
3. What concrete act of repentance, faith, mercy, or vocation would embody **hear jesus' words and put them into practice** this week without turning obedience into a basis of justification?

#### Prayer

Lord Jesus Christ, You call Your people to hear jesus' words and put them into practice. Forgive my failure, keep me in Your Word and gifts, and bear this fruit in me for the good of my neighbor and the glory of the Father. Amen.
