## Revelation: Endurance and the Churches

The risen Lord addresses His churches with warning, promise, correction, and hope.

<a id="command-071"></a>

### 71. Do not fear; be faithful unto death

**Corpus:** Revelation  
**Scope:** Universal, especially under suffering  
**Earlier-list crosswalk:** Added by canonical audit  
**Canonical passages:** Revelation 1:17-18; 2:10

#### Command-family wording

> Do not fear the powers of death; remain faithful to Christ even to death.

#### Scripture text (World English Bible)

##### Revelation 1:17-18

> **17** When I saw him, I fell at his feet like a dead man. He laid his right hand on me, saying, “Don’t be afraid. I am the first and the last,
>
> **18** and the Living one. I was dead, and behold, I am alive forever and ever. Amen. I have the keys of Death and of Hades.

##### Revelation 2:10

> **10** Don’t be afraid of the things which you are about to suffer. Behold, the devil is about to throw some of you into prison, that you may be tested; and you will have oppression for ten days. Be faithful to death, and I will give you the crown of life.

#### Reading the passages together

- The command rests on the identity of the First and the Last, who died and lives and holds the keys of death and Hades. Endurance is sustained by the victorious Lamb. Read within the address to the seven churches and attend carefully to the identified speaker.

#### Law, Gospel, and confessional synthesis

The command rests on the identity of the First and the Last, who died and lives and holds the keys of death and Hades. Endurance is sustained by the victorious Lamb. In a confessional Lutheran reading, this command is not a ladder by which sinners earn justification. The Law exposes sin and directs the baptized life; the Gospel forgives, creates faith, joins the believer to Christ, and brings forth the fruits named here. The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

#### Early Christian witnesses

**Didache 16.1 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Watch for your life's sake. Let not your lamps be quenched, nor your loins unloosed; but be ready, for you do not know the hour in which our Lord comes.

*Study significance:* The passage directly preserves the church's early reception of Jesus' readiness language.

**1 Clement 49.1 (Lightfoot translation, lightly modernized)**

> Let the one who has love in Christ keep the commandments of Christ.

*Study significance:* The sentence binds Christian love to Christ's actual commands rather than sentiment alone.

#### Lutheran confessional witnesses

**Augsburg Confession IV**

> People cannot be justified before God by their own strength, merits, or works, but are freely justified for Christ's sake, through faith, when they believe that they are received into favor and that their sins are forgiven for Christ's sake.

*Study significance:* The command must never be turned into a competing basis of justification.

**Augsburg Confession VI**

> This faith is bound to bring forth good fruits, and it is necessary to do good works commanded by God because of God's will; yet we should not rely on those works to merit justification before God.

*Study significance:* Good works are necessary fruits of faith, not merits that purchase God's favor.

**Further confessional anchors:** Augsburg Confession XVII (Christ's return and judgment); Small Catechism: Second and Third Articles of the Creed; Formula of Concord, Solid Declaration V, VI, and XI; Large Catechism: First Commandment and Creed.

#### Practicing the command

Confess Christ under pressure, support imprisoned and persecuted believers, prepare spiritually for suffering, and remember the resurrection.

#### Misuse caution

This is not a command to seek martyrdom, refuse lawful escape, or remain in preventable abuse.

#### Questions for examination and meditation

1. What exactly does Jesus command, forbid, promise, or warn in these passages, and who first receives the words?
2. Which words expose sin or false trust, and which words direct you to Christ's gift, forgiveness, or sustaining promise?
3. What concrete act of repentance, faith, mercy, or vocation would embody **do not fear; be faithful unto death** this week without turning obedience into a basis of justification?

#### Prayer

Lord Jesus Christ, You call Your people to do not fear; be faithful unto death. Forgive my failure, keep me in Your Word and gifts, and bear this fruit in me for the good of my neighbor and the glory of the Father. Amen.

>

>

>
> **14** “To the angel of the assembly in Laodicea write: “The Amen, the Faithful and True Witness, the Beginning of God’s creation, says these things:

The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

> Appoint, therefore, for yourselves bishops and deacons worthy of the Lord, men who are meek, not lovers of money, truthful, and proven; for they also render to you the service of prophets and teachers.

*Study significance:* Mission and pastoral care are entrusted to tested servants rather than self-appointed status seekers.

#### Lutheran confessional witnesses

**Augsburg Confession V**

> That we may obtain this faith, the Ministry of Teaching the Gospel and administering the Sacraments was instituted. Through the Word and Sacraments, as through instruments, the Holy Spirit is given, who works faith where and when it pleases God.

*Study significance:* Faith is created and sustained through Christ's appointed means rather than self-generated resolve.

**Augsburg Confession XIV**

> No one should publicly teach in the Church or administer the Sacraments unless he be regularly called.

#### Practicing the command

Read Revelation aloud with the rest of Scripture, preserve its words, teach it in its canonical context, and resist sensational rewriting.

### 73. Remember where you have fallen and what you received

**Corpus:** Revelation  
**Scope:** Universal through the churches  
**Earlier-list crosswalk:** Added by canonical audit  
**Canonical passages:** Revelation 2:5; 3:3

#### Command-family wording

> Remember the gifts and teaching once received, and recognize departure from them.

#### Scripture text (World English Bible)

##### Revelation 2:5

> **5** Remember therefore from where you have fallen, and repent and do the first works; or else I am coming to you swiftly, and will move your lamp stand out of its place, unless you repent.

##### Revelation 3:3

> **3** Remember therefore how you have received and heard. Keep it and repent. If therefore you won’t watch, I will come as a thief, and you won’t know what hour I will come upon you.

#### Reading the passages together

- Christ's call to remember is not nostalgia; memory serves repentance by bringing the congregation back under the apostolic Gospel and its first works. Read within the address to the seven churches and attend carefully to the identified speaker.

#### Law, Gospel, and confessional synthesis

Christ's call to remember is not nostalgia; memory serves repentance by bringing the congregation back under the apostolic Gospel and its first works. In a confessional Lutheran reading, this command is not a ladder by which sinners earn justification. The Law exposes sin and directs the baptized life; the Gospel forgives, creates faith, joins the believer to Christ, and brings forth the fruits named here. The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

#### Early Christian witnesses

**Didache 4.14 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> In church you shall acknowledge your transgressions, and you shall not come near for your prayer with an evil conscience. This is the way of life.

*Study significance:* Repentance is concrete, ecclesial, and joined to prayer rather than reduced to vague regret.

**Didache 16.1 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Watch for your life's sake. Let not your lamps be quenched, nor your loins unloosed; but be ready, for you do not know the hour in which our Lord comes.

*Study significance:* The passage directly preserves the church's early reception of Jesus' readiness language.

#### Lutheran confessional witnesses

**Augsburg Confession XII**

> Properly speaking, repentance consists of these two parts: one is contrition, that is, terrors smiting the conscience through the knowledge of sin; the other is faith, born of the Gospel or of absolution, which believes that for Christ's sake sins are forgiven, comforts the conscience, and delivers it from terrors.

*Study significance:* Repentance includes both truthful sorrow over sin and faith in Christ's forgiveness.

**Augsburg Confession IV**

> People cannot be justified before God by their own strength, merits, or works, but are freely justified for Christ's sake, through faith, when they believe that they are received into favor and that their sins are forgiven for Christ's sake.

*Study significance:* The command must never be turned into a competing basis of justification.

**Further confessional anchors:** Augsburg Confession XVII (Christ's return and judgment); Small Catechism: Second and Third Articles of the Creed; Formula of Concord, Solid Declaration V, VI, and XI; Large Catechism: First Commandment and Creed.

#### Practicing the command

Review Scripture and catechesis, identify where love or doctrine cooled, confess institutional drift, and recover neglected practices that serve faith and love.

#### Misuse caution

Memory must not canonize every past custom or become resistance to faithful reform.

#### Questions for examination and meditation

1. What exactly does Jesus command, forbid, promise, or warn in these passages, and who first receives the words?
2. Which words expose sin or false trust, and which words direct you to Christ's gift, forgiveness, or sustaining promise?
3. What concrete act of repentance, faith, mercy, or vocation would embody **remember where you have fallen and what you received** this week without turning obedience into a basis of justification?

#### Prayer

Lord Jesus Christ, You call Your people to remember where you have fallen and what you received. Forgive my failure, keep me in Your Word and gifts, and bear this fruit in me for the good of my neighbor and the glory of the Father. Amen.

<a id="command-074"></a>

### 74. Repent of tolerated falsehood, immorality, deadness, and lukewarmness

**Corpus:** Revelation  
**Scope:** Universal through the churches  
**Earlier-list crosswalk:** Added by canonical audit  
**Canonical passages:** Revelation 2:5,16,21-22; 3:3,19

#### Command-family wording

> Turn from the specific sins Christ exposes in individuals and congregations.

#### Scripture text (World English Bible)

##### Revelation 2:5,16,21-22

> **5** Remember therefore from where you have fallen, and repent and do the first works; or else I am coming to you swiftly, and will move your lamp stand out of its place, unless you repent.
>
> **16** Repent therefore, or else I am coming to you quickly and I will make war against them with the sword of my mouth.
>
> **21** I gave her time to repent, but she refuses to repent of her sexual immorality.
>
> **22** Behold, I will throw her and those who commit adultery with her into a bed of great oppression, unless they repent of her works.

##### Revelation 3:3,19

> **3** Remember therefore how you have received and heard. Keep it and repent. If therefore you won’t watch, I will come as a thief, and you won’t know what hour I will come upon you.
>
> **19** As many as I love, I reprove and chasten. Be zealous therefore, and repent.

#### Reading the passages together

- The risen Lord's rebukes are Law spoken by the One who loves His churches. Repentance receives His mercy and therefore refuses both doctrinal indifference and moral accommodation. Read within the address to the seven churches and attend carefully to the identified speaker.

#### Law, Gospel, and confessional synthesis

The risen Lord's rebukes are Law spoken by the One who loves His churches. Repentance receives His mercy and therefore refuses both doctrinal indifference and moral accommodation. In a confessional Lutheran reading, this command is not a ladder by which sinners earn justification. The Law exposes sin and directs the baptized life; the Gospel forgives, creates faith, joins the believer to Christ, and brings forth the fruits named here. The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

#### Early Christian witnesses

**Didache 2.2 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> You shall not commit murder; you shall not commit adultery; you shall not commit sexual immorality; you shall not steal; you shall not practice magic or witchcraft; you shall not murder a child by abortion nor kill that which is born.

*Study significance:* This early catechesis applies the way of life to the protection of bodies, marriage, property, and children.

**Didache 4.14 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> In church you shall acknowledge your transgressions, and you shall not come near for your prayer with an evil conscience. This is the way of life.

*Study significance:* Repentance is concrete, ecclesial, and joined to prayer rather than reduced to vague regret.

#### Lutheran confessional witnesses

**Augsburg Confession XII**

> Properly speaking, repentance consists of these two parts: one is contrition, that is, terrors smiting the conscience through the knowledge of sin; the other is faith, born of the Gospel or of absolution, which believes that for Christ's sake sins are forgiven, comforts the conscience, and delivers it from terrors.

*Study significance:* Repentance includes both truthful sorrow over sin and faith in Christ's forgiveness.

**Augsburg Confession VI**

> This faith is bound to bring forth good fruits, and it is necessary to do good works commanded by God because of God's will; yet we should not rely on those works to merit justification before God.

*Study significance:* Good works are necessary fruits of faith, not merits that purchase God's favor.

**Further confessional anchors:** Augsburg Confession XVII (Christ's return and judgment); Small Catechism: Second and Third Articles of the Creed; Formula of Concord, Solid Declaration V, VI, and XI; Large Catechism: First Commandment and Creed.

#### Practicing the command

Name the teaching or conduct at issue, use church discipline lawfully, protect those harmed, seek absolution, and make verifiable changes.

#### Misuse caution

Do not generalize calls to repentance so vaguely that leaders or institutions avoid concrete accountability.

#### Questions for examination and meditation

1. What exactly does Jesus command, forbid, promise, or warn in these passages, and who first receives the words?
2. Which words expose sin or false trust, and which words direct you to Christ's gift, forgiveness, or sustaining promise?
3. What concrete act of repentance, faith, mercy, or vocation would embody **repent of tolerated falsehood, immorality, deadness, and lukewarmness** this week without turning obedience into a basis of justification?

#### Prayer

Lord Jesus Christ, You call Your people to repent of tolerated falsehood, immorality, deadness, and lukewarmness. Forgive my failure, keep me in Your Word and gifts, and bear this fruit in me for the good of my neighbor and the glory of the Father. Amen.

### 75. Return to the works that flow from your first love

**Corpus:** Revelation  
**Scope:** Universal through the churches  
**Earlier-list crosswalk:** Added by canonical audit  
**Canonical passages:** Revelation 2:4-5

#### Command-family wording

> Recover the love and resulting works abandoned by the church at Ephesus.

#### Scripture text (World English Bible)

##### Revelation 2:4-5

> **4** But I have this against you, that you left your first love.
>
> **5** Remember therefore from where you have fallen, and repent and do the first works; or else I am coming to you swiftly, and will move your lamp stand out of its place, unless you repent.

#### Reading the passages together

- Orthodox testing without love is not the fullness of faithful life. Christ calls His Church back to love that acts, while keeping its resistance to false apostles. Read within the address to the seven churches and attend carefully to the identified speaker.

#### Law, Gospel, and confessional synthesis

Orthodox testing without love is not the fullness of faithful life. Christ calls His Church back to love that acts, while keeping its resistance to false apostles. In a confessional Lutheran reading, this command is not a ladder by which sinners earn justification. The Law exposes sin and directs the baptized life; the Gospel forgives, creates faith, joins the believer to Christ, and brings forth the fruits named here. The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

#### Early Christian witnesses

**1 Clement 49.1 (Lightfoot translation, lightly modernized)**

> Let the one who has love in Christ keep the commandments of Christ.

*Study significance:* The sentence binds Christian love to Christ's actual commands rather than sentiment alone.

**Didache 1.1-2 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> There are two ways, one of life and one of death; but there is a great difference between these two ways. The way of life, then, is this: First, you shall love God who made you; second, your neighbor as yourself; and all things whatsoever you would not have done to you, do not do to another.

*Study significance:* An early Christian catechetical summary joins love of God, love of neighbor, and concrete conduct.

#### Lutheran confessional witnesses

**Augsburg Confession VI**

> This faith is bound to bring forth good fruits, and it is necessary to do good works commanded by God because of God's will; yet we should not rely on those works to merit justification before God.

*Study significance:* Good works are necessary fruits of faith, not merits that purchase God's favor.

**Augsburg Confession IV**

> People cannot be justified before God by their own strength, merits, or works, but are freely justified for Christ's sake, through faith, when they believe that they are received into favor and that their sins are forgiven for Christ's sake.

*Study significance:* The command must never be turned into a competing basis of justification.

**Further confessional anchors:** Augsburg Confession XVII (Christ's return and judgment); Small Catechism: Second and Third Articles of the Creed; Formula of Concord, Solid Declaration V, VI, and XI; Large Catechism: First Commandment and Creed.

#### Practicing the command

Restore prayer, mercy, hospitality, patient teaching, reconciliation, and care for the weak without surrendering doctrinal truth.

#### Misuse caution

Do not pit love against doctrine or confuse emotional intensity with the first works Jesus names.

#### Questions for examination and meditation

2. Which words expose sin or false trust, and which words direct you to Christ's gift, forgiveness, or sustaining promise?
3. What concrete act of repentance, faith, mercy, or vocation would embody **return to the works that flow from your first love** this week without turning obedience into a basis of justification?

### 76. Hear what the Spirit says to the churches

**Corpus:** Revelation  
**Scope:** Universal  
**Earlier-list crosswalk:** Added by canonical audit  
**Canonical passages:** Revelation 2:7,11,17,29; 3:6,13,22

#### Command-family wording

> Hear and receive the Spirit's message in Christ's words to all the churches.

#### Scripture text (World English Bible)

##### Revelation 2:7,11,17,29

> **7** He who has an ear, let him hear what the Spirit says to the assemblies. To him who overcomes I will give to eat from the tree of life, which is in the Paradise of my God.
>
> **11** He who has an ear, let him hear what the Spirit says to the assemblies. He who overcomes won’t be harmed by the second death.
>
> **17** He who has an ear, let him hear what the Spirit says to the assemblies. To him who overcomes, to him I will give of the hidden manna, and I will give him a white stone, and on the stone a new name written which no one knows but he who receives it.
>
> **29** He who has an ear, let him hear what the Spirit says to the assemblies.

##### Revelation 3:6,13,22

> **6** He who has an ear, let him hear what the Spirit says to the assemblies.
>
> **13** He who has an ear, let him hear what the Spirit says to the assemblies.
>
> **22** He who has an ear, let him hear what the Spirit says to the assemblies.”

#### Reading the passages together

- The refrain makes each local letter a word for the whole Church. The Spirit does not bypass Christ's testimony but speaks through it. Read within the address to the seven churches and attend carefully to the identified speaker.

#### Law, Gospel, and confessional synthesis

The refrain makes each local letter a word for the whole Church. The Spirit does not bypass Christ's testimony but speaks through it. In a confessional Lutheran reading, this command is not a ladder by which sinners earn justification. The Law exposes sin and directs the baptized life; the Gospel forgives, creates faith, joins the believer to Christ, and brings forth the fruits named here. The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

#### Early Christian witnesses

**Justin Martyr, First Apology 67 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> On the day called Sunday, all who live in cities or in the country gather together to one place, and the memoirs of the apostles or the writings of the prophets are read as long as time permits.

*Study significance:* The church gathers around apostolic and prophetic Scripture rather than private inspiration alone.

**Didache 16.1 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Watch for your life's sake. Let not your lamps be quenched, nor your loins unloosed; but be ready, for you do not know the hour in which our Lord comes.

*Study significance:* The passage directly preserves the church's early reception of Jesus' readiness language.

#### Lutheran confessional witnesses

**Augsburg Confession V**

> That we may obtain this faith, the Ministry of Teaching the Gospel and administering the Sacraments was instituted. Through the Word and Sacraments, as through instruments, the Holy Spirit is given, who works faith where and when it pleases God.

*Study significance:* Faith is created and sustained through Christ's appointed means rather than self-generated resolve.

**Augsburg Confession VI**

> This faith is bound to bring forth good fruits, and it is necessary to do good works commanded by God because of God's will; yet we should not rely on those works to merit justification before God.

*Study significance:* Good works are necessary fruits of faith, not merits that purchase God's favor.

**Further confessional anchors:** Augsburg Confession XVII (Christ's return and judgment); Small Catechism: Second and Third Articles of the Creed; Formula of Concord, Solid Declaration V, VI, and XI; Large Catechism: First Commandment and Creed.

#### Practicing the command

Read every letter, identify both warning and promise, discuss it congregationally, and let the canonical Word examine present practice.

#### Misuse caution

Do not use the refrain to elevate private impressions above Scripture or to apply only the rebukes meant for others.

#### Questions for examination and meditation

1. What exactly does Jesus command, forbid, promise, or warn in these passages, and who first receives the words?
2. Which words expose sin or false trust, and which words direct you to Christ's gift, forgiveness, or sustaining promise?
3. What concrete act of repentance, faith, mercy, or vocation would embody **hear what the spirit says to the churches** this week without turning obedience into a basis of justification?

#### Prayer

Lord Jesus Christ, You call Your people to hear what the spirit says to the churches. Forgive my failure, keep me in Your Word and gifts, and bear this fruit in me for the good of my neighbor and the glory of the Father. Amen.

### 80. Buy from Christ true riches, white garments, and salve

**Corpus:** Revelation  
**Scope:** Universal through the churches  
**Earlier-list crosswalk:** Added by canonical audit  
**Canonical passages:** Revelation 3:17-18

#### Command-family wording

> Recognize spiritual poverty and receive from Christ the riches, righteousness, and sight only He supplies.

#### Scripture text (World English Bible)

##### Revelation 3:17-18

> **17** Because you say, ‘I am rich, and have gotten riches, and have need of nothing,’ and don’t know that you are the wretched one, miserable, poor, blind, and naked;
>
> **18** I counsel you to buy from me gold refined by fire, that you may become rich; and white garments, that you may clothe yourself, and that the shame of your nakedness may not be revealed; and eye salve to anoint your eyes, that you may see.

#### Reading the passages together

- The paradoxical command to buy from Christ exposes self-sufficiency: the needed goods are His gifts, received from the One who counsels the destitute. Read within the address to the seven churches and attend carefully to the identified speaker.

#### Law, Gospel, and confessional synthesis

The paradoxical command to buy from Christ exposes self-sufficiency: the needed goods are His gifts, received from the One who counsels the destitute. In a confessional Lutheran reading, this command is not a ladder by which sinners earn justification. The Law exposes sin and directs the baptized life; the Gospel forgives, creates faith, joins the believer to Christ, and brings forth the fruits named here. The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

#### Early Christian witnesses

**Augustine, Confessions 10.29 (Pusey translation, lightly modernized)**

> Give what You command, and command what You will.

*Study significance:* Augustine confesses that divine commands expose need and that God must supply the grace by which they are obeyed.

**Didache 1.5 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Give to everyone who asks you, and ask it not back; for the Father wills that to all should be given of our own blessings.

*Study significance:* The command is received as real generosity flowing from the Father's gifts.

#### Lutheran confessional witnesses

**Augsburg Confession IV**

> People cannot be justified before God by their own strength, merits, or works, but are freely justified for Christ's sake, through faith, when they believe that they are received into favor and that their sins are forgiven for Christ's sake.

*Study significance:* The command must never be turned into a competing basis of justification.

**Large Catechism, First Commandment**

> A god means that from which we are to expect all good and to which we are to take refuge in all distress.

*Study significance:* This exposes whatever receives the trust, fear, or refuge that belongs to God.

**Further confessional anchors:** Augsburg Confession XVII (Christ's return and judgment); Small Catechism: Second and Third Articles of the Creed; Formula of Concord, Solid Declaration V, VI, and XI; Large Catechism: First Commandment and Creed.

#### Practicing the command

Confess need, return to Gospel and Sacraments, ask trusted Christians to expose blind spots, and use material wealth for mercy rather than identity.

#### Misuse caution

Do not turn the imagery into a commercial transaction, prosperity promise, or secret spiritual product.

#### Questions for examination and meditation

1. What exactly does Jesus command, forbid, promise, or warn in these passages, and who first receives the words?
2. Which words expose sin or false trust, and which words direct you to Christ's gift, forgiveness, or sustaining promise?
3. What concrete act of repentance, faith, mercy, or vocation would embody **buy from christ true riches, white garments, and salve** this week without turning obedience into a basis of justification?

#### Prayer

Lord Jesus Christ, You call Your people to buy from christ true riches, white garments, and salve. Forgive my failure, keep me in Your Word and gifts, and bear this fruit in me for the good of my neighbor and the glory of the Father. Amen.

<a id="command-081"></a>

### 81. Be zealous in response to Christ's loving discipline

**Corpus:** Revelation  
**Scope:** Universal  
**Earlier-list crosswalk:** Added by canonical audit  
**Canonical passages:** Revelation 3:19

#### Command-family wording

> Respond earnestly to Christ's rebuke and repent.

#### Scripture text (World English Bible)

##### Revelation 3:19

> **19** As many as I love, I reprove and chasten. Be zealous therefore, and repent.

#### Reading the passages together

- His discipline proceeds from love, not rejection. Zeal is the serious, prompt fruit of being addressed by the faithful Witness. Read within the address to the seven churches and attend carefully to the identified speaker.

#### Law, Gospel, and confessional synthesis

His discipline proceeds from love, not rejection. Zeal is the serious, prompt fruit of being addressed by the faithful Witness. In a confessional Lutheran reading, this command is not a ladder by which sinners earn justification. The Law exposes sin and directs the baptized life; the Gospel forgives, creates faith, joins the believer to Christ, and brings forth the fruits named here. The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

#### Early Christian witnesses

**Didache 16.1 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Watch for your life's sake. Let not your lamps be quenched, nor your loins unloosed; but be ready, for you do not know the hour in which our Lord comes.

*Study significance:* The passage directly preserves the church's early reception of Jesus' readiness language.

**Didache 4.14 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> In church you shall acknowledge your transgressions, and you shall not come near for your prayer with an evil conscience. This is the way of life.

*Study significance:* Repentance is concrete, ecclesial, and joined to prayer rather than reduced to vague regret.

#### Lutheran confessional witnesses

**Augsburg Confession XII**

> Properly speaking, repentance consists of these two parts: one is contrition, that is, terrors smiting the conscience through the knowledge of sin; the other is faith, born of the Gospel or of absolution, which believes that for Christ's sake sins are forgiven, comforts the conscience, and delivers it from terrors.

*Study significance:* Repentance includes both truthful sorrow over sin and faith in Christ's forgiveness.

**Augsburg Confession VI**

> This faith is bound to bring forth good fruits, and it is necessary to do good works commanded by God because of God's will; yet we should not rely on those works to merit justification before God.

*Study significance:* Good works are necessary fruits of faith, not merits that purchase God's favor.

**Further confessional anchors:** Augsburg Confession XVII (Christ's return and judgment); Small Catechism: Second and Third Articles of the Creed; Formula of Concord, Solid Declaration V, VI, and XI; Large Catechism: First Commandment and Creed.

#### Practicing the command

Receive correction without delay, make restitution where possible, set measurable reforms, and seek ongoing pastoral accountability.

#### Misuse caution

Zeal is not frenzy, coercion, public shaming, or confidence in intensity rather than Christ.

#### Questions for examination and meditation

1. What exactly does Jesus command, forbid, promise, or warn in these passages, and who first receives the words?
2. Which words expose sin or false trust, and which words direct you to Christ's gift, forgiveness, or sustaining promise?
3. What concrete act of repentance, faith, mercy, or vocation would embody **be zealous in response to christ's loving discipline** this week without turning obedience into a basis of justification?

#### Prayer

Lord Jesus Christ, You call Your people to be zealous in response to christ's loving discipline. Forgive my failure, keep me in Your Word and gifts, and bear this fruit in me for the good of my neighbor and the glory of the Father. Amen.

<a id="command-082"></a>

### 82. Hear Christ's voice and open the door

**Corpus:** Revelation  
**Scope:** Addressed to a church, with personal application  
**Earlier-list crosswalk:** Added by canonical audit  
**Canonical passages:** Revelation 3:20

#### Command-family wording

> Hear the excluded Lord's call and receive renewed fellowship with Him.

#### Scripture text (World English Bible)

##### Revelation 3:20

> **20** Behold, I stand at the door and knock. If anyone hears my voice and opens the door, then I will come in to him and will dine with him, and he with me.

#### Reading the passages together

- The famous image first addresses a self-satisfied congregation. Christ graciously seeks table fellowship with those He has just rebuked; it is not a proof that salvation originates in autonomous human choice. Read within the address to the seven churches and attend carefully to the identified speaker.

#### Law, Gospel, and confessional synthesis

The famous image first addresses a self-satisfied congregation. Christ graciously seeks table fellowship with those He has just rebuked; it is not a proof that salvation originates in autonomous human choice. In a confessional Lutheran reading, this command is not a ladder by which sinners earn justification. The Law exposes sin and directs the baptized life; the Gospel forgives, creates faith, joins the believer to Christ, and brings forth the fruits named here. The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

#### Early Christian witnesses

**Augustine, Confessions 1.1 (Pusey translation, lightly modernized)**

> You have made us for Yourself, and our heart is restless until it rests in You.

*Study significance:* The line helps distinguish Christ's promised rest from mere favorable circumstances.

**1 Clement 49.1 (Lightfoot translation, lightly modernized)**

> Let the one who has love in Christ keep the commandments of Christ.

*Study significance:* The sentence binds Christian love to Christ's actual commands rather than sentiment alone.

#### Lutheran confessional witnesses

**Augsburg Confession IV**

> People cannot be justified before God by their own strength, merits, or works, but are freely justified for Christ's sake, through faith, when they believe that they are received into favor and that their sins are forgiven for Christ's sake.

*Study significance:* The command must never be turned into a competing basis of justification.

**Augsburg Confession V**

> That we may obtain this faith, the Ministry of Teaching the Gospel and administering the Sacraments was instituted. Through the Word and Sacraments, as through instruments, the Holy Spirit is given, who works faith where and when it pleases God.

*Study significance:* Faith is created and sustained through Christ's appointed means rather than self-generated resolve.

**Further confessional anchors:** Augsburg Confession XVII (Christ's return and judgment); Small Catechism: Second and Third Articles of the Creed; Formula of Concord, Solid Declaration V, VI, and XI; Large Catechism: First Commandment and Creed.

#### Practicing the command

Let the Word reopen closed congregational life, repent of self-sufficiency, restore worship and fellowship, and personally receive His promise in faith.

#### Misuse caution

Do not isolate the verse from the churchly context or portray Christ as helpless before the human will.

#### Questions for examination and meditation

1. What exactly does Jesus command, forbid, promise, or warn in these passages, and who first receives the words?
2. Which words expose sin or false trust, and which words direct you to Christ's gift, forgiveness, or sustaining promise?
3. What concrete act of repentance, faith, mercy, or vocation would embody **hear christ's voice and open the door** this week without turning obedience into a basis of justification?

#### Prayer

Lord Jesus Christ, You call Your people to hear christ's voice and open the door. Forgive my failure, keep me in Your Word and gifts, and bear this fruit in me for the good of my neighbor and the glory of the Father. Amen.

<a id="command-083"></a>

### 83. Keep the words of this prophecy and answer Christ's coming with Amen

**Corpus:** Revelation  
**Scope:** Universal  
**Earlier-list crosswalk:** Added by canonical audit  
**Canonical passages:** Revelation 22:7,12-13,16,20

#### Command-family wording

> Guard Revelation's testimony and live in longing for Jesus' promised coming.

#### Scripture text (World English Bible)

##### Revelation 22:7,12-13,16,20

> **7** “Behold, I am coming soon! Blessed is he who keeps the words of the prophecy of this book.”
>
> **12** “Behold, I am coming soon! My reward is with me, to repay to each man according to his work.
>
> **13** I am the Alpha and the Omega, the First and the Last, the Beginning and the End.
>
> **16** I, Jesus, have sent my angel to testify these things to you for the assemblies. I am the root and the offspring of David, the Bright and Morning Star.”
>
> **20** He who testifies these things says, “Yes, I am coming soon.” Amen! Yes, come, Lord Jesus!

#### Reading the passages together

- Blessing attaches to keeping the prophecy, not decoding a private timetable. The Alpha and Omega authenticates the book and promises His return to the churches. Read within the address to the seven churches and attend carefully to the identified speaker.

#### Law, Gospel, and confessional synthesis

Blessing attaches to keeping the prophecy, not decoding a private timetable. The Alpha and Omega authenticates the book and promises His return to the churches. In a confessional Lutheran reading, this command is not a ladder by which sinners earn justification. The Law exposes sin and directs the baptized life; the Gospel forgives, creates faith, joins the believer to Christ, and brings forth the fruits named here. The command must therefore be preached in the whole counsel of God: with Christ's completed saving work, the means by which He gives His gifts, the distinction between justification and sanctification, and the Christian's concrete vocations.

#### Early Christian witnesses

**Didache 16.1 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Watch for your life's sake. Let not your lamps be quenched, nor your loins unloosed; but be ready, for you do not know the hour in which our Lord comes.

*Study significance:* The passage directly preserves the church's early reception of Jesus' readiness language.

**Justin Martyr, First Apology 67 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> On the day called Sunday, all who live in cities or in the country gather together to one place, and the memoirs of the apostles or the writings of the prophets are read as long as time permits.

*Study significance:* The church gathers around apostolic and prophetic Scripture rather than private inspiration alone.

#### Lutheran confessional witnesses

**Augsburg Confession IV**

> People cannot be justified before God by their own strength, merits, or works, but are freely justified for Christ's sake, through faith, when they believe that they are received into favor and that their sins are forgiven for Christ's sake.

*Study significance:* The command must never be turned into a competing basis of justification.

**Augsburg Confession XII**

> Properly speaking, repentance consists of these two parts: one is contrition, that is, terrors smiting the conscience through the knowledge of sin; the other is faith, born of the Gospel or of absolution, which believes that for Christ's sake sins are forgiven, comforts the conscience, and delivers it from terrors.

*Study significance:* Repentance includes both truthful sorrow over sin and faith in Christ's forgiveness.

**Further confessional anchors:** Augsburg Confession XVII (Christ's return and judgment); Small Catechism: Second and Third Articles of the Creed; Formula of Concord, Solid Declaration V, VI, and XI; Large Catechism: First Commandment and Creed.

#### Practicing the command

Read the book as Christian worship and witness, heed its warnings, trust the Lamb's victory, and pray, Come, Lord Jesus.

#### Misuse caution

Do not add speculative certainty to the text, neglect the rest of Scripture, or use apocalyptic imagery to set dates.

#### Questions for examination and meditation

1. What exactly does Jesus command, forbid, promise, or warn in these passages, and who first receives the words?
2. Which words expose sin or false trust, and which words direct you to Christ's gift, forgiveness, or sustaining promise?
3. What concrete act of repentance, faith, mercy, or vocation would embody **keep the words of this prophecy and answer christ's coming with amen** this week without turning obedience into a basis of justification?

#### Prayer

Lord Jesus Christ, You call Your people to keep the words of this prophecy and answer christ's coming with amen. Forgive my failure, keep me in Your Word and gifts, and bear this fruit in me for the good of my neighbor and the glory of the Father. Amen.

# Primary Source Reader

The following excerpts collect, in one place, the early-Christian and Lutheran texts quoted throughout the studies. They are included as substantive study material rather than bare references. The short contextual comments are editorial.

## Early Christian texts

### The Two Ways

**Didache 1.1-2 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> There are two ways, one of life and one of death; but there is a great difference between these two ways. The way of life, then, is this: First, you shall love God who made you; second, your neighbor as yourself; and all things whatsoever you would not have done to you, do not do to another.

**Why it matters:** An early Christian catechetical summary joins love of God, love of neighbor, and concrete conduct.

### Enemy-love

**Didache 1.3 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Bless those who curse you, and pray for your enemies, and fast for those who persecute you. For what thank is there if you love those who love you? Do not even the Gentiles do the same? But love those who hate you, and you shall not have an enemy.

**Why it matters:** The text shows how early Christians heard Jesus' command as prayerful goodwill rather than retaliation.

### Non-retaliation

**Didache 1.4 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Abstain from fleshly and worldly lusts. If anyone gives you a blow on your right cheek, turn to him the other also; and you shall be perfect.

**Why it matters:** This directly echoes the Sermon on the Mount and links non-retaliation with holiness.

### Open-handed giving

**Didache 1.5 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Give to everyone who asks you, and ask it not back; for the Father wills that to all should be given of our own blessings.

**Why it matters:** The command is received as real generosity flowing from the Father's gifts.

### The way of life in bodily conduct

**Didache 2.2 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> You shall not commit murder; you shall not commit adultery; you shall not commit sexual immorality; you shall not steal; you shall not practice magic or witchcraft; you shall not murder a child by abortion nor kill that which is born.

**Why it matters:** This early catechesis applies the way of life to the protection of bodies, marriage, property, and children.

### Truthful speech

**Didache 2.4-5; 4.4 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> You shall not be double-minded nor double-tongued, for to be double-tongued is a snare of death. Your speech shall not be false or empty, but fulfilled by deed.

**Why it matters:** The witness treats integrity as speech verified by action.

### Confession before prayer

**Didache 4.14 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> In church you shall acknowledge your transgressions, and you shall not come near for your prayer with an evil conscience. This is the way of life.

**Why it matters:** Repentance is concrete, ecclesial, and joined to prayer rather than reduced to vague regret.

### Baptism

**Didache 7.1 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Concerning baptism, baptize thus: Having first rehearsed all these things, baptize in the name of the Father and of the Son and of the Holy Spirit, in living water.

**Why it matters:** The early church explicitly connects catechesis, water, and the Triune name.

### The Lord's Prayer

**Didache 8.2-3 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Neither pray as the hypocrites, but as the Lord commanded in his Gospel, thus pray: Our Father, who art in heaven. Three times in the day pray so.

**Why it matters:** Jesus' model prayer was received as a repeated, formative practice rather than empty repetition.

### Eucharistic thanksgiving

**Didache 9.1, 4 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Now concerning the Eucharist, give thanks after this manner. As this broken bread was scattered upon the mountains and, being gathered together, became one, so may your Church be gathered together from the ends of the earth into your kingdom.

**Why it matters:** The prayer connects the meal with thanksgiving, the kingdom, and the unity of the Church.

### Reconciliation before the Eucharistic assembly

**Didache 14.1-2 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> On the Lord's Day of the Lord come together, break bread, and give thanks, having first confessed your transgressions, that your sacrifice may be pure. Let no one who is at variance with his fellow come together with you until they are reconciled.

**Why it matters:** Worship and reconciliation are held together rather than separated.

### Ordered ministry

**Didache 15.1 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Appoint, therefore, for yourselves bishops and deacons worthy of the Lord, men who are meek, not lovers of money, truthful, and proven; for they also render to you the service of prophets and teachers.

**Why it matters:** Mission and pastoral care are entrusted to tested servants rather than self-appointed status seekers.

### Watchfulness

**Didache 16.1 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Watch for your life's sake. Let not your lamps be quenched, nor your loins unloosed; but be ready, for you do not know the hour in which our Lord comes.

**Why it matters:** The passage directly preserves the church's early reception of Jesus' readiness language.

### Testing teachers

**Didache 11.1-2 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Whoever comes and teaches you all these things that have been said before, receive him. But if the teacher himself turns and teaches another doctrine to the destruction of this, do not hear him.

**Why it matters:** Hospitality and openness are joined to doctrinal discernment.

### Chastity of the heart

**Justin Martyr, First Apology 15 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Concerning chastity, He uttered such sentiments as these: Whoever looks upon a woman to lust after her has committed adultery with her already in his heart before God.

**Why it matters:** Justin quotes Jesus as the standard for inward as well as outward chastity.

### Patient endurance and public witness

**Justin Martyr, First Apology 16 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Concerning our being patient of injuries: Whoever shall strike you on the right cheek, turn to him the other also. And: Let your good works shine before men, that they, seeing them, may glorify your Father who is in heaven.

**Why it matters:** The early apologist places non-retaliation and visible good works together as Christian witness.

### Civil obligations

**Justin Martyr, First Apology 17 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Everywhere we, more readily than all people, endeavor to pay to those appointed by you the taxes and assessments, as we have been taught by Him.

**Why it matters:** Justin applies Jesus' teaching about Caesar to ordinary civic duties without making the state divine.

### Christian baptism

**Justin Martyr, First Apology 61 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Then they are brought by us where there is water and are regenerated in the same manner in which we were ourselves regenerated: in the name of God, the Father and Lord of the universe, and of our Savior Jesus Christ, and of the Holy Spirit.

**Why it matters:** This second-century description witnesses to baptism with water and the Triune name.

### The Eucharist

**Justin Martyr, First Apology 66 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> This food is called among us Eucharistia. We do not receive these as common bread and common drink; but as Jesus Christ our Savior was made flesh, so likewise have we been taught that the food blessed by the prayer of His word is the flesh and blood of that Jesus who was made flesh.

**Why it matters:** Justin gives an actual early description of the church's reverent reception of the Supper.

### The Lord's Day assembly

**Justin Martyr, First Apology 67 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> On the day called Sunday, all who live in cities or in the country gather together to one place, and the memoirs of the apostles or the writings of the prophets are read as long as time permits.

**Why it matters:** The church gathers around apostolic and prophetic Scripture rather than private inspiration alone.

### Humility, mercy, and forgiveness

**1 Clement 13.1-2 (Lightfoot/Roberts-Donaldson tradition, lightly modernized)**

> Let us be humble-minded, brethren, laying aside all haughtiness and pride and foolishness and angry feelings, especially remembering the words of the Lord Jesus: Be merciful, that you may obtain mercy; forgive, that it may be forgiven unto you.

**Why it matters:** Clement explicitly uses Jesus' words to form a peaceful and humble congregation.

### Love and obedience

**1 Clement 49.1 (Lightfoot translation, lightly modernized)**

> Let the one who has love in Christ keep the commandments of Christ.

**Why it matters:** The sentence binds Christian love to Christ's actual commands rather than sentiment alone.

### Judgment, mercy, and forgiveness

**Polycarp, Epistle to the Philippians 2 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Remembering what the Lord said in His teaching: Judge not, that you be not judged; forgive, and it shall be forgiven unto you; be merciful, that you may obtain mercy; with what measure you measure, it shall be measured to you again.

**Why it matters:** Polycarp preserves several sayings of Jesus as a unified pattern for congregational life.

### Christ and the gathered Church

**Ignatius, Smyrnaeans 8 (Roberts-Donaldson translation, lightly modernized)**

> Wherever the bishop shall appear, there let the multitude also be; even as, wherever Jesus Christ is, there is the catholic Church.

**Why it matters:** The statement reflects an early concern for visible, ordered communion centered on Christ.

### Grace and obedience

**Augustine, Confessions 10.29 (Pusey translation, lightly modernized)**

> Give what You command, and command what You will.

**Why it matters:** Augustine confesses that divine commands expose need and that God must supply the grace by which they are obeyed.

### Rest in God

**Augustine, Confessions 1.1 (Pusey translation, lightly modernized)**

> You have made us for Yourself, and our heart is restless until it rests in You.

**Why it matters:** The line helps distinguish Christ's promised rest from mere favorable circumstances.

## Lutheran confessional texts

### Justification

**Augsburg Confession IV**

> People cannot be justified before God by their own strength, merits, or works, but are freely justified for Christ's sake, through faith, when they believe that they are received into favor and that their sins are forgiven for Christ's sake.

**Why it matters:** The command must never be turned into a competing basis of justification.

### The ministry of Word and Sacrament

**Augsburg Confession V**

> That we may obtain this faith, the Ministry of Teaching the Gospel and administering the Sacraments was instituted. Through the Word and Sacraments, as through instruments, the Holy Spirit is given, who works faith where and when it pleases God.

**Why it matters:** Faith is created and sustained through Christ's appointed means rather than self-generated resolve.

### New obedience

**Augsburg Confession VI**

> This faith is bound to bring forth good fruits, and it is necessary to do good works commanded by God because of God's will; yet we should not rely on those works to merit justification before God.

**Why it matters:** Good works are necessary fruits of faith, not merits that purchase God's favor.

### Repentance

**Augsburg Confession XII**

> Properly speaking, repentance consists of these two parts: one is contrition, that is, terrors smiting the conscience through the knowledge of sin; the other is faith, born of the Gospel or of absolution, which believes that for Christ's sake sins are forgiven, comforts the conscience, and delivers it from terrors.

**Why it matters:** Repentance includes both truthful sorrow over sin and faith in Christ's forgiveness.

### Ordered public ministry

**Augsburg Confession XIV**

> No one should publicly teach in the Church or administer the Sacraments unless he be regularly called.

**Why it matters:** Christ's mission is carried out through ordered vocation rather than self-authorization.

### Civil affairs

**Augsburg Confession XVI**

> Lawful civil ordinances are good works of God, and it is right for Christians to bear civil office, sit as judges, judge matters by existing laws, and engage in just wars.

**Why it matters:** The confession receives civil vocation as a real sphere of neighbor-love under God.

### The First Commandment

**Small Catechism, First Commandment**

> We should fear, love, and trust in God above all things.

**Why it matters:** Every command family is finally ordered under trust in the true God.

### What a god is

**Large Catechism, First Commandment**

> A god means that from which we are to expect all good and to which we are to take refuge in all distress.

**Why it matters:** This exposes whatever receives the trust, fear, or refuge that belongs to God.

### Honor within vocation

**Small Catechism, Fourth Commandment**

> We should fear and love God that we may not despise our parents and masters nor provoke them to anger, but give them honor, serve and obey them, and hold them in love and esteem.

**Why it matters:** Honor is concrete service under God, not permission for authority to command sin.

### Protect the neighbor's life

**Small Catechism, Fifth Commandment**

> We should fear and love God that we may not hurt nor harm our neighbor in his body, but help and befriend him in every bodily need.

**Why it matters:** Neighbor-love includes active bodily help, not merely refraining from violence.

### Chastity and marriage

**Small Catechism, Sixth Commandment**

> We should fear and love God that we may lead a chaste and decent life in words and deeds, and each love and honor his spouse.

**Why it matters:** Jesus' teaching about desire and marriage is received as positive chastity and faithful honor.

### Protect the neighbor's property

**Small Catechism, Seventh Commandment**

> We should fear and love God that we may not take our neighbor's money or property, nor get them by false ware or dealing, but help him to improve and protect his property and business.

**Why it matters:** Generosity and economic integrity seek the neighbor's material good.

### Protect the neighbor's reputation

**Small Catechism, Eighth Commandment**

> We should fear and love God that we may not deceitfully belie, betray, slander, or defame our neighbor, but defend him, speak well of him, and put the best construction on everything.

**Why it matters:** Truthful correction and charitable interpretation belong together.

### What gives Baptism its power

**Small Catechism, Baptism III**

> It is not the water indeed that does these things, but the word of God which is in and with the water, and faith which trusts this word of God in the water.

**Why it matters:** Baptism is God's act through water joined to His word and promise.

### Daily use of Baptism

**Small Catechism, Baptism IV**

> The Old Adam in us should, by daily contrition and repentance, be drowned and die with all sins and evil lusts, and a new person daily come forth and arise, who shall live before God in righteousness and purity forever.

**Why it matters:** The baptized life is daily repentance and new life, not a one-time memory.

### The confidence of prayer

**Small Catechism, Lord's Prayer, Introduction**

> God would thereby tenderly invite us to believe that He is our true Father and that we are His true children, so that we may ask Him confidently with all assurance, as dear children ask their dear father.

**Why it matters:** Christian prayer rests on the Father's promise rather than quantity of words or emotional intensity.

### The Sacrament of the Altar

**Small Catechism, Sacrament of the Altar I**

> It is the true body and blood of our Lord Jesus Christ, under the bread and wine, for us Christians to eat and to drink, instituted by Christ Himself.

**Why it matters:** The Supper is received according to Christ's institution and promise.

# Sources and editorial notes

- **Scripture:** World English Bible, American English edition. The WEB is a public-domain modern English Bible.
- **Didache, Justin Martyr, Polycarp, Ignatius, and 1 Clement:** public-domain translations in the Ante-Nicene Fathers / Lightfoot tradition; wording lightly modernized.
- **Augustine:** public-domain Pusey translation of the *Confessions*; wording lightly modernized.
- **Lutheran Confessions:** public-domain English wording represented by the *Concordia Triglotta* (1921), lightly normalized for readability.
- **Text-critical notes:** readers should consult a current critical Greek New Testament and the notes in an owned study Bible for Mark 16:9-20 and John 7:53-8:11.

This is an independent personal-study resource, not an official publication of the LCMS, Concordia Publishing House, the ESV publisher, or any patristic-text publisher.
